1254     // Contains 
1255     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1256     /** 
1257      * <p>Checks if String contains a search character, handling <code>null</code>. 
1258      * This method uses {@link String#indexOf(int)}.</p> 
1259      * 
1260      * <p>A <code>null</code> or empty ("") String will return <code>false</code>.</p> 
1261      * 
1262      * <pre> 
1263      * StringUtils.contains(null, *)    = false 
1264      * StringUtils.contains("", *)      = false 
1265      * StringUtils.contains("abc", 'a') = true 
1266      * StringUtils.contains("abc", 'z') = false 
1267      * </pre> 
1268      * 
1269      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1270      * @param searchChar  the character to find 
1271      * @return true if the String contains the search character, 
1272      *  false if not or <code>null</code> string input 
1273      * @since 2.0 
1274      */ 
1275     public static boolean contains(String str, char searchChar) { 
1276         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
1277             return false; 
1278         } 
1279         return str.indexOf(searchChar) >= 0; 
1280     } 
1281  
1282     /** 
1283      * <p>Checks if String contains a search String, handling <code>null</code>. 
1284      * This method uses {@link String#indexOf(String)}.</p> 
1285      * 
1286      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>false</code>.</p> 
1287      * 
1288      * <pre> 
1289      * StringUtils.contains(null, *)     = false 
1290      * StringUtils.contains(*, null)     = false 
1291      * StringUtils.contains("", "")      = true 
1292      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "")   = true 
1293      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "a")  = true 
1294      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "z")  = false 
1295      * </pre> 
1296      * 
1297      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1298      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
1299      * @return true if the String contains the search String, 
1300      *  false if not or <code>null</code> string input 
1301      * @since 2.0 
1302      */ 
1303     public static boolean contains(String str, String searchStr) { 
1304         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
1305             return false; 
1306         } 
1307         return str.indexOf(searchStr) >= 0; 
1308     } 
1309  
1310     /** 
1311      * <p>Checks if String contains a search String irrespective of case, 
1312      * handling <code>null</code>. Case-insensitivity is defined as by 
1313      * {@link String#equalsIgnoreCase(String)}. 
1314      * 
1315      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>false</code>.</p> 
1316      * 
1317      * <pre> 
1318      * StringUtils.contains(null, *) = false 
1319      * StringUtils.contains(*, null) = false 
1320      * StringUtils.contains("", "") = true 
1321      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "") = true 
1322      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "a") = true 
1323      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "z") = false 
1324      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "A") = true 
1325      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "Z") = false 
1326      * </pre> 
1327      * 
1328      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1329      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
1330      * @return true if the String contains the search String irrespective of 
1331      * case or false if not or <code>null</code> string input 
1332      */ 
1333     public static boolean containsIgnoreCase(String str, String searchStr) { 
1334         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
1335             return false; 
1336         } 
1337         int len = searchStr.length(); 
1338         int max = str.length() - len; 
1339         for (int i = 0; i <= max; i++) { 
1340             if (str.regionMatches(true, i, searchStr, 0, len)) { 
1341                 return true; 
1342             } 
1343         } 
1344         return false; 
1345     } 
1346  
1347     // IndexOfAny chars 
1348     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1349     /** 
1350      * <p>Search a CharSequence to find the first index of any 
1351      * character in the given set of characters.</p> 
1352      * 
1353      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1354      * A <code>null</code> or zero length search array will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1355      * 
1356      * <pre> 
1357      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(null, *)                = -1 
1358      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("", *)                  = -1 
1359      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, null)                = -1 
1360      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, [])                  = -1 
1361      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx",['z','a']) = 0 
1362      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx",['b','y']) = 3 
1363      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("aba", ['z'])           = -1 
1364      * </pre> 
1365      * 
1366      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1367      * @param searchChars  the chars to search for, may be null 
1368      * @return the index of any of the chars, -1 if no match or null input 
1369      * @since 2.0 
1370      */ 
1371     public static int indexOfAny(CharSequence cs, char[] searchChars) { 
1372         if (isEmpty(cs) || ArrayUtils.isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
1373             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1374         } 
1375         for (int i = 0; i < cs.length(); i++) { 
1376             char ch = cs.charAt(i); 
1377             for (int j = 0; j < searchChars.length; j++) { 
1378                 if (searchChars[j] == ch) { 
1379                     return i; 
1380                 } 
1381             } 
1382         } 
1383         return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1384     } 
1385  
1386     /** 
1387      * <p>Search a CharSequence to find the first index of any 
1388      * character in the given set of characters.</p> 
1389      * 
1390      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1391      * A <code>null</code> search string will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1392      * 
1393      * <pre> 
1394      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(null, *)            = -1 
1395      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("", *)              = -1 
1396      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, null)            = -1 
1397      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, "")              = -1 
1398      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", "za") = 0 
1399      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", "by") = 3 
1400      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("aba","z")          = -1 
1401      * </pre> 
1402      * 
1403      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1404      * @param searchChars  the chars to search for, may be null 
1405      * @return the index of any of the chars, -1 if no match or null input 
1406      * @since 2.0 
1407      */ 
1408     public static int indexOfAny(CharSequence cs, String searchChars) { 
1409         if (isEmpty(cs) || isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
1410             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1411         } 
1412         return indexOfAny(cs, searchChars.toCharArray()); 
1413     } 
1414  
1415     // ContainsAny 
1416     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1417     /** 
1418      * <p>Checks if the CharSequence contains any character in the given 
1419      * set of characters.</p> 
1420      * 
1421      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>false</code>. 
1422      * A <code>null</code> or zero length search array will return <code>false</code>.</p> 
1423      * 
1424      * <pre> 
1425      * StringUtils.containsAny(null, *)                = false 
1426      * StringUtils.containsAny("", *)                  = false 
1427      * StringUtils.containsAny(*, null)                = false 
1428      * StringUtils.containsAny(*, [])                  = false 
1429      * StringUtils.containsAny("zzabyycdxx",['z','a']) = true 
1430      * StringUtils.containsAny("zzabyycdxx",['b','y']) = true 
1431      * StringUtils.containsAny("aba", ['z'])           = false 
1432      * </pre> 
1433      * 
1434      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1435      * @param searchChars  the chars to search for, may be null 
1436      * @return the <code>true</code> if any of the chars are found, 
1437      * <code>false</code> if no match or null input 
1438      * @since 2.4 
1439      */ 
1440 	public static boolean containsAny(CharSequence cs, char[] searchChars) { 
1441 		if (isEmpty(cs) || ArrayUtils.isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
1442 			return false; 
1443 		} 
1444 		int csLength = cs.length(); 
1445 		int searchLength = searchChars.length; 
1446 		for (int i = 0; i < csLength; i++) { 
1447 			char ch = cs.charAt(i); 
1448 			for (int j = 0; j < searchLength; j++) { 
1449 				if (searchChars[j] == ch) { 
1450 						// ch is a supplementary character 
1451 						// ch is in the Basic Multilingual Plane 
1452 						return true; 
1453 				} 
1454 			} 
1455 		} 
1456 		return false; 
1457 	} 
1458  
1459     /** 
1460      * <p> 
1461      * Checks if the CharSequence contains any character in the given set of characters. 
1462      * </p> 
1463      *  
1464      * <p> 
1465      * A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>false</code>. A <code>null</code> search CharSequence will return 
1466      * <code>false</code>. 
1467      * </p> 
1468      *  
1469      * <pre> 
1470      * StringUtils.containsAny(null, *)            = false 
1471      * StringUtils.containsAny("", *)              = false 
1472      * StringUtils.containsAny(*, null)            = false 
1473      * StringUtils.containsAny(*, "")              = false 
1474      * StringUtils.containsAny("zzabyycdxx", "za") = true 
1475      * StringUtils.containsAny("zzabyycdxx", "by") = true 
1476      * StringUtils.containsAny("aba","z")          = false 
1477      * </pre> 
1478      *  
1479      * @param cs 
1480      *            the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1481      * @param searchChars 
1482      *            the chars to search for, may be null 
1483      * @return the <code>true</code> if any of the chars are found, <code>false</code> if no match or null input 
1484      * @since 2.4 
1485      */ 
1486     public static boolean containsAny(CharSequence cs, String searchChars) { 
1487         if (searchChars == null) { 
1488             return false; 
1489         } 
1490         return containsAny(cs, searchChars.toCharArray()); 
1491     } 
1492  
1493     // IndexOfAnyBut chars 
1494     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1495     /** 
1496      * <p>Searches a CharSequence to find the first index of any 
1497      * character not in the given set of characters.</p> 
1498      * 
1499      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>-1</code>. 
1500      * A <code>null</code> or zero length search array will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1501      * 
1502      * <pre> 
1503      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(null, *)           = -1 
1504      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("", *)             = -1 
1505      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(*, null)           = -1 
1506      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(*, [])             = -1 
1507      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("zzabyycdxx",'za') = 3 
1508      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("zzabyycdxx", '')  = 0 
1509      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("aba", 'ab')       = -1 
1510      * </pre> 
1511      * 
1512      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1513      * @param searchChars  the chars to search for, may be null 
1514      * @return the index of any of the chars, -1 if no match or null input 
1515      * @since 2.0 
1516      */ 
1517     public static int indexOfAnyBut(CharSequence cs, char[] searchChars) { 
1518         if (isEmpty(cs) || ArrayUtils.isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
1519             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1520         } 
1521         outer : for (int i = 0; i < cs.length(); i++) { 
1522             char ch = cs.charAt(i); 
1523             for (int j = 0; j < searchChars.length; j++) { 
1524                 if (searchChars[j] == ch) { 
1525                     continue outer; 
1526                 } 
1527             } 
1528             return i; 
1529         } 
1530         return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1531     } 
1532  
1533     /** 
1534      * <p>Search a String to find the first index of any 
1535      * character not in the given set of characters.</p> 
1536      * 
1537      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1538      * A <code>null</code> search string will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1539      * 
1540      * <pre> 
1541      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(null, *)            = -1 
1542      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("", *)              = -1 
1543      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(*, null)            = -1 
1544      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(*, "")              = -1 
1545      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("zzabyycdxx", "za") = 3 
1546      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("zzabyycdxx", "")   = 0 
1547      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("aba","ab")         = -1 
1548      * </pre> 
1549      * 
1550      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1551      * @param searchChars  the chars to search for, may be null 
1552      * @return the index of any of the chars, -1 if no match or null input 
1553      * @since 2.0 
1554      */ 
1555     public static int indexOfAnyBut(String str, String searchChars) { 
1556         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
1557             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1558         } 
1559         for (int i = 0; i < str.length(); i++) { 
1560             if (searchChars.indexOf(str.charAt(i)) < 0) { 
1561                 return i; 
1562             } 
1563         } 
1564         return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1565     } 
1566  
1567     // ContainsOnly 
1568     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1569     /** 
1570      * <p>Checks if the CharSequence contains only certain characters.</p> 
1571      * 
1572      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>false</code>. 
1573      * A <code>null</code> valid character array will return <code>false</code>. 
1574      * An empty CharSequence (length()=0) always returns <code>true</code>.</p> 
1575      * 
1576      * <pre> 
1577      * StringUtils.containsOnly(null, *)       = false 
1578      * StringUtils.containsOnly(*, null)       = false 
1579      * StringUtils.containsOnly("", *)         = true 
1580      * StringUtils.containsOnly("ab", '')      = false 
1581      * StringUtils.containsOnly("abab", 'abc') = true 
1582      * StringUtils.containsOnly("ab1", 'abc')  = false 
1583      * StringUtils.containsOnly("abz", 'abc')  = false 
1584      * </pre> 
1585      * 
1586      * @param cs  the String to check, may be null 
1587      * @param valid  an array of valid chars, may be null 
1588      * @return true if it only contains valid chars and is non-null 
1589      */ 
1590     public static boolean containsOnly(CharSequence cs, char[] valid) { 
1591         // All these pre-checks are to maintain API with an older version 
1592         if (valid == null || cs == null) { 
1593             return false; 
1594         } 
1595         if (cs.length() == 0) { 
1596             return true; 
1597         } 
1598         if (valid.length == 0) { 
1599             return false; 
1600         } 
1601         return indexOfAnyBut(cs, valid) == INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1602     } 
1603  
1604     /** 
1605      * <p>Checks if the CharSequence contains only certain characters.</p> 
1606      * 
1607      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>false</code>. 
1608      * A <code>null</code> valid character String will return <code>false</code>. 
1609      * An empty String (length()=0) always returns <code>true</code>.</p> 
1610      * 
1611      * <pre> 
1612      * StringUtils.containsOnly(null, *)       = false 
1613      * StringUtils.containsOnly(*, null)       = false 
1614      * StringUtils.containsOnly("", *)         = true 
1615      * StringUtils.containsOnly("ab", "")      = false 
1616      * StringUtils.containsOnly("abab", "abc") = true 
1617      * StringUtils.containsOnly("ab1", "abc")  = false 
1618      * StringUtils.containsOnly("abz", "abc")  = false 
1619      * </pre> 
1620      * 
1621      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1622      * @param validChars  a String of valid chars, may be null 
1623      * @return true if it only contains valid chars and is non-null 
1624      * @since 2.0 
1625      */ 
1626     public static boolean containsOnly(CharSequence cs, String validChars) { 
1627         if (cs == null || validChars == null) { 
1628             return false; 
1629         } 
1630         return containsOnly(cs, validChars.toCharArray()); 
1631     } 
1632  
1633     // ContainsNone 
1634     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1635     /** 
1636      * <p>Checks that the CharSequence does not contain certain characters.</p> 
1637      * 
1638      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>true</code>. 
1639      * A <code>null</code> invalid character array will return <code>true</code>. 
1640      * An empty CharSequence (length()=0) always returns true.</p> 
1641      * 
1642      * <pre> 
1643      * StringUtils.containsNone(null, *)       = true 
1644      * StringUtils.containsNone(*, null)       = true 
1645      * StringUtils.containsNone("", *)         = true 
1646      * StringUtils.containsNone("ab", '')      = true 
1647      * StringUtils.containsNone("abab", 'xyz') = true 
1648      * StringUtils.containsNone("ab1", 'xyz')  = true 
1649      * StringUtils.containsNone("abz", 'xyz')  = false 
1650      * </pre> 
1651      * 
1652      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1653      * @param invalidChars  an array of invalid chars, may be null 
1654      * @return true if it contains none of the invalid chars, or is null 
1655      * @since 2.0 
1656      */ 
1657     public static boolean containsNone(CharSequence cs, char[] invalidChars) { 
1658         if (cs == null || invalidChars == null) { 
1659             return true; 
1660         } 
1661         int strSize = cs.length(); 
1662         int validSize = invalidChars.length; 
1663         for (int i = 0; i < strSize; i++) { 
1664             char ch = cs.charAt(i); 
1665             for (int j = 0; j < validSize; j++) { 
1666                 if (invalidChars[j] == ch) { 
1667                     return false; 
1668                 } 
1669             } 
1670         } 
1671         return true; 
1672     } 
1673  
1674     /** 
1675      * <p>Checks that the CharSequence does not contain certain characters.</p> 
1676      * 
1677      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>true</code>. 
1678      * A <code>null</code> invalid character array will return <code>true</code>. 
1679      * An empty String ("") always returns true.</p> 
1680      * 
1681      * <pre> 
1682      * StringUtils.containsNone(null, *)       = true 
1683      * StringUtils.containsNone(*, null)       = true 
1684      * StringUtils.containsNone("", *)         = true 
1685      * StringUtils.containsNone("ab", "")      = true 
1686      * StringUtils.containsNone("abab", "xyz") = true 
1687      * StringUtils.containsNone("ab1", "xyz")  = true 
1688      * StringUtils.containsNone("abz", "xyz")  = false 
1689      * </pre> 
1690      * 
1691      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1692      * @param invalidChars  a String of invalid chars, may be null 
1693      * @return true if it contains none of the invalid chars, or is null 
1694      * @since 2.0 
1695      */ 
1696     public static boolean containsNone(CharSequence cs, String invalidChars) { 
1697         if (cs == null || invalidChars == null) { 
1698             return true; 
1699         } 
1700         return containsNone(cs, invalidChars.toCharArray()); 
1701     } 
1702  
1703     // IndexOfAny strings 
1704     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1705     /** 
1706      * <p>Find the first index of any of a set of potential substrings.</p> 
1707      * 
1708      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1709      * A <code>null</code> or zero length search array will return <code>-1</code>. 
1710      * A <code>null</code> search array entry will be ignored, but a search 
1711      * array containing "" will return <code>0</code> if <code>str</code> is not 
1712      * null. This method uses {@link String#indexOf(String)}.</p> 
1713      * 
1714      * <pre> 
1715      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(null, *)                     = -1 
1716      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, null)                     = -1 
1717      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, [])                       = -1 
1718      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["ab","cd"])   = 2 
1719      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["cd","ab"])   = 2 
1720      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["mn","op"])   = -1 
1721      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["zab","aby"]) = 1 
1722      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", [""])          = 0 
1723      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("", [""])                    = 0 
1724      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("", ["a"])                   = -1 
1725      * </pre> 
1726      * 
1727      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1728      * @param searchStrs  the Strings to search for, may be null 
1729      * @return the first index of any of the searchStrs in str, -1 if no match 
1730      */ 
1731     public static int indexOfAny(String str, String[] searchStrs) { 
1732         if (str == null || searchStrs == null) { 
1733             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1734         } 
1735         int sz = searchStrs.length; 
1736  
1737         // String's can't have a MAX_VALUEth index. 
1738         int ret = Integer.MAX_VALUE; 
1739  
1740         int tmp = 0; 
1741         for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) { 
1742             String search = searchStrs[i]; 
1743             if (search == null) { 
1744                 continue; 
1745             } 
1746             tmp = str.indexOf(search); 
1747             if (tmp == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
1748                 continue; 
1749             } 
1750  
1751             if (tmp < ret) { 
1752                 ret = tmp; 
1753             } 
1754         } 
1755  
1756         return (ret == Integer.MAX_VALUE) ? INDEX_NOT_FOUND : ret; 
1757     } 
1758  
1759     /** 
1760      * <p>Find the latest index of any of a set of potential substrings.</p> 
1761      * 
1762      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1763      * A <code>null</code> search array will return <code>-1</code>. 
1764      * A <code>null</code> or zero length search array entry will be ignored, 
1765      * but a search array containing "" will return the length of <code>str</code> 
1766      * if <code>str</code> is not null. This method uses {@link String#indexOf(String)}</p> 
1767      * 
1768      * <pre> 
1769      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny(null, *)                   = -1 
1770      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny(*, null)                   = -1 
1771      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny(*, [])                     = -1 
1772      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny(*, [null])                 = -1 
1773      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["ab","cd"]) = 6 
1774      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["cd","ab"]) = 6 
1775      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["mn","op"]) = -1 
1776      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["mn","op"]) = -1 
1777      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["mn",""])   = 10 
1778      * </pre> 
1779      * 
1780      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1781      * @param searchStrs  the Strings to search for, may be null 
1782      * @return the last index of any of the Strings, -1 if no match 
1783      */ 
1784     public static int lastIndexOfAny(String str, String[] searchStrs) { 
1785         if (str == null || searchStrs == null) { 
1786             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1787         } 
1788         int sz = searchStrs.length; 
1789         int ret = INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1790         int tmp = 0; 
1791         for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) { 
1792             String search = searchStrs[i]; 
1793             if (search == null) { 
1794                 continue; 
1795             } 
1796             tmp = str.lastIndexOf(search); 
1797             if (tmp > ret) { 
1798                 ret = tmp; 
1799             } 
1800         } 
1801         return ret; 
1802     } 
1803  
1804     // Substring 
1805     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1806     /** 
1807      * <p>Gets a substring from the specified String avoiding exceptions.</p> 
1808      * 
1809      * <p>A negative start position can be used to start <code>n</code> 
1810      * characters from the end of the String.</p> 
1811      * 
1812      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>null</code>. 
1813      * An empty ("") String will return "".</p> 
1814      * 
1815      * <pre> 
1816      * StringUtils.substring(null, *)   = null 
1817      * StringUtils.substring("", *)     = "" 
1818      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 0)  = "abc" 
1819      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 2)  = "c" 
1820      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 4)  = "" 
1821      * StringUtils.substring("abc", -2) = "bc" 
1822      * StringUtils.substring("abc", -4) = "abc" 
1823      * </pre> 
1824      * 
1825      * @param str  the String to get the substring from, may be null 
1826      * @param start  the position to start from, negative means 
1827      *  count back from the end of the String by this many characters 
1828      * @return substring from start position, <code>null</code> if null String input 
1829      */ 
1830     public static String substring(String str, int start) { 
1831         if (str == null) { 
1832             return null; 
1833         } 
1834  
1835         // handle negatives, which means last n characters 
1836         if (start < 0) { 
1837             start = str.length() + start; // remember start is negative 
1838         } 
1839  
1840         if (start < 0) { 
1841             start = 0; 
1842         } 
1843         if (start > str.length()) { 
1844             return EMPTY; 
1845         } 
1846  
1847         return str.substring(start); 
1848     } 
1849  
1850     /** 
1851      * <p>Gets a substring from the specified String avoiding exceptions.</p> 
1852      * 
1853      * <p>A negative start position can be used to start/end <code>n</code> 
1854      * characters from the end of the String.</p> 
1855      * 
1856      * <p>The returned substring starts with the character in the <code>start</code> 
1857      * position and ends before the <code>end</code> position. All position counting is 
1858      * zero-based -- i.e., to start at the beginning of the string use 
1859      * <code>start = 0</code>. Negative start and end positions can be used to 
1860      * specify offsets relative to the end of the String.</p> 
1861      * 
1862      * <p>If <code>start</code> is not strictly to the left of <code>end</code>, "" 
1863      * is returned.</p> 
1864      * 
1865      * <pre> 
1866      * StringUtils.substring(null, *, *)    = null 
1867      * StringUtils.substring("", * ,  *)    = ""; 
1868      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 0, 2)   = "ab" 
1869      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 2, 0)   = "" 
1870      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 2, 4)   = "c" 
1871      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 4, 6)   = "" 
1872      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 2, 2)   = "" 
1873      * StringUtils.substring("abc", -2, -1) = "b" 
1874      * StringUtils.substring("abc", -4, 2)  = "ab" 
1875      * </pre> 
1876      * 
1877      * @param str  the String to get the substring from, may be null 
1878      * @param start  the position to start from, negative means 
1879      *  count back from the end of the String by this many characters 
1880      * @param end  the position to end at (exclusive), negative means 
1881      *  count back from the end of the String by this many characters 
1882      * @return substring from start position to end positon, 
1883      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
1884      */ 
1885     public static String substring(String str, int start, int end) { 
1886         if (str == null) { 
1887             return null; 
1888         } 
1889  
1890         // handle negatives 
1891         if (end < 0) { 
1892             end = str.length() + end; // remember end is negative 
1893         } 
1894         if (start < 0) { 
1895             start = str.length() + start; // remember start is negative 
1896         } 
1897  
1898         // check length next 
1899         if (end > str.length()) { 
1900             end = str.length(); 
1901         } 
1902  
1903         // if start is greater than end, return "" 
1904         if (start > end) { 
1905             return EMPTY; 
1906         } 
1907  
1908         if (start < 0) { 
1909             start = 0; 
1910         } 
1911         if (end < 0) { 
1912             end = 0; 
1913         } 
1914  
1915         return str.substring(start, end); 
1916     } 
1917  
1918     // Left/Right/Mid 
1919     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1920     /** 
1921      * <p>Gets the leftmost <code>len</code> characters of a String.</p> 
1922      * 
1923      * <p>If <code>len</code> characters are not available, or the 
1924      * String is <code>null</code>, the String will be returned without 
1925      * an exception. An exception is thrown if len is negative.</p> 
1926      * 
1927      * <pre> 
1928      * StringUtils.left(null, *)    = null 
1929      * StringUtils.left(*, -ve)     = "" 
1930      * StringUtils.left("", *)      = "" 
1931      * StringUtils.left("abc", 0)   = "" 
1932      * StringUtils.left("abc", 2)   = "ab" 
1933      * StringUtils.left("abc", 4)   = "abc" 
1934      * </pre> 
1935      * 
1936      * @param str  the String to get the leftmost characters from, may be null 
1937      * @param len  the length of the required String, must be zero or positive 
1938      * @return the leftmost characters, <code>null</code> if null String input 
1939      */ 
1940     public static String left(String str, int len) { 
1941         if (str == null) { 
1942             return null; 
1943         } 
1944         if (len < 0) { 
1945             return EMPTY; 
1946         } 
1947         if (str.length() <= len) { 
1948             return str; 
1949         } 
1950         return str.substring(0, len); 
1951     } 
1952  
1953     /** 
1954      * <p>Gets the rightmost <code>len</code> characters of a String.</p> 
1955      * 
1956      * <p>If <code>len</code> characters are not available, or the String 
1957      * is <code>null</code>, the String will be returned without an 
1958      * an exception. An exception is thrown if len is negative.</p> 
1959      * 
1960      * <pre> 
1961      * StringUtils.right(null, *)    = null 
1962      * StringUtils.right(*, -ve)     = "" 
1963      * StringUtils.right("", *)      = "" 
1964      * StringUtils.right("abc", 0)   = "" 
1965      * StringUtils.right("abc", 2)   = "bc" 
1966      * StringUtils.right("abc", 4)   = "abc" 
1967      * </pre> 
1968      * 
1969      * @param str  the String to get the rightmost characters from, may be null 
1970      * @param len  the length of the required String, must be zero or positive 
1971      * @return the rightmost characters, <code>null</code> if null String input 
1972      */ 
1973     public static String right(String str, int len) { 
1974         if (str == null) { 
1975             return null; 
1976         } 
1977         if (len < 0) { 
1978             return EMPTY; 
1979         } 
1980         if (str.length() <= len) { 
1981             return str; 
1982         } 
1983         return str.substring(str.length() - len); 
1984     } 
1985  
1986     /** 
1987      * <p>Gets <code>len</code> characters from the middle of a String.</p> 
1988      * 
1989      * <p>If <code>len</code> characters are not available, the remainder 
1990      * of the String will be returned without an exception. If the 
1991      * String is <code>null</code>, <code>null</code> will be returned. 
1992      * An exception is thrown if len is negative.</p> 
1993      * 
1994      * <pre> 
1995      * StringUtils.mid(null, *, *)    = null 
1996      * StringUtils.mid(*, *, -ve)     = "" 
1997      * StringUtils.mid("", 0, *)      = "" 
1998      * StringUtils.mid("abc", 0, 2)   = "ab" 
1999      * StringUtils.mid("abc", 0, 4)   = "abc" 
2000      * StringUtils.mid("abc", 2, 4)   = "c" 
2001      * StringUtils.mid("abc", 4, 2)   = "" 
2002      * StringUtils.mid("abc", -2, 2)  = "ab" 
2003      * </pre> 
2004      * 
2005      * @param str  the String to get the characters from, may be null 
2006      * @param pos  the position to start from, negative treated as zero 
2007      * @param len  the length of the required String, must be zero or positive 
2008      * @return the middle characters, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2009      */ 
2010     public static String mid(String str, int pos, int len) { 
2011         if (str == null) { 
2012             return null; 
2013         } 
2014         if (len < 0 || pos > str.length()) { 
2015             return EMPTY; 
2016         } 
2017         if (pos < 0) { 
2018             pos = 0; 
2019         } 
2020         if (str.length() <= (pos + len)) { 
2021             return str.substring(pos); 
2022         } 
2023         return str.substring(pos, pos + len); 
2024     } 
2025  
2026     // SubStringAfter/SubStringBefore 
2027     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
2028     /** 
2029      * <p>Gets the substring before the first occurrence of a separator. 
2030      * The separator is not returned.</p> 
2031      * 
2032      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input will return <code>null</code>. 
2033      * An empty ("") string input will return the empty string. 
2034      * A <code>null</code> separator will return the input string.</p> 
2035      * 
2036      * <p>If nothing is found, the string input is returned.</p> 
2037      * 
2038      * <pre> 
2039      * StringUtils.substringBefore(null, *)      = null 
2040      * StringUtils.substringBefore("", *)        = "" 
2041      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abc", "a")   = "" 
2042      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abcba", "b") = "a" 
2043      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abc", "c")   = "ab" 
2044      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abc", "d")   = "abc" 
2045      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abc", "")    = "" 
2046      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abc", null)  = "abc" 
2047      * </pre> 
2048      * 
2049      * @param str  the String to get a substring from, may be null 
2050      * @param separator  the String to search for, may be null 
2051      * @return the substring before the first occurrence of the separator, 
2052      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
2053      * @since 2.0 
2054      */ 
2055     public static String substringBefore(String str, String separator) { 
2056         if (isEmpty(str) || separator == null) { 
2057             return str; 
2058         } 
2059         if (separator.length() == 0) { 
2060             return EMPTY; 
2061         } 
2062         int pos = str.indexOf(separator); 
2063         if (pos == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
2064             return str; 
2065         } 
2066         return str.substring(0, pos); 
2067     } 
2068  
2069     /** 
2070      * <p>Gets the substring after the first occurrence of a separator. 
2071      * The separator is not returned.</p> 
2072      * 
2073      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input will return <code>null</code>. 
2074      * An empty ("") string input will return the empty string. 
2075      * A <code>null</code> separator will return the empty string if the 
2076      * input string is not <code>null</code>.</p> 
2077      * 
2078      * <p>If nothing is found, the empty string is returned.</p> 
2079      * 
2080      * <pre> 
2081      * StringUtils.substringAfter(null, *)      = null 
2082      * StringUtils.substringAfter("", *)        = "" 
2083      * StringUtils.substringAfter(*, null)      = "" 
2084      * StringUtils.substringAfter("abc", "a")   = "bc" 
2085      * StringUtils.substringAfter("abcba", "b") = "cba" 
2086      * StringUtils.substringAfter("abc", "c")   = "" 
2087      * StringUtils.substringAfter("abc", "d")   = "" 
2088      * StringUtils.substringAfter("abc", "")    = "abc" 
2089      * </pre> 
2090      * 
2091      * @param str  the String to get a substring from, may be null 
2092      * @param separator  the String to search for, may be null 
2093      * @return the substring after the first occurrence of the separator, 
2094      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
2095      * @since 2.0 
2096      */ 
2097     public static String substringAfter(String str, String separator) { 
2098         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
2099             return str; 
2100         } 
2101         if (separator == null) { 
2102             return EMPTY; 
2103         } 
2104         int pos = str.indexOf(separator); 
2105         if (pos == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
2106             return EMPTY; 
2107         } 
2108         return str.substring(pos + separator.length()); 
2109     } 
2110  
2111     /** 
2112      * <p>Gets the substring before the last occurrence of a separator. 
2113      * The separator is not returned.</p> 
2114      * 
2115      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input will return <code>null</code>. 
2116      * An empty ("") string input will return the empty string. 
2117      * An empty or <code>null</code> separator will return the input string.</p> 
2118      * 
2119      * <p>If nothing is found, the string input is returned.</p> 
2120      * 
2121      * <pre> 
2122      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast(null, *)      = null 
2123      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("", *)        = "" 
2124      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("abcba", "b") = "abc" 
2125      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("abc", "c")   = "ab" 
2126      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("a", "a")     = "" 
2127      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("a", "z")     = "a" 
2128      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("a", null)    = "a" 
2129      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("a", "")      = "a" 
2130      * </pre> 
2131      * 
2132      * @param str  the String to get a substring from, may be null 
2133      * @param separator  the String to search for, may be null 
2134      * @return the substring before the last occurrence of the separator, 
2135      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
2136      * @since 2.0 
2137      */ 
2138     public static String substringBeforeLast(String str, String separator) { 
2139         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(separator)) { 
2140             return str; 
2141         } 
2142         int pos = str.lastIndexOf(separator); 
2143         if (pos == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
2144             return str; 
2145         } 
2146         return str.substring(0, pos); 
2147     } 
2148  
2149     /** 
2150      * <p>Gets the substring after the last occurrence of a separator. 
2151      * The separator is not returned.</p> 
2152      * 
2153      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input will return <code>null</code>. 
2154      * An empty ("") string input will return the empty string. 
2155      * An empty or <code>null</code> separator will return the empty string if 
2156      * the input string is not <code>null</code>.</p> 
2157      * 
2158      * <p>If nothing is found, the empty string is returned.</p> 
2159      * 
2160      * <pre> 
2161      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast(null, *)      = null 
2162      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("", *)        = "" 
2163      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast(*, "")        = "" 
2164      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast(*, null)      = "" 
2165      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("abc", "a")   = "bc" 
2166      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("abcba", "b") = "a" 
2167      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("abc", "c")   = "" 
2168      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("a", "a")     = "" 
2169      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("a", "z")     = "" 
2170      * </pre> 
2171      * 
2172      * @param str  the String to get a substring from, may be null 
2173      * @param separator  the String to search for, may be null 
2174      * @return the substring after the last occurrence of the separator, 
2175      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
2176      * @since 2.0 
2177      */ 
2178     public static String substringAfterLast(String str, String separator) { 
2179         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
2180             return str; 
2181         } 
2182         if (isEmpty(separator)) { 
2183             return EMPTY; 
2184         } 
2185         int pos = str.lastIndexOf(separator); 
2186         if (pos == INDEX_NOT_FOUND || pos == (str.length() - separator.length())) { 
2187             return EMPTY; 
2188         } 
2189         return str.substring(pos + separator.length()); 
2190     }

2192     // Substring between 
2193     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
2194     /** 
2195      * <p>Gets the String that is nested in between two instances of the 
2196      * same String.</p> 
2197      * 
2198      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2199      * A <code>null</code> tag returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
2200      * 
2201      * <pre> 
2202      * StringUtils.substringBetween(null, *)            = null 
2203      * StringUtils.substringBetween("", "")             = "" 
2204      * StringUtils.substringBetween("", "tag")          = null 
2205      * StringUtils.substringBetween("tagabctag", null)  = null 
2206      * StringUtils.substringBetween("tagabctag", "")    = "" 
2207      * StringUtils.substringBetween("tagabctag", "tag") = "abc" 
2208      * </pre> 
2209      * 
2210      * @param str  the String containing the substring, may be null 
2211      * @param tag  the String before and after the substring, may be null 
2212      * @return the substring, <code>null</code> if no match 
2213      * @since 2.0 
2214      */ 
2215     public static String substringBetween(String str, String tag) { 
2216         return substringBetween(str, tag, tag); 
2217     } 
2218  
2219     /** 
2220      * <p>Gets the String that is nested in between two Strings. 
2221      * Only the first match is returned.</p> 
2222      * 
2223      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2224      * A <code>null</code> open/close returns <code>null</code> (no match). 
2225      * An empty ("") open and close returns an empty string.</p> 
2226      * 
2227      * <pre> 
2228      * StringUtils.substringBetween("wx[b]yz", "[", "]") = "b" 
2229      * StringUtils.substringBetween(null, *, *)          = null 
2230      * StringUtils.substringBetween(*, null, *)          = null 
2231      * StringUtils.substringBetween(*, *, null)          = null 
2232      * StringUtils.substringBetween("", "", "")          = "" 
2233      * StringUtils.substringBetween("", "", "]")         = null 
2234      * StringUtils.substringBetween("", "[", "]")        = null 
2235      * StringUtils.substringBetween("yabcz", "", "")     = "" 
2236      * StringUtils.substringBetween("yabcz", "y", "z")   = "abc" 
2237      * StringUtils.substringBetween("yabczyabcz", "y", "z")   = "abc" 
2238      * </pre> 
2239      * 
2240      * @param str  the String containing the substring, may be null 
2241      * @param open  the String before the substring, may be null 
2242      * @param close  the String after the substring, may be null 
2243      * @return the substring, <code>null</code> if no match 
2244      * @since 2.0 
2245      */ 
2246     public static String substringBetween(String str, String open, String close) { 
2247         if (str == null || open == null || close == null) { 
2248             return null; 
2249         } 
2250         int start = str.indexOf(open); 
2251         if (start != INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
2252             int end = str.indexOf(close, start + open.length()); 
2253             if (end != INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
2254                 return str.substring(start + open.length(), end); 
2255             } 
2256         } 
2257         return null; 
2258     } 
2259  
2260     /** 
2261      * <p>Searches a String for substrings delimited by a start and end tag, 
2262      * returning all matching substrings in an array.</p> 
2263      * 
2264      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2265      * A <code>null</code> open/close returns <code>null</code> (no match). 
2266      * An empty ("") open/close returns <code>null</code> (no match).</p> 
2267      * 
2268      * <pre> 
2269      * StringUtils.substringsBetween("[a][b][c]", "[", "]") = ["a","b","c"] 
2270      * StringUtils.substringsBetween(null, *, *)            = null 
2271      * StringUtils.substringsBetween(*, null, *)            = null 
2272      * StringUtils.substringsBetween(*, *, null)            = null 
2273      * StringUtils.substringsBetween("", "[", "]")          = [] 
2274      * </pre> 
2275      * 
2276      * @param str  the String containing the substrings, null returns null, empty returns empty 
2277      * @param open  the String identifying the start of the substring, empty returns null 
2278      * @param close  the String identifying the end of the substring, empty returns null 
2279      * @return a String Array of substrings, or <code>null</code> if no match 
2280      * @since 2.3 
2281      */ 
2282     public static String[] substringsBetween(String str, String open, String close) { 
2283         if (str == null || isEmpty(open) || isEmpty(close)) { 
2284             return null; 
2285         } 
2286         int strLen = str.length(); 
2287         if (strLen == 0) { 
2288             return ArrayUtils.EMPTY_STRING_ARRAY; 
2289         } 
2290         int closeLen = close.length(); 
2291         int openLen = open.length(); 
2292         List<String> list = new ArrayList<String>(); 
2293         int pos = 0; 
2294         while (pos < (strLen - closeLen)) { 
2295             int start = str.indexOf(open, pos); 
2296             if (start < 0) { 
2297                 break; 
2298             } 
2299             start += openLen; 
2300             int end = str.indexOf(close, start); 
2301             if (end < 0) { 
2302                 break; 
2303             } 
2304             list.add(str.substring(start, end)); 
2305             pos = end + closeLen; 
2306         } 
2307         if (list.isEmpty()) { 
2308             return null; 
2309         }  
2310         return list.toArray(new String [list.size()]); 
2311     }

2318     /** 
2319      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, using whitespace as the 
2320      * separator. 
2321      * Whitespace is defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
2322      * 
2323      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2324      * Adjacent separators are treated as one separator. 
2325      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2326      * 
2327      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
2328      * 
2329      * <pre> 
2330      * StringUtils.split(null)       = null 
2331      * StringUtils.split("")         = [] 
2332      * StringUtils.split("abc def")  = ["abc", "def"] 
2333      * StringUtils.split("abc  def") = ["abc", "def"] 
2334      * StringUtils.split(" abc ")    = ["abc"] 
2335      * </pre> 
2336      * 
2337      * @param str  the String to parse, may be null 
2338      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2339      */ 
2340     public static String[] split(String str) { 
2341         return split(str, null, -1); 
2342     }

2349      * Adjacent separators are treated as one separator.

2359      * StringUtils.split("a:b:c", '.')    = ["a:b:c"] 
2360      * StringUtils.split("a b c", ' ')    = ["a", "b", "c"] 
2361      * </pre> 
2362      *

2428      *  array. A zero or negative value implies no limit 
2429      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2430      */ 
2431     public static String[] split(String str, String separatorChars, int max) { 
2432         return splitWorker(str, separatorChars, max, false); 
2433     }

2526      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2527      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2528      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2529      * 
2530      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2531      * A <code>null</code> separator splits on whitespace.</p> 
2532      * 
2533      * <pre> 
2534      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens(null, *, *)               = null 
2535      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("", *, *)                 = [] 
2536      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab de fg", null, 0)      = ["ab", "de", "fg"] 
2537      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null, 0)    = ["ab", "", "", "de", "fg"] 
2538      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef", ":", 2)       = ["ab", "cd:ef"] 
2539      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab-!-cd-!-ef", "-!-", 5) = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2540      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab-!-cd-!-ef", "-!-", 2) = ["ab", "cd-!-ef"] 
2541      * </pre> 
2542      * 
2543      * @param str  the String to parse, may be null 
2544      * @param separator  String containing the String to be used as a delimiter, 
2545      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace 
2546      * @param max  the maximum number of elements to include in the returned 
2547      *  array. A zero or negative value implies no limit. 
2548      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String was input 
2549      * @since 2.4 
2550      */ 
2551     public static String[] splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens(String str, String separator, int max) { 
2552         return splitByWholeSeparatorWorker(str, separator, max, true); 
2553     } 
2554  
2555     /** 
2556      * Performs the logic for the <code>splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens</code> methods. 
2557      * 
2558      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2559      * @param separator  String containing the String to be used as a delimiter, 
2560      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace 
2561      * @param max  the maximum number of elements to include in the returned 
2562      *  array. A zero or negative value implies no limit. 
2563      * @param preserveAllTokens if <code>true</code>, adjacent separators are 
2564      * treated as empty token separators; if <code>false</code>, adjacent 
2565      * separators are treated as one separator. 
2566      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2567      * @since 2.4 
2568      */ 
2569     private static String[] splitByWholeSeparatorWorker(String str, String separator, int max,  
2570                                                         boolean preserveAllTokens)  
2571     { 
2572         if (str == null) { 
2573             return null; 
2574         } 
2575  
2576         int len = str.length(); 
2577  
2578         if (len == 0) { 
2579             return ArrayUtils.EMPTY_STRING_ARRAY; 
2580         } 
2581  
2582         if ((separator == null) || (EMPTY.equals(separator))) { 
2583             // Split on whitespace. 
2584             return splitWorker(str, null, max, preserveAllTokens); 
2585         } 
2586  
2587         int separatorLength = separator.length(); 
2588  
2589         ArrayList<String> substrings = new ArrayList<String>(); 
2590         int numberOfSubstrings = 0; 
2591         int beg = 0; 
2592         int end = 0; 
2593         while (end < len) { 
2594             end = str.indexOf(separator, beg); 
2595  
2596             if (end > -1) { 
2597                 if (end > beg) { 
2598                     numberOfSubstrings += 1; 
2599  
2600                     if (numberOfSubstrings == max) { 
2601                         end = len; 
2602                         substrings.add(str.substring(beg)); 
2603                     } else { 
2604                         // The following is OK, because String.substring( beg, end ) excludes 
2605                         // the character at the position 'end'. 
2606                         substrings.add(str.substring(beg, end)); 
2607  
2608                         // Set the starting point for the next search. 
2609                         // The following is equivalent to beg = end + (separatorLength - 1) + 1, 
2610                         // which is the right calculation: 
2611                         beg = end + separatorLength; 
2612                     } 
2613                 } else { 
2614                     // We found a consecutive occurrence of the separator, so skip it. 
2615                     if (preserveAllTokens) { 
2616                         numberOfSubstrings += 1; 
2617                         if (numberOfSubstrings == max) { 
2618                             end = len; 
2619                             substrings.add(str.substring(beg)); 
2620                         } else { 
2621                             substrings.add(EMPTY); 
2622                         } 
2623                     } 
2624                     beg = end + separatorLength; 
2625                 } 
2626             } else { 
2627                 // String.substring( beg ) goes from 'beg' to the end of the String. 
2628                 substrings.add(str.substring(beg)); 
2629                 end = len; 
2630             } 
2631         } 
2632  
2633         return substrings.toArray(new String[substrings.size()]); 
2634     } 
2635  
2636     // ----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
2637     /** 
2638      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, using whitespace as the 
2639      * separator, preserving all tokens, including empty tokens created by  
2640      * adjacent separators. This is an alternative to using StringTokenizer. 
2641      * Whitespace is defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
2642      * 
2643      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2644      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2645      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2646      * 
2647      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
2648      * 
2649      * <pre> 
2650      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(null)       = null 
2651      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("")         = [] 
2652      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("abc def")  = ["abc", "def"] 
2653      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("abc  def") = ["abc", "", "def"] 
2654      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(" abc ")    = ["", "abc", ""] 
2655      * </pre> 
2656      * 
2657      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2658      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2659      * @since 2.1 
2660      */ 
2661     public static String[] splitPreserveAllTokens(String str) { 
2662         return splitWorker(str, null, -1, true); 
2663     } 
2664  
2665     /** 
2666      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, separator specified, 
2667      * preserving all tokens, including empty tokens created by adjacent 
2668      * separators. This is an alternative to using StringTokenizer.</p> 
2669      * 
2670      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2671      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2672      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2673      * 
2674      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
2675      * 
2676      * <pre> 
2677      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(null, *)         = null 
2678      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("", *)           = [] 
2679      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a.b.c", '.')    = ["a", "b", "c"] 
2680      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a..b.c", '.')   = ["a", "", "b", "c"] 
2681      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a:b:c", '.')    = ["a:b:c"] 
2682      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a\tb\nc", null) = ["a", "b", "c"] 
2683      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a b c", ' ')    = ["a", "b", "c"] 
2684      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a b c ", ' ')   = ["a", "b", "c", ""] 
2685      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a b c  ", ' ')   = ["a", "b", "c", "", ""] 
2686      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(" a b c", ' ')   = ["", a", "b", "c"] 
2687      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("  a b c", ' ')  = ["", "", a", "b", "c"] 
2688      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(" a b c ", ' ')  = ["", a", "b", "c", ""] 
2689      * </pre> 
2690      * 
2691      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2692      * @param separatorChar  the character used as the delimiter, 
2693      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace 
2694      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2695      * @since 2.1 
2696      */ 
2697     public static String[] splitPreserveAllTokens(String str, char separatorChar) { 
2698         return splitWorker(str, separatorChar, true); 
2699     } 
2700  
2701     /** 
2702      * Performs the logic for the <code>split</code> and  
2703      * <code>splitPreserveAllTokens</code> methods that do not return a 
2704      * maximum array length. 
2705      * 
2706      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2707      * @param separatorChar the separate character 
2708      * @param preserveAllTokens if <code>true</code>, adjacent separators are 
2709      * treated as empty token separators; if <code>false</code>, adjacent 
2710      * separators are treated as one separator. 
2711      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2712      */ 
2713     private static String[] splitWorker(String str, char separatorChar, boolean preserveAllTokens) { 
2714         // Performance tuned for 2.0 (JDK1.4) 
2715  
2716         if (str == null) { 
2717             return null; 
2718         } 
2719         int len = str.length(); 
2720         if (len == 0) { 
2721             return ArrayUtils.EMPTY_STRING_ARRAY; 
2722         } 
2723         List<String> list = new ArrayList<String>(); 
2724         int i = 0, start = 0; 
2725         boolean match = false; 
2726         boolean lastMatch = false; 
2727         while (i < len) { 
2728             if (str.charAt(i) == separatorChar) { 
2729                 if (match || preserveAllTokens) { 
2730                     list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2731                     match = false; 
2732                     lastMatch = true; 
2733                 } 
2734                 start = ++i; 
2735                 continue; 
2736             } 
2737             lastMatch = false; 
2738             match = true; 
2739             i++; 
2740         } 
2741         if (match || (preserveAllTokens && lastMatch)) { 
2742             list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2743         } 
2744         return list.toArray(new String[list.size()]); 
2745     } 
2746  
2747     /** 
2748      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, separators specified,  
2749      * preserving all tokens, including empty tokens created by adjacent 
2750      * separators. This is an alternative to using StringTokenizer.</p> 
2751      * 
2752      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2753      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2754      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2755      * 
2756      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2757      * A <code>null</code> separatorChars splits on whitespace.</p> 
2758      * 
2759      * <pre> 
2760      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(null, *)           = null 
2761      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("", *)             = [] 
2762      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("abc def", null)   = ["abc", "def"] 
2763      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("abc def", " ")    = ["abc", "def"] 
2764      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("abc  def", " ")   = ["abc", "", def"] 
2765      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef", ":")   = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2766      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef:", ":")  = ["ab", "cd", "ef", ""] 
2767      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef::", ":") = ["ab", "cd", "ef", "", ""] 
2768      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab::cd:ef", ":")  = ["ab", "", cd", "ef"] 
2769      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(":cd:ef", ":")     = ["", cd", "ef"] 
2770      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("::cd:ef", ":")    = ["", "", cd", "ef"] 
2771      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(":cd:ef:", ":")    = ["", cd", "ef", ""] 
2772      * </pre> 
2773      * 
2774      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2775      * @param separatorChars  the characters used as the delimiters, 
2776      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace 
2777      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2778      * @since 2.1 
2779      */ 
2780     public static String[] splitPreserveAllTokens(String str, String separatorChars) { 
2781         return splitWorker(str, separatorChars, -1, true); 
2782     } 
2783  
2784     /** 
2785      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array with a maximum length, 
2786      * separators specified, preserving all tokens, including empty tokens  
2787      * created by adjacent separators.</p> 
2788      * 
2789      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2790      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2791      * Adjacent separators are treated as one separator.</p> 
2792      * 
2793      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2794      * A <code>null</code> separatorChars splits on whitespace.</p> 
2795      * 
2796      * <p>If more than <code>max</code> delimited substrings are found, the last 
2797      * returned string includes all characters after the first <code>max - 1</code> 
2798      * returned strings (including separator characters).</p> 
2799      * 
2800      * <pre> 
2801      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(null, *, *)            = null 
2802      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("", *, *)              = [] 
2803      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab de fg", null, 0)   = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2804      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null, 0) = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2805      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef", ":", 0)    = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2806      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef", ":", 2)    = ["ab", "cd:ef"] 
2807      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null, 2) = ["ab", "  de fg"] 
2808      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null, 3) = ["ab", "", " de fg"] 
2809      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null, 4) = ["ab", "", "", "de fg"] 
2810      * </pre> 
2811      * 
2812      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2813      * @param separatorChars  the characters used as the delimiters, 
2814      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace 
2815      * @param max  the maximum number of elements to include in the 
2816      *  array. A zero or negative value implies no limit 
2817      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2818      * @since 2.1 
2819      */ 
2820     public static String[] splitPreserveAllTokens(String str, String separatorChars, int max) { 
2821         return splitWorker(str, separatorChars, max, true); 
2822     } 
2823  
2824     /** 
2825      * Performs the logic for the <code>split</code> and  
2826      * <code>splitPreserveAllTokens</code> methods that return a maximum array  
2827      * length. 
2828      * 
2829      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2830      * @param separatorChars the separate character 
2831      * @param max  the maximum number of elements to include in the 
2832      *  array. A zero or negative value implies no limit. 
2833      * @param preserveAllTokens if <code>true</code>, adjacent separators are 
2834      * treated as empty token separators; if <code>false</code>, adjacent 
2835      * separators are treated as one separator. 
2836      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2837      */ 
2838     private static String[] splitWorker(String str, String separatorChars, int max, boolean preserveAllTokens) { 
2839         // Performance tuned for 2.0 (JDK1.4) 
2840         // Direct code is quicker than StringTokenizer. 
2841         // Also, StringTokenizer uses isSpace() not isWhitespace() 
2842  
2843         if (str == null) { 
2844             return null; 
2845         } 
2846         int len = str.length(); 
2847         if (len == 0) { 
2848             return ArrayUtils.EMPTY_STRING_ARRAY; 
2849         } 
2850         List<String> list = new ArrayList<String>(); 
2851         int sizePlus1 = 1; 
2852         int i = 0, start = 0; 
2853         boolean match = false; 
2854         boolean lastMatch = false; 
2855         if (separatorChars == null) { 
2856             // Null separator means use whitespace 
2857             while (i < len) { 
2858                 if (Character.isWhitespace(str.charAt(i))) { 
2859                     if (match || preserveAllTokens) { 
2860                         lastMatch = true; 
2861                         if (sizePlus1++ == max) { 
2862                             i = len; 
2863                             lastMatch = false; 
2864                         } 
2865                         list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2866                         match = false; 
2867                     } 
2868                     start = ++i; 
2869                     continue; 
2870                 } 
2871                 lastMatch = false; 
2872                 match = true; 
2873                 i++; 
2874             } 
2875         } else if (separatorChars.length() == 1) { 
2876             // Optimise 1 character case 
2877             char sep = separatorChars.charAt(0); 
2878             while (i < len) { 
2879                 if (str.charAt(i) == sep) { 
2880                     if (match || preserveAllTokens) { 
2881                         lastMatch = true; 
2882                         if (sizePlus1++ == max) { 
2883                             i = len; 
2884                             lastMatch = false; 
2885                         } 
2886                         list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2887                         match = false; 
2888                     } 
2889                     start = ++i; 
2890                     continue; 
2891                 } 
2892                 lastMatch = false; 
2893                 match = true; 
2894                 i++; 
2895             } 
2896         } else { 
2897             // standard case 
2898             while (i < len) { 
2899                 if (separatorChars.indexOf(str.charAt(i)) >= 0) { 
2900                     if (match || preserveAllTokens) { 
2901                         lastMatch = true; 
2902                         if (sizePlus1++ == max) { 
2903                             i = len; 
2904                             lastMatch = false; 
2905                         } 
2906                         list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2907                         match = false; 
2908                     } 
2909                     start = ++i; 
2910                     continue; 
2911                 } 
2912                 lastMatch = false; 
2913                 match = true; 
2914                 i++; 
2915             } 
2916         } 
2917         if (match || (preserveAllTokens && lastMatch)) { 
2918             list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2919         } 
2920         return list.toArray(new String[list.size()]); 
2921     }

2923     /** 
2924      * <p>Splits a String by Character type as returned by 
2925      * <code>java.lang.Character.getType(char)</code>. Groups of contiguous 
2926      * characters of the same type are returned as complete tokens.  
2927      * <pre> 
2928      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType(null)         = null 
2929      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("")           = [] 
2930      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("ab de fg")   = ["ab", " ", "de", " ", "fg"] 
2931      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("ab   de fg") = ["ab", "   ", "de", " ", "fg"] 
2932      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("ab:cd:ef")   = ["ab", ":", "cd", ":", "ef"] 
2933      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("number5")    = ["number", "5"] 
2934      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("fooBar")     = ["foo", "B", "ar"] 
2935      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("foo200Bar")  = ["foo", "200", "B", "ar"] 
2936      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("ASFRules")   = ["ASFR", "ules"] 
2937      * </pre> 
2938      * @param str the String to split, may be <code>null</code> 
2939      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2940      * @since 2.4 
2941      */ 
2942     public static String[] splitByCharacterType(String str) { 
2943         return splitByCharacterType(str, false); 
2944     }

2946     /** 
2947      * <p>Splits a String by Character type as returned by 
2948      * <code>java.lang.Character.getType(char)</code>. Groups of contiguous 
2949      * characters of the same type are returned as complete tokens, with the 
2950      * following exception: the character of type 
2951      * <code>Character.UPPERCASE_LETTER</code>, if any, immediately 
2952      * preceding a token of type <code>Character.LOWERCASE_LETTER</code> 
2953      * will belong to the following token rather than to the preceding, if any, 
2954      * <code>Character.UPPERCASE_LETTER</code> token.  
2955      * <pre> 
2956      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase(null)         = null 
2957      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("")           = [] 
2958      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("ab de fg")   = ["ab", " ", "de", " ", "fg"] 
2959      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("ab   de fg") = ["ab", "   ", "de", " ", "fg"] 
2960      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("ab:cd:ef")   = ["ab", ":", "cd", ":", "ef"] 
2961      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("number5")    = ["number", "5"] 
2962      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("fooBar")     = ["foo", "Bar"] 
2963      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("foo200Bar")  = ["foo", "200", "Bar"] 
2964      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("ASFRules")   = ["ASF", "Rules"] 
2965      * </pre> 
2966      * @param str the String to split, may be <code>null</code> 
2967      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2968      * @since 2.4 
2969      */ 
2970     public static String[] splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase(String str) { 
2971         return splitByCharacterType(str, true); 
2972     } 
2973  
2974     /** 
2975      * <p>Splits a String by Character type as returned by 
2976      * <code>java.lang.Character.getType(char)</code>. Groups of contiguous 
2977      * characters of the same type are returned as complete tokens, with the 
2978      * following exception: if <code>camelCase</code> is <code>true</code>, 
2979      * the character of type <code>Character.UPPERCASE_LETTER</code>, if any, 
2980      * immediately preceding a token of type <code>Character.LOWERCASE_LETTER</code> 
2981      * will belong to the following token rather than to the preceding, if any, 
2982      * <code>Character.UPPERCASE_LETTER</code> token.  
2983      * @param str the String to split, may be <code>null</code> 
2984      * @param camelCase whether to use so-called "camel-case" for letter types 
2985      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2986      * @since 2.4 
2987      */ 
2988     private static String[] splitByCharacterType(String str, boolean camelCase) { 
2989         if (str == null) { 
2990             return null; 
2991         } 
2992         if (str.length() == 0) { 
2993             return ArrayUtils.EMPTY_STRING_ARRAY; 
2994         } 
2995         char[] c = str.toCharArray(); 
2996         List<String> list = new ArrayList<String>(); 
2997         int tokenStart = 0; 
2998         int currentType = Character.getType(c[tokenStart]); 
2999         for (int pos = tokenStart + 1; pos < c.length; pos++) { 
3000             int type = Character.getType(c[pos]); 
3001             if (type == currentType) { 
3002                 continue; 
3003             } 
3004             if (camelCase && type == Character.LOWERCASE_LETTER && currentType == Character.UPPERCASE_LETTER) { 
3005                 int newTokenStart = pos - 1; 
3006                 if (newTokenStart != tokenStart) { 
3007                     list.add(new String(c, tokenStart, newTokenStart - tokenStart)); 
3008                     tokenStart = newTokenStart; 
3009                 } 
3010             } else { 
3011                 list.add(new String(c, tokenStart, pos - tokenStart)); 
3012                 tokenStart = pos; 
3013             } 
3014             currentType = type; 
3015         } 
3016         list.add(new String(c, tokenStart, c.length - tokenStart)); 
3017         return list.toArray(new String[list.size()]); 
3018     }

3022     /** 
3023      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided array into a single String 
3024      * containing the provided list of elements.</p> 
3025      * 
3026      * <p>No separator is added to the joined String. 
3027      * Null objects or empty strings within the array are represented by 
3028      * empty strings.</p> 
3029      * 
3030      * <pre> 
3031      * StringUtils.join(null)            = null 
3032      * StringUtils.join([])              = "" 
3033      * StringUtils.join([null])          = "" 
3034      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"]) = "abc" 
3035      * StringUtils.join([null, "", "a"]) = "a" 
3036      * </pre> 
3037      * 
3038      * @param array  the array of values to join together, may be null 
3039      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null array input 
3040      * @since 2.0 
3041      */ 
3042     public static String join(Object[] array) { 
3043         return join(array, null); 
3044     }

3046     /** 
3047      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided array into a single String 
3048      * containing the provided list of elements.</p> 
3049      * 
3050      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3051      * Null objects or empty strings within the array are represented by 
3052      * empty strings.</p> 
3053      * 
3054      * <pre> 
3055      * StringUtils.join(null, *)               = null 
3056      * StringUtils.join([], *)                 = "" 
3057      * StringUtils.join([null], *)             = "" 
3058      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], ';')  = "a;b;c" 
3059      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], null) = "abc" 
3060      * StringUtils.join([null, "", "a"], ';')  = ";;a" 
3061      * </pre> 
3062      * 
3063      * @param array  the array of values to join together, may be null 
3064      * @param separator  the separator character to use 
3065      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null array input 
3066      * @since 2.0 
3067      */ 
3068     public static String join(Object[] array, char separator) { 
3069         if (array == null) { 
3070             return null; 
3071         } 
3072  
3073         return join(array, separator, 0, array.length); 
3074     }

3076     /** 
3077      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided array into a single String 
3078      * containing the provided list of elements.</p> 
3079      * 
3080      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3081      * Null objects or empty strings within the array are represented by 
3082      * empty strings.</p> 
3083      * 
3084      * <pre> 
3085      * StringUtils.join(null, *)               = null 
3086      * StringUtils.join([], *)                 = "" 
3087      * StringUtils.join([null], *)             = "" 
3088      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], ';')  = "a;b;c" 
3089      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], null) = "abc" 
3090      * StringUtils.join([null, "", "a"], ';')  = ";;a" 
3091      * </pre> 
3092      * 
3093      * @param array  the array of values to join together, may be null 
3094      * @param separator  the separator character to use 
3095      * @param startIndex the first index to start joining from.  It is 
3096      * an error to pass in an end index past the end of the array 
3097      * @param endIndex the index to stop joining from (exclusive). It is 
3098      * an error to pass in an end index past the end of the array 
3099      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null array input 
3100      * @since 2.0 
3101      */ 
3102     public static String join(Object[] array, char separator, int startIndex, int endIndex) { 
3103         if (array == null) { 
3104             return null; 
3105         } 
3106         int bufSize = (endIndex - startIndex); 
3107         if (bufSize <= 0) { 
3108             return EMPTY; 
3109         } 
3110  
3111         bufSize *= ((array[startIndex] == null ? 16 : array[startIndex].toString().length()) + 1); 
3112         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(bufSize); 
3113  
3114         for (int i = startIndex; i < endIndex; i++) { 
3115             if (i > startIndex) { 
3116                 buf.append(separator); 
3117             } 
3118             if (array[i] != null) { 
3119                 buf.append(array[i]); 
3120             } 
3121         } 
3122         return buf.toString(); 
3123     }
