3126     /** 
3127      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided array into a single String 
3128      * containing the provided list of elements.</p> 
3129      * 
3130      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3131      * A <code>null</code> separator is the same as an empty String (""). 
3132      * Null objects or empty strings within the array are represented by 
3133      * empty strings.</p> 
3134      * 
3135      * <pre> 
3136      * StringUtils.join(null, *)                = null 
3137      * StringUtils.join([], *)                  = "" 
3138      * StringUtils.join([null], *)              = "" 
3139      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], "--")  = "a--b--c" 
3140      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], null)  = "abc" 
3141      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], "")    = "abc" 
3142      * StringUtils.join([null, "", "a"], ',')   = ",,a" 
3143      * </pre> 
3144      * 
3145      * @param array  the array of values to join together, may be null 
3146      * @param separator  the separator character to use, null treated as "" 
3147      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null array input 
3148      */ 
3149     public static String join(Object[] array, String separator) { 
3150         if (array == null) { 
3151             return null; 
3152         } 
3153         return join(array, separator, 0, array.length); 
3154     }

3156     /** 
3157      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided array into a single String 
3158      * containing the provided list of elements.</p> 
3159      * 
3160      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3161      * A <code>null</code> separator is the same as an empty String (""). 
3162      * Null objects or empty strings within the array are represented by 
3163      * empty strings.</p> 
3164      * 
3165      * <pre> 
3166      * StringUtils.join(null, *)                = null 
3167      * StringUtils.join([], *)                  = "" 
3168      * StringUtils.join([null], *)              = "" 
3169      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], "--")  = "a--b--c" 
3170      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], null)  = "abc" 
3171      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], "")    = "abc" 
3172      * StringUtils.join([null, "", "a"], ',')   = ",,a" 
3173      * </pre> 
3174      * 
3175      * @param array  the array of values to join together, may be null 
3176      * @param separator  the separator character to use, null treated as "" 
3177      * @param startIndex the first index to start joining from.  It is 
3178      * an error to pass in an end index past the end of the array 
3179      * @param endIndex the index to stop joining from (exclusive). It is 
3180      * an error to pass in an end index past the end of the array 
3181      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null array input 
3182      */ 
3183     public static String join(Object[] array, String separator, int startIndex, int endIndex) { 
3184         if (array == null) { 
3185             return null; 
3186         } 
3187         if (separator == null) { 
3188             separator = EMPTY; 
3189         } 
3190  
3191         // endIndex - startIndex > 0:   Len = NofStrings *(len(firstString) + len(separator)) 
3192         //           (Assuming that all Strings are roughly equally long) 
3193         int bufSize = (endIndex - startIndex); 
3194         if (bufSize <= 0) { 
3195             return EMPTY; 
3196         } 
3197  
3198         bufSize *= ((array[startIndex] == null ? 16 : array[startIndex].toString().length()) 
3199                         + separator.length()); 
3200  
3201         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(bufSize); 
3202  
3203         for (int i = startIndex; i < endIndex; i++) { 
3204             if (i > startIndex) { 
3205                 buf.append(separator); 
3206             } 
3207             if (array[i] != null) { 
3208                 buf.append(array[i]); 
3209             } 
3210         } 
3211         return buf.toString(); 
3212     }

3214     /** 
3215      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided <code>Iterator</code> into 
3216      * a single String containing the provided elements.</p> 
3217      * 
3218      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. Null objects or empty 
3219      * strings within the iteration are represented by empty strings.</p> 
3220      * 
3221      * <p>See the examples here: {@link #join(Object[],char)}. </p> 
3222      * 
3223      * @param iterator  the <code>Iterator</code> of values to join together, may be null 
3224      * @param separator  the separator character to use 
3225      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null iterator input 
3226      * @since 2.0 
3227      */ 
3228     public static String join(Iterator<?> iterator, char separator) { 
3229  
3230         // handle null, zero and one elements before building a buffer 
3231         if (iterator == null) { 
3232             return null; 
3233         } 
3234         if (!iterator.hasNext()) { 
3235             return EMPTY; 
3236         } 
3237         Object first = iterator.next(); 
3238         if (!iterator.hasNext()) { 
3239             return ObjectUtils.toString(first); 
3240         } 
3241  
3242         // two or more elements 
3243         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(256); // Java default is 16, probably too small 
3244         if (first != null) { 
3245             buf.append(first); 
3246         } 
3247  
3248         while (iterator.hasNext()) { 
3249             buf.append(separator); 
3250             Object obj = iterator.next(); 
3251             if (obj != null) { 
3252                 buf.append(obj); 
3253             } 
3254         } 
3255  
3256         return buf.toString(); 
3257     } 
3258  
3259     /** 
3260      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided <code>Iterator</code> into 
3261      * a single String containing the provided elements.</p> 
3262      * 
3263      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3264      * A <code>null</code> separator is the same as an empty String ("").</p> 
3265      * 
3266      * <p>See the examples here: {@link #join(Object[],String)}. </p> 
3267      * 
3268      * @param iterator  the <code>Iterator</code> of values to join together, may be null 
3269      * @param separator  the separator character to use, null treated as "" 
3270      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null iterator input 
3271      */ 
3272     public static String join(Iterator<?> iterator, String separator) { 
3273  
3274         // handle null, zero and one elements before building a buffer 
3275         if (iterator == null) { 
3276             return null; 
3277         } 
3278         if (!iterator.hasNext()) { 
3279             return EMPTY; 
3280         } 
3281         Object first = iterator.next(); 
3282         if (!iterator.hasNext()) { 
3283             return ObjectUtils.toString(first); 
3284         } 
3285  
3286         // two or more elements 
3287         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(256); // Java default is 16, probably too small 
3288         if (first != null) { 
3289             buf.append(first); 
3290         } 
3291  
3292         while (iterator.hasNext()) { 
3293             if (separator != null) { 
3294                 buf.append(separator); 
3295             } 
3296             Object obj = iterator.next(); 
3297             if (obj != null) { 
3298                 buf.append(obj); 
3299             } 
3300         } 
3301         return buf.toString(); 
3302     } 
3303  
3304     /** 
3305      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided <code>Iterable</code> into 
3306      * a single String containing the provided elements.</p> 
3307      * 
3308      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. Null objects or empty 
3309      * strings within the iteration are represented by empty strings.</p> 
3310      * 
3311      * <p>See the examples here: {@link #join(Object[],char)}. </p> 
3312      * 
3313      * @param iterable  the <code>Iterable</code> providing the values to join together, may be null 
3314      * @param separator  the separator character to use 
3315      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null iterator input 
3316      * @since 2.3 
3317      */ 
3318     public static String join(Iterable<?> iterable, char separator) { 
3319         if (iterable == null) { 
3320             return null; 
3321         } 
3322         return join(iterable.iterator(), separator); 
3323     } 
3324  
3325     /** 
3326      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided <code>Iterable</code> into 
3327      * a single String containing the provided elements.</p> 
3328      * 
3329      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3330      * A <code>null</code> separator is the same as an empty String ("").</p> 
3331      * 
3332      * <p>See the examples here: {@link #join(Object[],String)}. </p> 
3333      * 
3334      * @param iterable  the <code>Iterable</code> providing the values to join together, may be null 
3335      * @param separator  the separator character to use, null treated as "" 
3336      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null iterator input 
3337      * @since 2.3 
3338      */ 
3339     public static String join(Iterable<?> iterable, String separator) { 
3340         if (iterable == null) { 
3341             return null; 
3342         } 
3343         return join(iterable.iterator(), separator); 
3344     } 
3345  
3346     // Delete 
3347     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
3348     /** 
3349      * <p>Deletes all whitespaces from a String as defined by 
3350      * {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
3351      * 
3352      * <pre> 
3353      * StringUtils.deleteWhitespace(null)         = null 
3354      * StringUtils.deleteWhitespace("")           = "" 
3355      * StringUtils.deleteWhitespace("abc")        = "abc" 
3356      * StringUtils.deleteWhitespace("   ab  c  ") = "abc" 
3357      * </pre> 
3358      * 
3359      * @param str  the String to delete whitespace from, may be null 
3360      * @return the String without whitespaces, <code>null</code> if null String input 
3361      */ 
3362     public static String deleteWhitespace(String str) { 
3363         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
3364             return str; 
3365         } 
3366         int sz = str.length(); 
3367         char[] chs = new char[sz]; 
3368         int count = 0; 
3369         for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) { 
3370             if (!Character.isWhitespace(str.charAt(i))) { 
3371                 chs[count++] = str.charAt(i); 
3372             } 
3373         } 
3374         if (count == sz) { 
3375             return str; 
3376         } 
3377         return new String(chs, 0, count); 
3378     } 
3379  
3380     // Remove 
3381     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
3382     /** 
3383      * <p>Removes a substring only if it is at the begining of a source string, 
3384      * otherwise returns the source string.</p> 
3385      * 
3386      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3387      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string. 
3388      * A <code>null</code> search string will return the source string.</p> 
3389      * 
3390      * <pre> 
3391      * StringUtils.removeStart(null, *)      = null 
3392      * StringUtils.removeStart("", *)        = "" 
3393      * StringUtils.removeStart(*, null)      = * 
3394      * StringUtils.removeStart("www.domain.com", "www.")   = "domain.com" 
3395      * StringUtils.removeStart("domain.com", "www.")       = "domain.com" 
3396      * StringUtils.removeStart("www.domain.com", "domain") = "www.domain.com" 
3397      * StringUtils.removeStart("abc", "")    = "abc" 
3398      * </pre> 
3399      * 
3400      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3401      * @param remove  the String to search for and remove, may be null 
3402      * @return the substring with the string removed if found, 
3403      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3404      * @since 2.1 
3405      */ 
3406     public static String removeStart(String str, String remove) { 
3407         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(remove)) { 
3408             return str; 
3409         } 
3410         if (str.startsWith(remove)){ 
3411             return str.substring(remove.length()); 
3412         } 
3413         return str; 
3414     } 
3415  
3416     /** 
3417      * <p>Case insensitive removal of a substring if it is at the begining of a source string, 
3418      * otherwise returns the source string.</p> 
3419      * 
3420      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3421      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string. 
3422      * A <code>null</code> search string will return the source string.</p> 
3423      * 
3424      * <pre> 
3425      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase(null, *)      = null 
3426      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("", *)        = "" 
3427      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase(*, null)      = * 
3428      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", "www.")   = "domain.com" 
3429      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", "WWW.")   = "domain.com" 
3430      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("domain.com", "www.")       = "domain.com" 
3431      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", "domain") = "www.domain.com" 
3432      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("abc", "")    = "abc" 
3433      * </pre> 
3434      * 
3435      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3436      * @param remove  the String to search for (case insensitive) and remove, may be null 
3437      * @return the substring with the string removed if found, 
3438      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3439      * @since 2.4 
3440      */ 
3441     public static String removeStartIgnoreCase(String str, String remove) { 
3442         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(remove)) { 
3443             return str; 
3444         } 
3445         if (startsWithIgnoreCase(str, remove)) { 
3446             return str.substring(remove.length()); 
3447         } 
3448         return str; 
3449     }

3451     /** 
3452      * <p>Removes a substring only if it is at the end of a source string, 
3453      * otherwise returns the source string.</p> 
3454      * 
3455      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3456      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string. 
3457      * A <code>null</code> search string will return the source string.</p> 
3458      * 
3459      * <pre> 
3460      * StringUtils.removeEnd(null, *)      = null 
3461      * StringUtils.removeEnd("", *)        = "" 
3462      * StringUtils.removeEnd(*, null)      = * 
3463      * StringUtils.removeEnd("www.domain.com", ".com.")  = "www.domain.com" 
3464      * StringUtils.removeEnd("www.domain.com", ".com")   = "www.domain" 
3465      * StringUtils.removeEnd("www.domain.com", "domain") = "www.domain.com" 
3466      * StringUtils.removeEnd("abc", "")    = "abc" 
3467      * </pre> 
3468      * 
3469      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3470      * @param remove  the String to search for and remove, may be null 
3471      * @return the substring with the string removed if found, 
3472      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3473      * @since 2.1 
3474      */ 
3475     public static String removeEnd(String str, String remove) { 
3476         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(remove)) { 
3477             return str; 
3478         } 
3479         if (str.endsWith(remove)) { 
3480             return str.substring(0, str.length() - remove.length()); 
3481         } 
3482         return str; 
3483     } 
3484  
3485     /** 
3486      * <p>Case insensitive removal of a substring if it is at the end of a source string, 
3487      * otherwise returns the source string.</p> 
3488      * 
3489      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3490      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string. 
3491      * A <code>null</code> search string will return the source string.</p> 
3492      * 
3493      * <pre> 
3494      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase(null, *)      = null 
3495      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("", *)        = "" 
3496      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase(*, null)      = * 
3497      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", ".com.")  = "www.domain.com" 
3498      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", ".com")   = "www.domain" 
3499      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", "domain") = "www.domain.com" 
3500      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("abc", "")    = "abc" 
3501      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", ".COM") = "www.domain") 
3502      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("www.domain.COM", ".com") = "www.domain") 
3503      * </pre> 
3504      * 
3505      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3506      * @param remove  the String to search for (case insensitive) and remove, may be null 
3507      * @return the substring with the string removed if found, 
3508      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3509      * @since 2.4 
3510      */ 
3511     public static String removeEndIgnoreCase(String str, String remove) { 
3512         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(remove)) { 
3513             return str; 
3514         } 
3515         if (endsWithIgnoreCase(str, remove)) { 
3516             return str.substring(0, str.length() - remove.length()); 
3517         } 
3518         return str; 
3519     }

3521     /** 
3522      * <p>Removes all occurrences of a substring from within the source string.</p> 
3523      * 
3524      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3525      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string. 
3526      * A <code>null</code> remove string will return the source string. 
3527      * An empty ("") remove string will return the source string.</p> 
3528      * 
3529      * <pre> 
3530      * StringUtils.remove(null, *)        = null 
3531      * StringUtils.remove("", *)          = "" 
3532      * StringUtils.remove(*, null)        = * 
3533      * StringUtils.remove(*, "")          = * 
3534      * StringUtils.remove("queued", "ue") = "qd" 
3535      * StringUtils.remove("queued", "zz") = "queued" 
3536      * </pre> 
3537      * 
3538      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3539      * @param remove  the String to search for and remove, may be null 
3540      * @return the substring with the string removed if found, 
3541      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3542      * @since 2.1 
3543      */ 
3544     public static String remove(String str, String remove) { 
3545         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(remove)) { 
3546             return str; 
3547         } 
3548         return replace(str, remove, EMPTY, -1); 
3549     } 
3550  
3551     /** 
3552      * <p>Removes all occurrences of a character from within the source string.</p> 
3553      * 
3554      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3555      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string.</p> 
3556      * 
3557      * <pre> 
3558      * StringUtils.remove(null, *)       = null 
3559      * StringUtils.remove("", *)         = "" 
3560      * StringUtils.remove("queued", 'u') = "qeed" 
3561      * StringUtils.remove("queued", 'z') = "queued" 
3562      * </pre> 
3563      * 
3564      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3565      * @param remove  the char to search for and remove, may be null 
3566      * @return the substring with the char removed if found, 
3567      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3568      * @since 2.1 
3569      */ 
3570     public static String remove(String str, char remove) { 
3571         if (isEmpty(str) || str.indexOf(remove) == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
3572             return str; 
3573         } 
3574         char[] chars = str.toCharArray(); 
3575         int pos = 0; 
3576         for (int i = 0; i < chars.length; i++) { 
3577             if (chars[i] != remove) { 
3578                 chars[pos++] = chars[i]; 
3579             } 
3580         } 
3581         return new String(chars, 0, pos); 
3582     }

3584     // Replacing 
3585     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
3586     /** 
3587      * <p>Replaces a String with another String inside a larger String, once.</p> 
3588      * 
3589      * <p>A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op.</p> 
3590      * 
3591      * <pre> 
3592      * StringUtils.replaceOnce(null, *, *)        = null 
3593      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("", *, *)          = "" 
3594      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("any", null, *)    = "any" 
3595      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("any", *, null)    = "any" 
3596      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("any", "", *)      = "any" 
3597      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("aba", "a", null)  = "aba" 
3598      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("aba", "a", "")    = "ba" 
3599      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("aba", "a", "z")   = "zba" 
3600      * </pre> 
3601      * 
3602      * @see #replace(String text, String searchString, String replacement, int max) 
3603      * @param text  text to search and replace in, may be null 
3604      * @param searchString  the String to search for, may be null 
3605      * @param replacement  the String to replace with, may be null 
3606      * @return the text with any replacements processed, 
3607      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3608      */ 
3609     public static String replaceOnce(String text, String searchString, String replacement) { 
3610         return replace(text, searchString, replacement, 1); 
3611     } 
3612  
3613     /** 
3614      * <p>Replaces all occurrences of a String within another String.</p> 
3615      * 
3616      * <p>A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op.</p> 
3617      * 
3618      * <pre> 
3619      * StringUtils.replace(null, *, *)        = null 
3620      * StringUtils.replace("", *, *)          = "" 
3621      * StringUtils.replace("any", null, *)    = "any" 
3622      * StringUtils.replace("any", *, null)    = "any" 
3623      * StringUtils.replace("any", "", *)      = "any" 
3624      * StringUtils.replace("aba", "a", null)  = "aba" 
3625      * StringUtils.replace("aba", "a", "")    = "b" 
3626      * StringUtils.replace("aba", "a", "z")   = "zbz" 
3627      * </pre> 
3628      * 
3629      * @see #replace(String text, String searchString, String replacement, int max) 
3630      * @param text  text to search and replace in, may be null 
3631      * @param searchString  the String to search for, may be null 
3632      * @param replacement  the String to replace it with, may be null 
3633      * @return the text with any replacements processed, 
3634      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3635      */ 
3636     public static String replace(String text, String searchString, String replacement) { 
3637         return replace(text, searchString, replacement, -1); 
3638     } 
3639  
3640     /** 
3641      * <p>Replaces a String with another String inside a larger String, 
3642      * for the first <code>max</code> values of the search String.</p> 
3643      * 
3644      * <p>A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op.</p> 
3645      * 
3646      * <pre> 
3647      * StringUtils.replace(null, *, *, *)         = null 
3648      * StringUtils.replace("", *, *, *)           = "" 
3649      * StringUtils.replace("any", null, *, *)     = "any" 
3650      * StringUtils.replace("any", *, null, *)     = "any" 
3651      * StringUtils.replace("any", "", *, *)       = "any" 
3652      * StringUtils.replace("any", *, *, 0)        = "any" 
3653      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", null, -1) = "abaa" 
3654      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", "", -1)   = "b" 
3655      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", "z", 0)   = "abaa" 
3656      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", "z", 1)   = "zbaa" 
3657      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", "z", 2)   = "zbza" 
3658      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", "z", -1)  = "zbzz" 
3659      * </pre> 
3660      * 
3661      * @param text  text to search and replace in, may be null 
3662      * @param searchString  the String to search for, may be null 
3663      * @param replacement  the String to replace it with, may be null 
3664      * @param max  maximum number of values to replace, or <code>-1</code> if no maximum 
3665      * @return the text with any replacements processed, 
3666      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3667      */ 
3668     public static String replace(String text, String searchString, String replacement, int max) { 
3669         if (isEmpty(text) || isEmpty(searchString) || replacement == null || max == 0) { 
3670             return text; 
3671         } 
3672         int start = 0; 
3673         int end = text.indexOf(searchString, start); 
3674         if (end == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
3675             return text; 
3676         } 
3677         int replLength = searchString.length(); 
3678         int increase = replacement.length() - replLength; 
3679         increase = (increase < 0 ? 0 : increase); 
3680         increase *= (max < 0 ? 16 : (max > 64 ? 64 : max)); 
3681         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(text.length() + increase); 
3682         while (end != INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
3683             buf.append(text.substring(start, end)).append(replacement); 
3684             start = end + replLength; 
3685             if (--max == 0) { 
3686                 break; 
3687             } 
3688             end = text.indexOf(searchString, start); 
3689         } 
3690         buf.append(text.substring(start)); 
3691         return buf.toString(); 
3692     } 
3693  
3694     /** 
3695      * <p> 
3696      * Replaces all occurrences of Strings within another String. 
3697      * </p> 
3698      *  
3699      * <p> 
3700      * A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op, or if 
3701      * any "search string" or "string to replace" is null, that replace will be 
3702      * ignored. This will not repeat. For repeating replaces, call the 
3703      * overloaded method. 
3704      * </p> 
3705      *  
3706      * <pre> 
3707      *  StringUtils.replaceEach(null, *, *)        = null 
3708      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("", *, *)          = "" 
3709      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, null) = "aba" 
3710      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[0], null) = "aba" 
3711      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, new String[0]) = "aba" 
3712      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, null)  = "aba" 
3713      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, new String[]{""})  = "b" 
3714      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{null}, new String[]{"a"})  = "aba" 
3715      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"w", "t"})  = "wcte" 
3716      *  (example of how it does not repeat) 
3717      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "t"})  = "dcte" 
3718      * </pre> 
3719      *  
3720      * @param text 
3721      *            text to search and replace in, no-op if null 
3722      * @param searchList 
3723      *            the Strings to search for, no-op if null 
3724      * @param replacementList 
3725      *            the Strings to replace them with, no-op if null 
3726      * @return the text with any replacements processed, <code>null</code> if 
3727      *         null String input 
3728      * @throws IndexOutOfBoundsException 
3729      *             if the lengths of the arrays are not the same (null is ok, 
3730      *             and/or size 0) 
3731      * @since 2.4 
3732      */ 
3733     public static String replaceEach(String text, String[] searchList, String[] replacementList) { 
3734         return replaceEach(text, searchList, replacementList, false, 0); 
3735     } 
3736  
3737     /** 
3738      * <p> 
3739      * Replaces all occurrences of Strings within another String. 
3740      * </p> 
3741      *  
3742      * <p> 
3743      * A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op, or if 
3744      * any "search string" or "string to replace" is null, that replace will be 
3745      * ignored. This will not repeat. For repeating replaces, call the 
3746      * overloaded method. 
3747      * </p> 
3748      *  
3749      * <pre> 
3750      *  StringUtils.replaceEach(null, *, *, *) = null 
3751      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("", *, *, *) = "" 
3752      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, null, *) = "aba" 
3753      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[0], null, *) = "aba" 
3754      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, new String[0], *) = "aba" 
3755      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, null, *) = "aba" 
3756      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, new String[]{""}, *) = "b" 
3757      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{null}, new String[]{"a"}, *) = "aba" 
3758      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"w", "t"}, *) = "wcte" 
3759      *  (example of how it repeats) 
3760      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "t"}, false) = "dcte" 
3761      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "t"}, true) = "tcte" 
3762      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "ab"}, true) = IllegalArgumentException 
3763      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "ab"}, false) = "dcabe" 
3764      * </pre> 
3765      *  
3766      * @param text 
3767      *            text to search and replace in, no-op if null 
3768      * @param searchList 
3769      *            the Strings to search for, no-op if null 
3770      * @param replacementList 
3771      *            the Strings to replace them with, no-op if null 
3772      * @return the text with any replacements processed, <code>null</code> if 
3773      *         null String input 
3774      * @throws IllegalArgumentException 
3775      *             if the search is repeating and there is an endless loop due 
3776      *             to outputs of one being inputs to another 
3777      * @throws IndexOutOfBoundsException 
3778      *             if the lengths of the arrays are not the same (null is ok, 
3779      *             and/or size 0) 
3780      * @since 2.4 
3781      */ 
3782     public static String replaceEachRepeatedly(String text, String[] searchList, String[] replacementList) { 
3783         // timeToLive should be 0 if not used or nothing to replace, else it's 
3784         // the length of the replace array 
3785         int timeToLive = searchList == null ? 0 : searchList.length; 
3786         return replaceEach(text, searchList, replacementList, true, timeToLive); 
3787     } 
3788  
3789     /** 
3790      * <p> 
3791      * Replaces all occurrences of Strings within another String. 
3792      * </p> 
3793      *  
3794      * <p> 
3795      * A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op, or if 
3796      * any "search string" or "string to replace" is null, that replace will be 
3797      * ignored.  
3798      * </p> 
3799      *  
3800      * <pre> 
3801      *  StringUtils.replaceEach(null, *, *, *) = null 
3802      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("", *, *, *) = "" 
3803      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, null, *) = "aba" 
3804      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[0], null, *) = "aba" 
3805      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, new String[0], *) = "aba" 
3806      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, null, *) = "aba" 
3807      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, new String[]{""}, *) = "b" 
3808      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{null}, new String[]{"a"}, *) = "aba" 
3809      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"w", "t"}, *) = "wcte" 
3810      *  (example of how it repeats) 
3811      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "t"}, false) = "dcte" 
3812      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "t"}, true) = "tcte" 
3813      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "ab"}, *) = IllegalArgumentException 
3814      * </pre> 
3815      *  
3816      * @param text 
3817      *            text to search and replace in, no-op if null 
3818      * @param searchList 
3819      *            the Strings to search for, no-op if null 
3820      * @param replacementList 
3821      *            the Strings to replace them with, no-op if null 
3822      * @param repeat if true, then replace repeatedly  
3823      *       until there are no more possible replacements or timeToLive < 0 
3824      * @param timeToLive 
3825      *            if less than 0 then there is a circular reference and endless 
3826      *            loop 
3827      * @return the text with any replacements processed, <code>null</code> if 
3828      *         null String input 
3829      * @throws IllegalArgumentException 
3830      *             if the search is repeating and there is an endless loop due 
3831      *             to outputs of one being inputs to another 
3832      * @throws IndexOutOfBoundsException 
3833      *             if the lengths of the arrays are not the same (null is ok, 
3834      *             and/or size 0) 
3835      * @since 2.4 
3836      */ 
3837     private static String replaceEach(String text, String[] searchList, String[] replacementList,  
3838                                       boolean repeat, int timeToLive)  
3839     { 
3840  
3841         // mchyzer Performance note: This creates very few new objects (one major goal) 
3842         // let me know if there are performance requests, we can create a harness to measure 
3843  
3844         if (text == null || text.length() == 0 || searchList == null ||  
3845             searchList.length == 0 || replacementList == null || replacementList.length == 0)  
3846         { 
3847             return text; 
3848         } 
3849  
3850         // if recursing, this shouldnt be less than 0 
3851         if (timeToLive < 0) { 
3852             throw new IllegalStateException("TimeToLive of " + timeToLive + " is less than 0: " + text); 
3853         } 
3854  
3855         int searchLength = searchList.length; 
3856         int replacementLength = replacementList.length; 
3857  
3858         // make sure lengths are ok, these need to be equal 
3859         if (searchLength != replacementLength) { 
3860             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Search and Replace array lengths don't match: " 
3861                 + searchLength 
3862                 + " vs " 
3863                 + replacementLength); 
3864         } 
3865  
3866         // keep track of which still have matches 
3867         boolean[] noMoreMatchesForReplIndex = new boolean[searchLength]; 
3868  
3869         // index on index that the match was found 
3870         int textIndex = -1; 
3871         int replaceIndex = -1; 
3872         int tempIndex = -1; 
3873  
3874         // index of replace array that will replace the search string found 
3875         // NOTE: logic duplicated below START 
3876         for (int i = 0; i < searchLength; i++) { 
3877             if (noMoreMatchesForReplIndex[i] || searchList[i] == null ||  
3878                 searchList[i].length() == 0 || replacementList[i] == null)  
3879             { 
3880                 continue; 
3881             } 
3882             tempIndex = text.indexOf(searchList[i]); 
3883  
3884             // see if we need to keep searching for this 
3885             if (tempIndex == -1) { 
3886                 noMoreMatchesForReplIndex[i] = true; 
3887             } else { 
3888                 if (textIndex == -1 || tempIndex < textIndex) { 
3889                     textIndex = tempIndex; 
3890                     replaceIndex = i; 
3891                 } 
3892             } 
3893         } 
3894         // NOTE: logic mostly below END 
3895  
3896         // no search strings found, we are done 
3897         if (textIndex == -1) { 
3898             return text; 
3899         } 
3900  
3901         int start = 0; 
3902  
3903         // get a good guess on the size of the result buffer so it doesnt have to double if it goes over a bit 
3904         int increase = 0; 
3905  
3906         // count the replacement text elements that are larger than their corresponding text being replaced 
3907         for (int i = 0; i < searchList.length; i++) { 
3908             if (searchList[i] == null || replacementList[i] == null) { 
3909                 continue; 
3910             } 
3911             int greater = replacementList[i].length() - searchList[i].length(); 
3912             if (greater > 0) { 
3913                 increase += 3 * greater; // assume 3 matches 
3914             } 
3915         } 
3916         // have upper-bound at 20% increase, then let Java take over 
3917         increase = Math.min(increase, text.length() / 5); 
3918  
3919         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(text.length() + increase); 
3920  
3921         while (textIndex != -1) { 
3922  
3923             for (int i = start; i < textIndex; i++) { 
3924                 buf.append(text.charAt(i)); 
3925             } 
3926             buf.append(replacementList[replaceIndex]); 
3927  
3928             start = textIndex + searchList[replaceIndex].length(); 
3929  
3930             textIndex = -1; 
3931             replaceIndex = -1; 
3932             tempIndex = -1; 
3933             // find the next earliest match 
3934             // NOTE: logic mostly duplicated above START 
3935             for (int i = 0; i < searchLength; i++) { 
3936                 if (noMoreMatchesForReplIndex[i] || searchList[i] == null ||  
3937                     searchList[i].length() == 0 || replacementList[i] == null)  
3938                 { 
3939                     continue; 
3940                 } 
3941                 tempIndex = text.indexOf(searchList[i], start); 
3942  
3943                 // see if we need to keep searching for this 
3944                 if (tempIndex == -1) { 
3945                     noMoreMatchesForReplIndex[i] = true; 
3946                 } else { 
3947                     if (textIndex == -1 || tempIndex < textIndex) { 
3948                         textIndex = tempIndex; 
3949                         replaceIndex = i; 
3950                     } 
3951                 } 
3952             } 
3953             // NOTE: logic duplicated above END 
3954  
3955         } 
3956         int textLength = text.length(); 
3957         for (int i = start; i < textLength; i++) { 
3958             buf.append(text.charAt(i)); 
3959         } 
3960         String result = buf.toString(); 
3961         if (!repeat) { 
3962             return result; 
3963         } 
3964  
3965         return replaceEach(result, searchList, replacementList, repeat, timeToLive - 1); 
3966     } 
3967  
3968     // Replace, character based 
3969     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
3970     /** 
3971      * <p>Replaces all occurrences of a character in a String with another. 
3972      * This is a null-safe version of {@link String#replace(char, char)}.</p> 
3973      * 
3974      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input returns <code>null</code>. 
3975      * An empty ("") string input returns an empty string.</p> 
3976      * 
3977      * <pre> 
3978      * StringUtils.replaceChars(null, *, *)        = null 
3979      * StringUtils.replaceChars("", *, *)          = "" 
3980      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abcba", 'b', 'y') = "aycya" 
3981      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abcba", 'z', 'y') = "abcba" 
3982      * </pre> 
3983      * 
3984      * @param str  String to replace characters in, may be null 
3985      * @param searchChar  the character to search for, may be null 
3986      * @param replaceChar  the character to replace, may be null 
3987      * @return modified String, <code>null</code> if null string input 
3988      * @since 2.0 
3989      */ 
3990     public static String replaceChars(String str, char searchChar, char replaceChar) { 
3991         if (str == null) { 
3992             return null; 
3993         } 
3994         return str.replace(searchChar, replaceChar); 
3995     } 
3996  
3997     /** 
3998      * <p>Replaces multiple characters in a String in one go. 
3999      * This method can also be used to delete characters.</p> 
4000      * 
4001      * <p>For example:<br /> 
4002      * <code>replaceChars(&quot;hello&quot;, &quot;ho&quot;, &quot;jy&quot;) = jelly</code>.</p> 
4003      * 
4004      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input returns <code>null</code>. 
4005      * An empty ("") string input returns an empty string. 
4006      * A null or empty set of search characters returns the input string.</p> 
4007      * 
4008      * <p>The length of the search characters should normally equal the length 
4009      * of the replace characters. 
4010      * If the search characters is longer, then the extra search characters 
4011      * are deleted. 
4012      * If the search characters is shorter, then the extra replace characters 
4013      * are ignored.</p> 
4014      * 
4015      * <pre> 
4016      * StringUtils.replaceChars(null, *, *)           = null 
4017      * StringUtils.replaceChars("", *, *)             = "" 
4018      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abc", null, *)       = "abc" 
4019      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abc", "", *)         = "abc" 
4020      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abc", "b", null)     = "ac" 
4021      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abc", "b", "")       = "ac" 
4022      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abcba", "bc", "yz")  = "ayzya" 
4023      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abcba", "bc", "y")   = "ayya" 
4024      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abcba", "bc", "yzx") = "ayzya" 
4025      * </pre> 
4026      * 
4027      * @param str  String to replace characters in, may be null 
4028      * @param searchChars  a set of characters to search for, may be null 
4029      * @param replaceChars  a set of characters to replace, may be null 
4030      * @return modified String, <code>null</code> if null string input 
4031      * @since 2.0 
4032      */ 
4033     public static String replaceChars(String str, String searchChars, String replaceChars) { 
4034         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
4035             return str; 
4036         } 
4037         if (replaceChars == null) { 
4038             replaceChars = EMPTY; 
4039         } 
4040         boolean modified = false; 
4041         int replaceCharsLength = replaceChars.length(); 
4042         int strLength = str.length(); 
4043         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(strLength); 
4044         for (int i = 0; i < strLength; i++) { 
4045             char ch = str.charAt(i); 
4046             int index = searchChars.indexOf(ch); 
4047             if (index >= 0) { 
4048                 modified = true; 
4049                 if (index < replaceCharsLength) { 
4050                     buf.append(replaceChars.charAt(index)); 
4051                 } 
4052             } else { 
4053                 buf.append(ch); 
4054             } 
4055         } 
4056         if (modified) { 
4057             return buf.toString(); 
4058         } 
4059         return str; 
4060     } 
4061  
4062     // Overlay 
4063     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4064     /** 
4065      * <p>Overlays part of a String with another String.</p> 
4066      * 
4067      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input returns <code>null</code>. 
4068      * A negative index is treated as zero. 
4069      * An index greater than the string length is treated as the string length. 
4070      * The start index is always the smaller of the two indices.</p> 
4071      * 
4072      * <pre> 
4073      * StringUtils.overlay(null, *, *, *)            = null 
4074      * StringUtils.overlay("", "abc", 0, 0)          = "abc" 
4075      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", null, 2, 4)     = "abef" 
4076      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "", 2, 4)       = "abef" 
4077      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "", 4, 2)       = "abef" 
4078      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", 2, 4)   = "abzzzzef" 
4079      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", 4, 2)   = "abzzzzef" 
4080      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", -1, 4)  = "zzzzef" 
4081      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", 2, 8)   = "abzzzz" 
4082      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", -2, -3) = "zzzzabcdef" 
4083      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", 8, 10)  = "abcdefzzzz" 
4084      * </pre> 
4085      * 
4086      * @param str  the String to do overlaying in, may be null 
4087      * @param overlay  the String to overlay, may be null 
4088      * @param start  the position to start overlaying at 
4089      * @param end  the position to stop overlaying before 
4090      * @return overlayed String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4091      * @since 2.0 
4092      */ 
4093     public static String overlay(String str, String overlay, int start, int end) { 
4094         if (str == null) { 
4095             return null; 
4096         } 
4097         if (overlay == null) { 
4098             overlay = EMPTY; 
4099         } 
4100         int len = str.length(); 
4101         if (start < 0) { 
4102             start = 0; 
4103         } 
4104         if (start > len) { 
4105             start = len; 
4106         } 
4107         if (end < 0) { 
4108             end = 0; 
4109         } 
4110         if (end > len) { 
4111             end = len; 
4112         } 
4113         if (start > end) { 
4114             int temp = start; 
4115             start = end; 
4116             end = temp; 
4117         } 
4118         return new StringBuilder(len + start - end + overlay.length() + 1) 
4119             .append(str.substring(0, start)) 
4120             .append(overlay) 
4121             .append(str.substring(end)) 
4122             .toString(); 
4123     } 
4124  
4125     // Chomping 
4126     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4127     /** 
4128      * <p>Removes one newline from end of a String if it's there, 
4129      * otherwise leave it alone.  A newline is &quot;<code>\n</code>&quot;, 
4130      * &quot;<code>\r</code>&quot;, or &quot;<code>\r\n</code>&quot;.</p> 
4131      * 
4132      * <p>NOTE: This method changed in 2.0. 
4133      * It now more closely matches Perl chomp.</p> 
4134      * 
4135      * <pre> 
4136      * StringUtils.chomp(null)          = null 
4137      * StringUtils.chomp("")            = "" 
4138      * StringUtils.chomp("abc \r")      = "abc " 
4139      * StringUtils.chomp("abc\n")       = "abc" 
4140      * StringUtils.chomp("abc\r\n")     = "abc" 
4141      * StringUtils.chomp("abc\r\n\r\n") = "abc\r\n" 
4142      * StringUtils.chomp("abc\n\r")     = "abc\n" 
4143      * StringUtils.chomp("abc\n\rabc")  = "abc\n\rabc" 
4144      * StringUtils.chomp("\r")          = "" 
4145      * StringUtils.chomp("\n")          = "" 
4146      * StringUtils.chomp("\r\n")        = "" 
4147      * </pre> 
4148      * 
4149      * @param str  the String to chomp a newline from, may be null 
4150      * @return String without newline, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4151      */ 
4152     public static String chomp(String str) { 
4153         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
4154             return str; 
4155         } 
4156  
4157         if (str.length() == 1) { 
4158             char ch = str.charAt(0); 
4159             if (ch == CharUtils.CR || ch == CharUtils.LF) { 
4160                 return EMPTY; 
4161             } 
4162             return str; 
4163         } 
4164  
4165         int lastIdx = str.length() - 1; 
4166         char last = str.charAt(lastIdx); 
4167  
4168         if (last == CharUtils.LF) { 
4169             if (str.charAt(lastIdx - 1) == CharUtils.CR) { 
4170                 lastIdx--; 
4171             } 
4172         } else if (last != CharUtils.CR) { 
4173             lastIdx++; 
4174         } 
4175         return str.substring(0, lastIdx); 
4176     } 
4177  
4178     /** 
4179      * <p>Removes <code>separator</code> from the end of 
4180      * <code>str</code> if it's there, otherwise leave it alone.</p> 
4181      * 
4182      * <p>NOTE: This method changed in version 2.0. 
4183      * It now more closely matches Perl chomp. 
4184      * For the previous behavior, use {@link #substringBeforeLast(String, String)}. 
4185      * This method uses {@link String#endsWith(String)}.</p> 
4186      * 
4187      * <pre> 
4188      * StringUtils.chomp(null, *)         = null 
4189      * StringUtils.chomp("", *)           = "" 
4190      * StringUtils.chomp("foobar", "bar") = "foo" 
4191      * StringUtils.chomp("foobar", "baz") = "foobar" 
4192      * StringUtils.chomp("foo", "foo")    = "" 
4193      * StringUtils.chomp("foo ", "foo")   = "foo " 
4194      * StringUtils.chomp(" foo", "foo")   = " " 
4195      * StringUtils.chomp("foo", "foooo")  = "foo" 
4196      * StringUtils.chomp("foo", "")       = "foo" 
4197      * StringUtils.chomp("foo", null)     = "foo" 
4198      * </pre> 
4199      * 
4200      * @param str  the String to chomp from, may be null 
4201      * @param separator  separator String, may be null 
4202      * @return String without trailing separator, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4203      */ 
4204     public static String chomp(String str, String separator) { 
4205         if (isEmpty(str) || separator == null) { 
4206             return str; 
4207         } 
4208         if (str.endsWith(separator)) { 
4209             return str.substring(0, str.length() - separator.length()); 
4210         } 
4211         return str; 
4212     } 
4213  
4214     // Chopping 
4215     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4216     /** 
4217      * <p>Remove the last character from a String.</p> 
4218      * 
4219      * <p>If the String ends in <code>\r\n</code>, then remove both 
4220      * of them.</p> 
4221      * 
4222      * <pre> 
4223      * StringUtils.chop(null)          = null 
4224      * StringUtils.chop("")            = "" 
4225      * StringUtils.chop("abc \r")      = "abc " 
4226      * StringUtils.chop("abc\n")       = "abc" 
4227      * StringUtils.chop("abc\r\n")     = "abc" 
4228      * StringUtils.chop("abc")         = "ab" 
4229      * StringUtils.chop("abc\nabc")    = "abc\nab" 
4230      * StringUtils.chop("a")           = "" 
4231      * StringUtils.chop("\r")          = "" 
4232      * StringUtils.chop("\n")          = "" 
4233      * StringUtils.chop("\r\n")        = "" 
4234      * </pre> 
4235      * 
4236      * @param str  the String to chop last character from, may be null 
4237      * @return String without last character, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4238      */ 
4239     public static String chop(String str) { 
4240         if (str == null) { 
4241             return null; 
4242         } 
4243         int strLen = str.length(); 
4244         if (strLen < 2) { 
4245             return EMPTY; 
4246         } 
4247         int lastIdx = strLen - 1; 
4248         String ret = str.substring(0, lastIdx); 
4249         char last = str.charAt(lastIdx); 
4250         if (last == CharUtils.LF) { 
4251             if (ret.charAt(lastIdx - 1) == CharUtils.CR) { 
4252                 return ret.substring(0, lastIdx - 1); 
4253             } 
4254         } 
4255         return ret; 
4256     } 
4257  
4258     // Conversion 
4259     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4260  
4261     // Padding 
4262     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4263     /** 
4264      * <p>Repeat a String <code>repeat</code> times to form a 
4265      * new String.</p> 
4266      * 
4267      * <pre> 
4268      * StringUtils.repeat(null, 2) = null 
4269      * StringUtils.repeat("", 0)   = "" 
4270      * StringUtils.repeat("", 2)   = "" 
4271      * StringUtils.repeat("a", 3)  = "aaa" 
4272      * StringUtils.repeat("ab", 2) = "abab" 
4273      * StringUtils.repeat("a", -2) = "" 
4274      * </pre> 
4275      * 
4276      * @param str  the String to repeat, may be null 
4277      * @param repeat  number of times to repeat str, negative treated as zero 
4278      * @return a new String consisting of the original String repeated, 
4279      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4280      * @since 2.5 
4281      */ 
4282     public static String repeat(String str, int repeat) { 
4283         // Performance tuned for 2.0 (JDK1.4) 
4284  
4285         if (str == null) { 
4286             return null; 
4287         } 
4288         if (repeat <= 0) { 
4289             return EMPTY; 
4290         } 
4291         int inputLength = str.length(); 
4292         if (repeat == 1 || inputLength == 0) { 
4293             return str; 
4294         } 
4295         if (inputLength == 1 && repeat <= PAD_LIMIT) { 
4296             return padding(repeat, str.charAt(0)); 
4297         } 
4298  
4299         int outputLength = inputLength * repeat; 
4300         switch (inputLength) { 
4301             case 1 : 
4302                 char ch = str.charAt(0); 
4303                 char[] output1 = new char[outputLength]; 
4304                 for (int i = repeat - 1; i >= 0; i--) { 
4305                     output1[i] = ch; 
4306                 } 
4307                 return new String(output1); 
4308             case 2 : 
4309                 char ch0 = str.charAt(0); 
4310                 char ch1 = str.charAt(1); 
4311                 char[] output2 = new char[outputLength]; 
4312                 for (int i = repeat * 2 - 2; i >= 0; i--, i--) { 
4313                     output2[i] = ch0; 
4314                     output2[i + 1] = ch1; 
4315                 } 
4316                 return new String(output2); 
4317             default : 
4318                 StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(outputLength); 
4319                 for (int i = 0; i < repeat; i++) { 
4320                     buf.append(str); 
4321                 } 
4322                 return buf.toString(); 
4323         } 
4324     } 
4325  
4326     /** 
4327      * <p>Repeat a String <code>repeat</code> times to form a 
4328      * new String, with a String separator injected each time. </p> 
4329      * 
4330      * <pre> 
4331      * StringUtils.repeat(null, null, 2) = null 
4332      * StringUtils.repeat(null, "x", 2)  = null 
4333      * StringUtils.repeat("", null, 0)   = "" 
4334      * StringUtils.repeat("", "", 2)     = "" 
4335      * StringUtils.repeat("", "x", 3)    = "xxx" 
4336      * StringUtils.repeat("?", ", ", 3)  = "?, ?, ?" 
4337      * </pre> 
4338      * 
4339      * @param str        the String to repeat, may be null 
4340      * @param separator  the String to inject, may be null 
4341      * @param repeat     number of times to repeat str, negative treated as zero 
4342      * @return a new String consisting of the original String repeated, 
4343      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4344      */ 
4345     public static String repeat(String str, String separator, int repeat) { 
4346         if(str == null || separator == null) { 
4347             return repeat(str, repeat); 
4348         } else { 
4349             // given that repeat(String, int) is quite optimized, better to rely on it than try and splice this into it 
4350             String result = repeat(str + separator, repeat); 
4351             return removeEnd(result, separator); 
4352         } 
4353     } 
4354  
4355     /** 
4356      * <p>Returns padding using the specified delimiter repeated 
4357      * to a given length.</p> 
4358      * 
4359      * <pre> 
4360      * StringUtils.padding(0, 'e')  = "" 
4361      * StringUtils.padding(3, 'e')  = "eee" 
4362      * StringUtils.padding(-2, 'e') = IndexOutOfBoundsException 
4363      * </pre> 
4364      * 
4365      * <p>Note: this method doesn't not support padding with 
4366      * <a href="http://www.unicode.org/glossary/#supplementary_character">Unicode Supplementary Characters</a> 
4367      * as they require a pair of <code>char</code>s to be represented. 
4368      * If you are needing to support full I18N of your applications 
4369      * consider using {@link #repeat(String, int)} instead.  
4370      * </p> 
4371      * 
4372      * @param repeat  number of times to repeat delim 
4373      * @param padChar  character to repeat 
4374      * @return String with repeated character 
4375      * @throws IndexOutOfBoundsException if <code>repeat &lt; 0</code> 
4376      * @see #repeat(String, int) 
4377      */ 
4378     private static String padding(int repeat, char padChar) throws IndexOutOfBoundsException { 
4379         if (repeat < 0) { 
4380             throw new IndexOutOfBoundsException("Cannot pad a negative amount: " + repeat); 
4381         } 
4382         final char[] buf = new char[repeat]; 
4383         for (int i = 0; i < buf.length; i++) { 
4384             buf[i] = padChar; 
4385         } 
4386         return new String(buf); 
4387     } 
4388  
4389     /** 
4390      * <p>Right pad a String with spaces (' ').</p> 
4391      * 
4392      * <p>The String is padded to the size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4393      * 
4394      * <pre> 
4395      * StringUtils.rightPad(null, *)   = null 
4396      * StringUtils.rightPad("", 3)     = "   " 
4397      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 3)  = "bat" 
4398      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 5)  = "bat  " 
4399      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 1)  = "bat" 
4400      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", -1) = "bat" 
4401      * </pre> 
4402      * 
4403      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4404      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4405      * @return right padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4406      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4407      */ 
4408     public static String rightPad(String str, int size) { 
4409         return rightPad(str, size, ' '); 
4410     } 
4411  
4412     /** 
4413      * <p>Right pad a String with a specified character.</p> 
4414      * 
4415      * <p>The String is padded to the size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4416      * 
4417      * <pre> 
4418      * StringUtils.rightPad(null, *, *)     = null 
4419      * StringUtils.rightPad("", 3, 'z')     = "zzz" 
4420      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 3, 'z')  = "bat" 
4421      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 5, 'z')  = "batzz" 
4422      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 1, 'z')  = "bat" 
4423      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", -1, 'z') = "bat" 
4424      * </pre> 
4425      * 
4426      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4427      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4428      * @param padChar  the character to pad with 
4429      * @return right padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4430      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4431      * @since 2.0 
4432      */ 
4433     public static String rightPad(String str, int size, char padChar) { 
4434         if (str == null) { 
4435             return null; 
4436         } 
4437         int pads = size - str.length(); 
4438         if (pads <= 0) { 
4439             return str; // returns original String when possible 
4440         } 
4441         if (pads > PAD_LIMIT) { 
4442             return rightPad(str, size, String.valueOf(padChar)); 
4443         } 
4444         return str.concat(padding(pads, padChar)); 
4445     } 
4446  
4447     /** 
4448      * <p>Right pad a String with a specified String.</p> 
4449      * 
4450      * <p>The String is padded to the size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4451      * 
4452      * <pre> 
4453      * StringUtils.rightPad(null, *, *)      = null 
4454      * StringUtils.rightPad("", 3, "z")      = "zzz" 
4455      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 3, "yz")  = "bat" 
4456      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 5, "yz")  = "batyz" 
4457      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 8, "yz")  = "batyzyzy" 
4458      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 1, "yz")  = "bat" 
4459      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", -1, "yz") = "bat" 
4460      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 5, null)  = "bat  " 
4461      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 5, "")    = "bat  " 
4462      * </pre> 
4463      * 
4464      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4465      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4466      * @param padStr  the String to pad with, null or empty treated as single space 
4467      * @return right padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4468      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4469      */ 
4470     public static String rightPad(String str, int size, String padStr) { 
4471         if (str == null) { 
4472             return null; 
4473         } 
4474         if (isEmpty(padStr)) { 
4475             padStr = " "; 
4476         } 
4477         int padLen = padStr.length(); 
4478         int strLen = str.length(); 
4479         int pads = size - strLen; 
4480         if (pads <= 0) { 
4481             return str; // returns original String when possible 
4482         } 
4483         if (padLen == 1 && pads <= PAD_LIMIT) { 
4484             return rightPad(str, size, padStr.charAt(0)); 
4485         } 
4486  
4487         if (pads == padLen) { 
4488             return str.concat(padStr); 
4489         } else if (pads < padLen) { 
4490             return str.concat(padStr.substring(0, pads)); 
4491         } else { 
4492             char[] padding = new char[pads]; 
4493             char[] padChars = padStr.toCharArray(); 
4494             for (int i = 0; i < pads; i++) { 
4495                 padding[i] = padChars[i % padLen]; 
4496             } 
4497             return str.concat(new String(padding)); 
4498         } 
4499     } 
4500  
4501     /** 
4502      * <p>Left pad a String with spaces (' ').</p> 
4503      * 
4504      * <p>The String is padded to the size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4505      * 
4506      * <pre> 
4507      * StringUtils.leftPad(null, *)   = null 
4508      * StringUtils.leftPad("", 3)     = "   " 
4509      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 3)  = "bat" 
4510      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 5)  = "  bat" 
4511      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 1)  = "bat" 
4512      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", -1) = "bat" 
4513      * </pre> 
4514      * 
4515      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4516      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4517      * @return left padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4518      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4519      */ 
4520     public static String leftPad(String str, int size) { 
4521         return leftPad(str, size, ' '); 
4522     } 
4523  
4524     /** 
4525      * <p>Left pad a String with a specified character.</p> 
4526      * 
4527      * <p>Pad to a size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4528      * 
4529      * <pre> 
4530      * StringUtils.leftPad(null, *, *)     = null 
4531      * StringUtils.leftPad("", 3, 'z')     = "zzz" 
4532      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 3, 'z')  = "bat" 
4533      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 5, 'z')  = "zzbat" 
4534      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 1, 'z')  = "bat" 
4535      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", -1, 'z') = "bat" 
4536      * </pre> 
4537      * 
4538      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4539      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4540      * @param padChar  the character to pad with 
4541      * @return left padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4542      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4543      * @since 2.0 
4544      */ 
4545     public static String leftPad(String str, int size, char padChar) { 
4546         if (str == null) { 
4547             return null; 
4548         } 
4549         int pads = size - str.length(); 
4550         if (pads <= 0) { 
4551             return str; // returns original String when possible 
4552         } 
4553         if (pads > PAD_LIMIT) { 
4554             return leftPad(str, size, String.valueOf(padChar)); 
4555         } 
4556         return padding(pads, padChar).concat(str); 
4557     } 
4558  
4559     /** 
4560      * <p>Left pad a String with a specified String.</p> 
4561      * 
4562      * <p>Pad to a size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4563      * 
4564      * <pre> 
4565      * StringUtils.leftPad(null, *, *)      = null 
4566      * StringUtils.leftPad("", 3, "z")      = "zzz" 
4567      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 3, "yz")  = "bat" 
4568      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 5, "yz")  = "yzbat" 
4569      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 8, "yz")  = "yzyzybat" 
4570      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 1, "yz")  = "bat" 
4571      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", -1, "yz") = "bat" 
4572      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 5, null)  = "  bat" 
4573      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 5, "")    = "  bat" 
4574      * </pre> 
4575      * 
4576      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4577      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4578      * @param padStr  the String to pad with, null or empty treated as single space 
4579      * @return left padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4580      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4581      */ 
4582     public static String leftPad(String str, int size, String padStr) { 
4583         if (str == null) { 
4584             return null; 
4585         } 
4586         if (isEmpty(padStr)) { 
4587             padStr = " "; 
4588         } 
4589         int padLen = padStr.length(); 
4590         int strLen = str.length(); 
4591         int pads = size - strLen; 
4592         if (pads <= 0) { 
4593             return str; // returns original String when possible 
4594         } 
4595         if (padLen == 1 && pads <= PAD_LIMIT) { 
4596             return leftPad(str, size, padStr.charAt(0)); 
4597         } 
4598  
4599         if (pads == padLen) { 
4600             return padStr.concat(str); 
4601         } else if (pads < padLen) { 
4602             return padStr.substring(0, pads).concat(str); 
4603         } else { 
4604             char[] padding = new char[pads]; 
4605             char[] padChars = padStr.toCharArray(); 
4606             for (int i = 0; i < pads; i++) { 
4607                 padding[i] = padChars[i % padLen]; 
4608             } 
4609             return new String(padding).concat(str); 
4610         } 
4611     }

4628     // Centering 
4629     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4630     /** 
4631      * <p>Centers a String in a larger String of size <code>size</code> 
4632      * using the space character (' ').<p> 
4633      * 
4634      * <p>If the size is less than the String length, the String is returned. 
4635      * A <code>null</code> String returns <code>null</code>. 
4636      * A negative size is treated as zero.</p> 
4637      * 
4638      * <p>Equivalent to <code>center(str, size, " ")</code>.</p> 
4639      * 
4640      * <pre> 
4641      * StringUtils.center(null, *)   = null 
4642      * StringUtils.center("", 4)     = "    " 
4643      * StringUtils.center("ab", -1)  = "ab" 
4644      * StringUtils.center("ab", 4)   = " ab " 
4645      * StringUtils.center("abcd", 2) = "abcd" 
4646      * StringUtils.center("a", 4)    = " a  " 
4647      * </pre> 
4648      * 
4649      * @param str  the String to center, may be null 
4650      * @param size  the int size of new String, negative treated as zero 
4651      * @return centered String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4652      */ 
4653     public static String center(String str, int size) { 
4654         return center(str, size, ' '); 
4655     } 
4656  
4657     /** 
4658      * <p>Centers a String in a larger String of size <code>size</code>. 
4659      * Uses a supplied character as the value to pad the String with.</p> 
4660      * 
4661      * <p>If the size is less than the String length, the String is returned. 
4662      * A <code>null</code> String returns <code>null</code>. 
4663      * A negative size is treated as zero.</p> 
4664      * 
4665      * <pre> 
4666      * StringUtils.center(null, *, *)     = null 
4667      * StringUtils.center("", 4, ' ')     = "    " 
4668      * StringUtils.center("ab", -1, ' ')  = "ab" 
4669      * StringUtils.center("ab", 4, ' ')   = " ab" 
4670      * StringUtils.center("abcd", 2, ' ') = "abcd" 
4671      * StringUtils.center("a", 4, ' ')    = " a  " 
4672      * StringUtils.center("a", 4, 'y')    = "yayy" 
4673      * </pre> 
4674      * 
4675      * @param str  the String to center, may be null 
4676      * @param size  the int size of new String, negative treated as zero 
4677      * @param padChar  the character to pad the new String with 
4678      * @return centered String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4679      * @since 2.0 
4680      */ 
4681     public static String center(String str, int size, char padChar) { 
4682         if (str == null || size <= 0) { 
4683             return str; 
4684         } 
4685         int strLen = str.length(); 
4686         int pads = size - strLen; 
4687         if (pads <= 0) { 
4688             return str; 
4689         } 
4690         str = leftPad(str, strLen + pads / 2, padChar); 
4691         str = rightPad(str, size, padChar); 
4692         return str; 
4693     } 
4694  
4695     /** 
4696      * <p>Centers a String in a larger String of size <code>size</code>. 
4697      * Uses a supplied String as the value to pad the String with.</p> 
4698      * 
4699      * <p>If the size is less than the String length, the String is returned. 
4700      * A <code>null</code> String returns <code>null</code>. 
4701      * A negative size is treated as zero.</p> 
4702      * 
4703      * <pre> 
4704      * StringUtils.center(null, *, *)     = null 
4705      * StringUtils.center("", 4, " ")     = "    " 
4706      * StringUtils.center("ab", -1, " ")  = "ab" 
4707      * StringUtils.center("ab", 4, " ")   = " ab" 
4708      * StringUtils.center("abcd", 2, " ") = "abcd" 
4709      * StringUtils.center("a", 4, " ")    = " a  " 
4710      * StringUtils.center("a", 4, "yz")   = "yayz" 
4711      * StringUtils.center("abc", 7, null) = "  abc  " 
4712      * StringUtils.center("abc", 7, "")   = "  abc  " 
4713      * </pre> 
4714      * 
4715      * @param str  the String to center, may be null 
4716      * @param size  the int size of new String, negative treated as zero 
4717      * @param padStr  the String to pad the new String with, must not be null or empty 
4718      * @return centered String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4719      * @throws IllegalArgumentException if padStr is <code>null</code> or empty 
4720      */ 
4721     public static String center(String str, int size, String padStr) { 
4722         if (str == null || size <= 0) { 
4723             return str; 
4724         } 
4725         if (isEmpty(padStr)) { 
4726             padStr = " "; 
4727         } 
4728         int strLen = str.length(); 
4729         int pads = size - strLen; 
4730         if (pads <= 0) { 
4731             return str; 
4732         } 
4733         str = leftPad(str, strLen + pads / 2, padStr); 
4734         str = rightPad(str, size, padStr); 
4735         return str; 
4736     }

4740     /** 
4741      * <p>Converts a String to upper case as per {@link String#toUpperCase()}.</p> 
4742      * 
4743      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
4744      * 
4745      * <pre> 
4746      * StringUtils.upperCase(null)  = null 
4747      * StringUtils.upperCase("")    = "" 
4748      * StringUtils.upperCase("aBc") = "ABC" 
4749      * </pre> 
4750      * 
4751      * <p><strong>Note:</strong> As described in the documentation for {@link String#toUpperCase()}, 
4752      * the result of this method is affected by the current locale. 
4753      * For platform-independent case transformations, the method {@link #lowerCase(String, Locale)} 
4754      * should be used with a specific locale (e.g. {@link Locale#ENGLISH}).</p> 
4755      * 
4756      * @param str  the String to upper case, may be null 
4757      * @return the upper cased String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4758      */ 
4759     public static String upperCase(String str) { 
4760         if (str == null) { 
4761             return null; 
4762         } 
4763         return str.toUpperCase(); 
4764     }

4768      * 
4769      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
4770      * 
4771      * <pre> 
4772      * StringUtils.upperCase(null, Locale.ENGLISH)  = null 
4773      * StringUtils.upperCase("", Locale.ENGLISH)    = "" 
4774      * StringUtils.upperCase("aBc", Locale.ENGLISH) = "ABC" 
4775      * </pre> 
4776      * 
4777      * @param str  the String to upper case, may be null 
4778      * @param locale  the locale that defines the case transformation rules, must not be null 
4779      * @return the upper cased String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4780      * @since 2.5 
4781      */ 
4782     public static String upperCase(String str, Locale locale) { 
4783         if (str == null) { 
4784             return null; 
4785         } 
4786         return str.toUpperCase(locale); 
4787     }

4797      * StringUtils.lowerCase("aBc") = "abc" 
4798      * </pre> 
4799      * 
4800      * <p><strong>Note:</strong> As described in the documentation for {@link String#toLowerCase()}, 
4801      * the result of this method is affected by the current locale.

4803      * should be used with a specific locale (e.g. {@link Locale#ENGLISH}).</p>
