4838     /** 
4839      * <p>Capitalizes a String changing the first letter to title case as 
4840      * per {@link Character#toTitleCase(char)}. No other letters are changed.</p> 
4841      * 
4842      * <p>For a word based algorithm, see {@link WordUtils#capitalize(String)}. 
4843      * A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
4844      * 
4845      * <pre> 
4846      * StringUtils.capitalize(null)  = null 
4847      * StringUtils.capitalize("")    = "" 
4848      * StringUtils.capitalize("cat") = "Cat" 
4849      * StringUtils.capitalize("cAt") = "CAt" 
4850      * </pre> 
4851      * 
4852      * @param cs the String to capitalize, may be null 
4853      * @return the capitalized String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4854      * @see WordUtils#capitalize(String) 
4855      * @see #uncapitalize(String) 
4856      * @since 2.0 
4857      */ 
4858     public static String capitalize(CharSequence cs) { 
4859         if (cs == null ) { 
4860             return null; 
4861         } 
4862         int strLen; 
4863         if ((strLen = cs.length()) == 0) { 
4864             return cs.toString(); 
4865         } 
4866         return new StringBuilder(strLen) 
4867             .append(Character.toTitleCase(cs.charAt(0))) 
4868             .append(CharSequenceUtils.subSequence(cs, 1)) 
4869             .toString(); 
4870     } 
4871  
4872     /** 
4873      * <p>Uncapitalizes a CharSequence changing the first letter to title case as 
4874      * per {@link Character#toLowerCase(char)}. No other letters are changed.</p> 
4875      * 
4876      * <p>For a word based algorithm, see {@link WordUtils#uncapitalize(String)}. 
4877      * A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
4878      * 
4879      * <pre> 
4880      * StringUtils.uncapitalize(null)  = null 
4881      * StringUtils.uncapitalize("")    = "" 
4882      * StringUtils.uncapitalize("Cat") = "cat" 
4883      * StringUtils.uncapitalize("CAT") = "cAT" 
4884      * </pre> 
4885      * 
4886      * @param cs the String to uncapitalize, may be null 
4887      * @return the uncapitalized String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4888      * @see WordUtils#uncapitalize(String) 
4889      * @see #capitalize(CharSequence) 
4890      * @since 2.0 
4891      */ 
4892     public static String uncapitalize(CharSequence cs) { 
4893         if (cs == null ) { 
4894             return null; 
4895         } 
4896         int strLen; 
4897         if ((strLen = cs.length()) == 0) { 
4898             return cs.toString(); 
4899         } 
4900         return new StringBuilder(strLen) 
4901             .append(Character.toLowerCase(cs.charAt(0))) 
4902             .append(CharSequenceUtils.subSequence(cs, 1)) 
4903             .toString(); 
4904     } 
4905  
4906     /** 
4907      * <p>Swaps the case of a String changing upper and title case to 
4908      * lower case, and lower case to upper case.</p> 
4909      * 
4910      * <ul> 
4911      *  <li>Upper case character converts to Lower case</li> 
4912      *  <li>Title case character converts to Lower case</li> 
4913      *  <li>Lower case character converts to Upper case</li> 
4914      * </ul> 
4915      * 
4916      * <p>For a word based algorithm, see {@link WordUtils#swapCase(String)}. 
4917      * A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
4918      * 
4919      * <pre> 
4920      * StringUtils.swapCase(null)                 = null 
4921      * StringUtils.swapCase("")                   = "" 
4922      * StringUtils.swapCase("The dog has a BONE") = "tHE DOG HAS A bone" 
4923      * </pre> 
4924      * 
4925      * <p>NOTE: This method changed in Lang version 2.0. 
4926      * It no longer performs a word based algorithm. 
4927      * If you only use ASCII, you will notice no change. 
4928      * That functionality is available in WordUtils.</p> 
4929      * 
4930      * @param str  the String to swap case, may be null 
4931      * @return the changed String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4932      */ 
4933     public static String swapCase(String str) { 
4934         int strLen; 
4935         if (str == null || (strLen = str.length()) == 0) { 
4936             return str; 
4937         } 
4938         StringBuilder buffer = new StringBuilder(strLen); 
4939  
4940         char ch = 0; 
4941         for (int i = 0; i < strLen; i++) { 
4942             ch = str.charAt(i); 
4943             if (Character.isUpperCase(ch)) { 
4944                 ch = Character.toLowerCase(ch); 
4945             } else if (Character.isTitleCase(ch)) { 
4946                 ch = Character.toLowerCase(ch); 
4947             } else if (Character.isLowerCase(ch)) { 
4948                 ch = Character.toUpperCase(ch); 
4949             } 
4950             buffer.append(ch); 
4951         } 
4952         return buffer.toString(); 
4953     } 
4954  
4955     // Count matches 
4956     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4957     /** 
4958      * <p>Counts how many times the substring appears in the larger String.</p> 
4959      * 
4960      * <p>A <code>null</code> or empty ("") String input returns <code>0</code>.</p> 
4961      * 
4962      * <pre> 
4963      * StringUtils.countMatches(null, *)       = 0 
4964      * StringUtils.countMatches("", *)         = 0 
4965      * StringUtils.countMatches("abba", null)  = 0 
4966      * StringUtils.countMatches("abba", "")    = 0 
4967      * StringUtils.countMatches("abba", "a")   = 2 
4968      * StringUtils.countMatches("abba", "ab")  = 1 
4969      * StringUtils.countMatches("abba", "xxx") = 0 
4970      * </pre> 
4971      * 
4972      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
4973      * @param sub  the substring to count, may be null 
4974      * @return the number of occurrences, 0 if either String is <code>null</code> 
4975      */ 
4976     public static int countMatches(String str, String sub) { 
4977         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(sub)) { 
4978             return 0; 
4979         } 
4980         int count = 0; 
4981         int idx = 0; 
4982         while ((idx = str.indexOf(sub, idx)) != INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
4983             count++; 
4984             idx += sub.length(); 
4985         } 
4986         return count; 
4987     }

4991     /** 
4992      * <p>Checks if the CharSequence contains only unicode letters.</p> 
4993      * 
4994      * <p><code>null</code> will return <code>false</code>. 
4995      * An empty CharSequence (length()=0) will return <code>true</code>.</p> 
4996      * 
4997      * <pre> 
4998      * StringUtils.isAlpha(null)   = false 
4999      * StringUtils.isAlpha("")     = true 
5000      * StringUtils.isAlpha("  ")   = false 
5001      * StringUtils.isAlpha("abc")  = true 
5002      * StringUtils.isAlpha("ab2c") = false 
5003      * StringUtils.isAlpha("ab-c") = false 
5004      * </pre> 
5005      * 
5006      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
5007      * @return <code>true</code> if only contains letters, and is non-null 
5008      */ 
5009     public static boolean isAlpha(CharSequence cs) { 
5010         if (cs == null) { 
5011             return false; 
5012         } 
5013         int sz = cs.length(); 
5014         for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) { 
5015             if (Character.isLetter(cs.charAt(i)) == false) { 
5016                 return false; 
5017             } 
5018         } 
5019         return true; 
5020     }

5325     // Defaults

5327     /** 
5328      * <p>Returns either the passed in String, 
5329      * or if the String is <code>null</code>, an empty String ("").</p> 
5330      * 
5331      * <pre> 
5332      * StringUtils.defaultString(null)  = "" 
5333      * StringUtils.defaultString("")    = "" 
5334      * StringUtils.defaultString("bat") = "bat" 
5335      * </pre> 
5336      * 
5337      * @see ObjectUtils#toString(Object) 
5338      * @see String#valueOf(Object) 
5339      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5340      * @return the passed in String, or the empty String if it 
5341      *  was <code>null</code> 
5342      */ 
5343     public static String defaultString(String str) { 
5344         return str == null ? EMPTY : str; 
5345     } 
5346  
5347     /** 
5348      * <p>Returns either the passed in String, or if the String is 
5349      * <code>null</code>, the value of <code>defaultStr</code>.</p> 
5350      * 
5351      * <pre> 
5352      * StringUtils.defaultString(null, "NULL")  = "NULL" 
5353      * StringUtils.defaultString("", "NULL")    = "" 
5354      * StringUtils.defaultString("bat", "NULL") = "bat" 
5355      * </pre> 
5356      * 
5357      * @see ObjectUtils#toString(Object,String) 
5358      * @see String#valueOf(Object) 
5359      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5360      * @param defaultStr  the default String to return 
5361      *  if the input is <code>null</code>, may be null 
5362      * @return the passed in String, or the default if it was <code>null</code> 
5363      */ 
5364     public static String defaultString(String str, String defaultStr) { 
5365         return str == null ? defaultStr : str; 
5366     } 
5367  
5368     /** 
5369      * <p>Returns either the passed in CharSequence, or if the CharSequence is 
5370      * empty or <code>null</code>, the value of <code>defaultStr</code>.</p> 
5371      * 
5372      * <pre> 
5373      * StringUtils.defaultIfEmpty(null, "NULL")  = "NULL" 
5374      * StringUtils.defaultIfEmpty("", "NULL")    = "NULL" 
5375      * StringUtils.defaultIfEmpty("bat", "NULL") = "bat" 
5376      * StringUtils.defaultIfEmpty("", null)      = null 
5377      * </pre> 
5378      * 
5379      * @see StringUtils#defaultString(String, String) 
5380      * @param str  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
5381      * @param defaultStr  the default CharSequence to return 
5382      *  if the input is empty ("") or <code>null</code>, may be null 
5383      * @return the passed in CharSequence, or the default 
5384      */ 
5385     public static CharSequence defaultIfEmpty(CharSequence str, CharSequence defaultStr) { 
5386         return StringUtils.isEmpty(str) ? defaultStr : str; 
5387     }

5389     // Reversing 
5390     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5391     /** 
5392      * <p>Reverses a String as per {@link StringBuilder#reverse()}.</p> 
5393      * 
5394      * <p>A <code>null</code> String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
5395      * 
5396      * <pre> 
5397      * StringUtils.reverse(null)  = null 
5398      * StringUtils.reverse("")    = "" 
5399      * StringUtils.reverse("bat") = "tab" 
5400      * </pre> 
5401      * 
5402      * @param str  the String to reverse, may be null 
5403      * @return the reversed String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
5404      */ 
5405     public static String reverse(String str) { 
5406         if (str == null) { 
5407             return null; 
5408         } 
5409         return CharSequenceUtils.reverse(str).toString(); 
5410     } 
5411  
5412     /** 
5413      * <p>Reverses a String that is delimited by a specific character.</p> 
5414      * 
5415      * <p>The Strings between the delimiters are not reversed. 
5416      * Thus java.lang.String becomes String.lang.java (if the delimiter 
5417      * is <code>'.'</code>).</p> 
5418      * 
5419      * <pre> 
5420      * StringUtils.reverseDelimited(null, *)      = null 
5421      * StringUtils.reverseDelimited("", *)        = "" 
5422      * StringUtils.reverseDelimited("a.b.c", 'x') = "a.b.c" 
5423      * StringUtils.reverseDelimited("a.b.c", ".") = "c.b.a" 
5424      * </pre> 
5425      * 
5426      * @param str  the String to reverse, may be null 
5427      * @param separatorChar  the separator character to use 
5428      * @return the reversed String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
5429      * @since 2.0 
5430      */ 
5431     public static String reverseDelimited(String str, char separatorChar) { 
5432         if (str == null) { 
5433             return null; 
5434         } 
5435         // could implement manually, but simple way is to reuse other, 
5436         // probably slower, methods. 
5437         String[] strs = split(str, separatorChar); 
5438         ArrayUtils.reverse(strs); 
5439         return join(strs, separatorChar); 
5440     } 
5441  
5442     // Abbreviating 
5443     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5444     /** 
5445      * <p>Abbreviates a String using ellipses. This will turn 
5446      * "Now is the time for all good men" into "Now is the time for..."</p> 
5447      * 
5448      * <p>Specifically: 
5449      * <ul> 
5450      *   <li>If <code>str</code> is less than <code>maxWidth</code> characters 
5451      *       long, return it.</li> 
5452      *   <li>Else abbreviate it to <code>(substring(str, 0, max-3) + "...")</code>.</li> 
5453      *   <li>If <code>maxWidth</code> is less than <code>4</code>, throw an 
5454      *       <code>IllegalArgumentException</code>.</li> 
5455      *   <li>In no case will it return a String of length greater than 
5456      *       <code>maxWidth</code>.</li> 
5457      * </ul> 
5458      * </p> 
5459      * 
5460      * <pre> 
5461      * StringUtils.abbreviate(null, *)      = null 
5462      * StringUtils.abbreviate("", 4)        = "" 
5463      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefg", 6) = "abc..." 
5464      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefg", 7) = "abcdefg" 
5465      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefg", 8) = "abcdefg" 
5466      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefg", 4) = "a..." 
5467      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefg", 3) = IllegalArgumentException 
5468      * </pre> 
5469      * 
5470      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5471      * @param maxWidth  maximum length of result String, must be at least 4 
5472      * @return abbreviated String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
5473      * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the width is too small 
5474      * @since 2.0 
5475      */ 
5476     public static String abbreviate(String str, int maxWidth) { 
5477         return abbreviate(str, 0, maxWidth); 
5478     } 
5479  
5480     /** 
5481      * <p>Abbreviates a String using ellipses. This will turn 
5482      * "Now is the time for all good men" into "...is the time for..."</p> 
5483      * 
5484      * <p>Works like <code>abbreviate(String, int)</code>, but allows you to specify 
5485      * a "left edge" offset.  Note that this left edge is not necessarily going to 
5486      * be the leftmost character in the result, or the first character following the 
5487      * ellipses, but it will appear somewhere in the result. 
5488      * 
5489      * <p>In no case will it return a String of length greater than 
5490      * <code>maxWidth</code>.</p> 
5491      * 
5492      * <pre> 
5493      * StringUtils.abbreviate(null, *, *)                = null 
5494      * StringUtils.abbreviate("", 0, 4)                  = "" 
5495      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", -1, 10) = "abcdefg..." 
5496      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 0, 10)  = "abcdefg..." 
5497      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 1, 10)  = "abcdefg..." 
5498      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 4, 10)  = "abcdefg..." 
5499      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 5, 10)  = "...fghi..." 
5500      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 6, 10)  = "...ghij..." 
5501      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 8, 10)  = "...ijklmno" 
5502      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 10, 10) = "...ijklmno" 
5503      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 12, 10) = "...ijklmno" 
5504      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghij", 0, 3)        = IllegalArgumentException 
5505      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghij", 5, 6)        = IllegalArgumentException 
5506      * </pre> 
5507      * 
5508      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5509      * @param offset  left edge of source String 
5510      * @param maxWidth  maximum length of result String, must be at least 4 
5511      * @return abbreviated String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
5512      * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the width is too small 
5513      * @since 2.0 
5514      */ 
5515     public static String abbreviate(String str, int offset, int maxWidth) { 
5516         if (str == null) { 
5517             return null; 
5518         } 
5519         if (maxWidth < 4) { 
5520             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Minimum abbreviation width is 4"); 
5521         } 
5522         if (str.length() <= maxWidth) { 
5523             return str; 
5524         } 
5525         if (offset > str.length()) { 
5526             offset = str.length(); 
5527         } 
5528         if ((str.length() - offset) < (maxWidth - 3)) { 
5529             offset = str.length() - (maxWidth - 3); 
5530         } 
5531         final String abrevMarker = "..."; 
5532 		if (offset <= 4) { 
5533             return str.substring(0, maxWidth - 3) + abrevMarker; 
5534         } 
5535         if (maxWidth < 7) { 
5536             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Minimum abbreviation width with offset is 7"); 
5537         } 
5538         if ((offset + (maxWidth - 3)) < str.length()) { 
5539             return abrevMarker + abbreviate(str.substring(offset), maxWidth - 3); 
5540         } 
5541         return abrevMarker + str.substring(str.length() - (maxWidth - 3)); 
5542     } 
5543      
5544     /** 
5545      * <p>Abbreviates a String to the length passed, replacing the middle characters with the supplied 
5546      * replacement String.</p> 
5547      * 
5548      * <p>This abbreviation only occurs if the following criteria is met: 
5549      * <ul> 
5550      * <li>Neither the String for abbreviation nor the replacement String are null or empty </li> 
5551      * <li>The length to truncate to is less than the length of the supplied String</li> 
5552      * <li>The length to truncate to is greater than 0</li> 
5553      * <li>The abbreviated String will have enough room for the length supplied replacement String 
5554      * and the first and last characters of the supplied String for abbreviation</li> 
5555      * </ul> 
5556      * Otherwise, the returned String will be the same as the supplied String for abbreviation. 
5557      * </p> 
5558      * 
5559      * <pre> 
5560      * StringUtils.abbreviateMiddle(null, null, 0)      = null 
5561      * StringUtils.abbreviateMiddle("abc", null, 0)      = "abc" 
5562      * StringUtils.abbreviateMiddle("abc", ".", 0)      = "abc" 
5563      * StringUtils.abbreviateMiddle("abc", ".", 3)      = "abc" 
5564      * StringUtils.abbreviateMiddle("abcdef", ".", 4)     = "ab.f" 
5565      * </pre> 
5566      * 
5567      * @param str  the String to abbreviate, may be null 
5568      * @param middle the String to replace the middle characters with, may be null 
5569      * @param length the length to abbreviate <code>str</code> to. 
5570      * @return the abbreviated String if the above criteria is met, or the original String supplied for abbreviation. 
5571      * @since 2.5 
5572      */ 
5573     public static String abbreviateMiddle(String str, String middle, int length) { 
5574         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(middle)) { 
5575             return str; 
5576         } 
5577        
5578         if (length >= str.length() || length < (middle.length()+2)) { 
5579             return str; 
5580         } 
5581  
5582         int targetSting = length-middle.length(); 
5583         int startOffset = targetSting/2+targetSting%2; 
5584         int endOffset = str.length()-targetSting/2; 
5585          
5586         StringBuilder builder = new StringBuilder(length); 
5587         builder.append(str.substring(0,startOffset)); 
5588         builder.append(middle); 
5589         builder.append(str.substring(endOffset)); 
5590          
5591         return builder.toString(); 
5592     } 
5593  
5594     // Difference 
5595     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5596     /** 
5597      * <p>Compares two Strings, and returns the portion where they differ. 
5598      * (More precisely, return the remainder of the second String, 
5599      * starting from where it's different from the first.)</p> 
5600      * 
5601      * <p>For example, 
5602      * <code>difference("i am a machine", "i am a robot") -> "robot"</code>.</p> 
5603      * 
5604      * <pre> 
5605      * StringUtils.difference(null, null) = null 
5606      * StringUtils.difference("", "") = "" 
5607      * StringUtils.difference("", "abc") = "abc" 
5608      * StringUtils.difference("abc", "") = "" 
5609      * StringUtils.difference("abc", "abc") = "" 
5610      * StringUtils.difference("ab", "abxyz") = "xyz" 
5611      * StringUtils.difference("abcde", "abxyz") = "xyz" 
5612      * StringUtils.difference("abcde", "xyz") = "xyz" 
5613      * </pre> 
5614      * 
5615      * @param str1  the first String, may be null 
5616      * @param str2  the second String, may be null 
5617      * @return the portion of str2 where it differs from str1; returns the 
5618      * empty String if they are equal 
5619      * @since 2.0 
5620      */ 
5621     public static String difference(String str1, String str2) { 
5622         if (str1 == null) { 
5623             return str2; 
5624         } 
5625         if (str2 == null) { 
5626             return str1; 
5627         } 
5628         int at = indexOfDifference(str1, str2); 
5629         if (at == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
5630             return EMPTY; 
5631         } 
5632         return str2.substring(at); 
5633     } 
5634  
5635     /** 
5636      * <p>Compares two CharSequences, and returns the index at which the 
5637      * CharSequences begin to differ.</p> 
5638      * 
5639      * <p>For example, 
5640      * <code>indexOfDifference("i am a machine", "i am a robot") -> 7</code></p> 
5641      * 
5642      * <pre> 
5643      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(null, null) = -1 
5644      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("", "") = -1 
5645      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("", "abc") = 0 
5646      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("abc", "") = 0 
5647      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("abc", "abc") = -1 
5648      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("ab", "abxyz") = 2 
5649      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("abcde", "abxyz") = 2 
5650      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("abcde", "xyz") = 0 
5651      * </pre> 
5652      * 
5653      * @param cs1  the first CharSequence, may be null 
5654      * @param cs2  the second CharSequence, may be null 
5655      * @return the index where cs1 and cs2 begin to differ; -1 if they are equal 
5656      * @since 2.0 
5657      */ 
5658     public static int indexOfDifference(CharSequence cs1, CharSequence cs2) { 
5659         if (cs1 == cs2) { 
5660             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
5661         } 
5662         if (cs1 == null || cs2 == null) { 
5663             return 0; 
5664         } 
5665         int i; 
5666         for (i = 0; i < cs1.length() && i < cs2.length(); ++i) { 
5667             if (cs1.charAt(i) != cs2.charAt(i)) { 
5668                 break; 
5669             } 
5670         } 
5671         if (i < cs2.length() || i < cs1.length()) { 
5672             return i; 
5673         } 
5674         return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
5675     } 
5676  
5677     /** 
5678      * <p>Compares all CharSequences in an array and returns the index at which the 
5679      * CharSequences begin to differ.</p> 
5680      * 
5681      * <p>For example, 
5682      * <code>indexOfDifference(new String[] {"i am a machine", "i am a robot"}) -> 7</code></p> 
5683      * 
5684      * <pre> 
5685      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(null) = -1 
5686      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {}) = -1 
5687      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abc"}) = -1 
5688      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {null, null}) = -1 
5689      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"", ""}) = -1 
5690      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"", null}) = 0 
5691      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abc", null, null}) = 0 
5692      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {null, null, "abc"}) = 0 
5693      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"", "abc"}) = 0 
5694      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abc", ""}) = 0 
5695      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abc", "abc"}) = -1 
5696      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abc", "a"}) = 1 
5697      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"ab", "abxyz"}) = 2 
5698      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abcde", "abxyz"}) = 2 
5699      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abcde", "xyz"}) = 0 
5700      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"xyz", "abcde"}) = 0 
5701      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"i am a machine", "i am a robot"}) = 7 
5702      * </pre> 
5703      * 
5704      * @param css  array of CharSequences, entries may be null 
5705      * @return the index where the strings begin to differ; -1 if they are all equal 
5706      * @since 2.4 
5707      */ 
5708     public static int indexOfDifference(CharSequence[] css) { 
5709         if (css == null || css.length <= 1) { 
5710             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
5711         } 
5712         boolean anyStringNull = false; 
5713         boolean allStringsNull = true; 
5714         int arrayLen = css.length; 
5715         int shortestStrLen = Integer.MAX_VALUE; 
5716         int longestStrLen = 0; 
5717  
5718         // find the min and max string lengths; this avoids checking to make 
5719         // sure we are not exceeding the length of the string each time through 
5720         // the bottom loop. 
5721         for (int i = 0; i < arrayLen; i++) { 
5722             if (css[i] == null) { 
5723                 anyStringNull = true; 
5724                 shortestStrLen = 0; 
5725             } else { 
5726                 allStringsNull = false; 
5727                 shortestStrLen = Math.min(css[i].length(), shortestStrLen); 
5728                 longestStrLen = Math.max(css[i].length(), longestStrLen); 
5729             } 
5730         } 
5731  
5732         // handle lists containing all nulls or all empty strings 
5733         if (allStringsNull || (longestStrLen == 0 && !anyStringNull)) { 
5734             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
5735         } 
5736  
5737         // handle lists containing some nulls or some empty strings 
5738         if (shortestStrLen == 0) { 
5739             return 0; 
5740         } 
5741  
5742         // find the position with the first difference across all strings 
5743         int firstDiff = -1; 
5744         for (int stringPos = 0; stringPos < shortestStrLen; stringPos++) { 
5745             char comparisonChar = css[0].charAt(stringPos); 
5746             for (int arrayPos = 1; arrayPos < arrayLen; arrayPos++) { 
5747                 if (css[arrayPos].charAt(stringPos) != comparisonChar) { 
5748                     firstDiff = stringPos; 
5749                     break; 
5750                 } 
5751             } 
5752             if (firstDiff != -1) { 
5753                 break; 
5754             } 
5755         } 
5756  
5757         if (firstDiff == -1 && shortestStrLen != longestStrLen) { 
5758             // we compared all of the characters up to the length of the 
5759             // shortest string and didn't find a match, but the string lengths 
5760             // vary, so return the length of the shortest string. 
5761             return shortestStrLen; 
5762         } 
5763         return firstDiff; 
5764     } 
5765      
5766     /** 
5767      * <p>Compares all Strings in an array and returns the initial sequence of  
5768      * characters that is common to all of them.</p> 
5769      * 
5770      * <p>For example, 
5771      * <code>getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"i am a machine", "i am a robot"}) -> "i am a "</code></p> 
5772      * 
5773      * <pre> 
5774      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(null) = "" 
5775      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {}) = "" 
5776      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abc"}) = "abc" 
5777      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {null, null}) = "" 
5778      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"", ""}) = "" 
5779      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"", null}) = "" 
5780      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abc", null, null}) = "" 
5781      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {null, null, "abc"}) = "" 
5782      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"", "abc"}) = "" 
5783      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abc", ""}) = "" 
5784      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abc", "abc"}) = "abc" 
5785      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abc", "a"}) = "a" 
5786      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"ab", "abxyz"}) = "ab" 
5787      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abcde", "abxyz"}) = "ab" 
5788      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abcde", "xyz"}) = "" 
5789      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"xyz", "abcde"}) = "" 
5790      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"i am a machine", "i am a robot"}) = "i am a " 
5791      * </pre> 
5792      * 
5793      * @param strs  array of String objects, entries may be null 
5794      * @return the initial sequence of characters that are common to all Strings 
5795      * in the array; empty String if the array is null, the elements are all null  
5796      * or if there is no common prefix.  
5797      * @since 2.4 
5798      */ 
5799     public static String getCommonPrefix(String[] strs) { 
5800         if (strs == null || strs.length == 0) { 
5801             return EMPTY; 
5802         } 
5803         int smallestIndexOfDiff = indexOfDifference(strs); 
5804         if (smallestIndexOfDiff == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
5805             // all strings were identical 
5806             if (strs[0] == null) { 
5807                 return EMPTY; 
5808             } 
5809             return strs[0]; 
5810         } else if (smallestIndexOfDiff == 0) { 
5811             // there were no common initial characters 
5812             return EMPTY; 
5813         } else { 
5814             // we found a common initial character sequence 
5815             return strs[0].substring(0, smallestIndexOfDiff); 
5816         } 
5817     }   
5818      
5819     // Misc 
5820     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5821     /** 
5822      * <p>Find the Levenshtein distance between two Strings.</p> 
5823      * 
5824      * <p>This is the number of changes needed to change one String into 
5825      * another, where each change is a single character modification (deletion, 
5826      * insertion or substitution).</p> 
5827      * 
5828      * <p>The previous implementation of the Levenshtein distance algorithm 
5829      * was from <a href="http://www.merriampark.com/ld.htm">http://www.merriampark.com/ld.htm</a></p> 
5830      * 
5831      * <p>Chas Emerick has written an implementation in Java, which avoids an OutOfMemoryError 
5832      * which can occur when my Java implementation is used with very large strings.<br> 
5833      * This implementation of the Levenshtein distance algorithm 
5834      * is from <a href="http://www.merriampark.com/ldjava.htm">http://www.merriampark.com/ldjava.htm</a></p> 
5835      * 
5836      * <pre> 
5837      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance(null, *)             = IllegalArgumentException 
5838      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance(*, null)             = IllegalArgumentException 
5839      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("","")               = 0 
5840      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("","a")              = 1 
5841      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("aaapppp", "")       = 7 
5842      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("frog", "fog")       = 1 
5843      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("fly", "ant")        = 3 
5844      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("elephant", "hippo") = 7 
5845      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("hippo", "elephant") = 7 
5846      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("hippo", "zzzzzzzz") = 8 
5847      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("hello", "hallo")    = 1 
5848      * </pre> 
5849      * 
5850      * @param s  the first String, must not be null 
5851      * @param t  the second String, must not be null 
5852      * @return result distance 
5853      * @throws IllegalArgumentException if either String input <code>null</code> 
5854      */ 
5855     public static int getLevenshteinDistance(CharSequence s, CharSequence t) { 
5856         if (s == null || t == null) { 
5857             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Strings must not be null"); 
5858         } 
5859  
5860         /* 
5861            The difference between this impl. and the previous is that, rather  
5862            than creating and retaining a matrix of size s.length()+1 by t.length()+1,  
5863            we maintain two single-dimensional arrays of length s.length()+1.  The first, d, 
5864            is the 'current working' distance array that maintains the newest distance cost 
5865            counts as we iterate through the characters of String s.  Each time we increment 
5866            the index of String t we are comparing, d is copied to p, the second int[].  Doing so 
5867            allows us to retain the previous cost counts as required by the algorithm (taking  
5868            the minimum of the cost count to the left, up one, and diagonally up and to the left 
5869            of the current cost count being calculated).  (Note that the arrays aren't really  
5870            copied anymore, just switched...this is clearly much better than cloning an array  
5871            or doing a System.arraycopy() each time  through the outer loop.) 
5872  
5873            Effectively, the difference between the two implementations is this one does not  
5874            cause an out of memory condition when calculating the LD over two very large strings. 
5875          */ 
5876  
5877         int n = s.length(); // length of s 
5878         int m = t.length(); // length of t 
5879  
5880         if (n == 0) { 
5881             return m; 
5882         } else if (m == 0) { 
5883             return n; 
5884         } 
5885  
5886         if (n > m) { 
5887             // swap the input strings to consume less memory 
5888         	CharSequence tmp = s; 
5889             s = t; 
5890             t = tmp; 
5891             n = m; 
5892             m = t.length(); 
5893         } 
5894  
5895         int p[] = new int[n+1]; //'previous' cost array, horizontally 
5896         int d[] = new int[n+1]; // cost array, horizontally 
5897         int _d[]; //placeholder to assist in swapping p and d 
5898  
5899         // indexes into strings s and t 
5900         int i; // iterates through s 
5901         int j; // iterates through t 
5902  
5903         char t_j; // jth character of t 
5904  
5905         int cost; // cost 
5906  
5907         for (i = 0; i<=n; i++) { 
5908             p[i] = i; 
5909         } 
5910  
5911         for (j = 1; j<=m; j++) { 
5912             t_j = t.charAt(j-1); 
5913             d[0] = j; 
5914  
5915             for (i=1; i<=n; i++) { 
5916                 cost = s.charAt(i-1)==t_j ? 0 : 1; 
5917                 // minimum of cell to the left+1, to the top+1, diagonally left and up +cost 
5918                 d[i] = Math.min(Math.min(d[i-1]+1, p[i]+1),  p[i-1]+cost); 
5919             } 
5920  
5921             // copy current distance counts to 'previous row' distance counts 
5922             _d = p; 
5923             p = d; 
5924             d = _d; 
5925         } 
5926  
5927         // our last action in the above loop was to switch d and p, so p now  
5928         // actually has the most recent cost counts 
5929         return p[n]; 
5930     } 
5931  
5932     /** 
5933      * <p>Gets the minimum of three <code>int</code> values.</p> 
5934      * 
5935      * @param a  value 1 
5936      * @param b  value 2 
5937      * @param c  value 3 
5938      * @return  the smallest of the values 
5939      */ 
5940 /* 
5941     private static int min(int a, int b, int c) { 
5942         // Method copied from NumberUtils to avoid dependency on subpackage 
5943         if (b < a) { 
5944             a = b; 
5945         } 
5946         if (c < a) { 
5947             a = c; 
5948         } 
5949         return a; 
5950     } 
5951 */

5953     // startsWith 
5954     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5955  
5956     /** 
5957      * <p>Check if a String starts with a specified prefix.</p> 
5958      * 
5959      * <p><code>null</code>s are handled without exceptions. Two <code>null</code> 
5960      * references are considered to be equal. The comparison is case sensitive.</p> 
5961      * 
5962      * <pre> 
5963      * StringUtils.startsWith(null, null)      = true 
5964      * StringUtils.startsWith(null, "abc")     = false 
5965      * StringUtils.startsWith("abcdef", null)  = false 
5966      * StringUtils.startsWith("abcdef", "abc") = true 
5967      * StringUtils.startsWith("ABCDEF", "abc") = false 
5968      * </pre> 
5969      * 
5970      * @see java.lang.String#startsWith(String) 
5971      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5972      * @param prefix the prefix to find, may be null 
5973      * @return <code>true</code> if the String starts with the prefix, case sensitive, or 
5974      *  both <code>null</code> 
5975      * @since 2.4 
5976      */ 
5977     public static boolean startsWith(String str, String prefix) { 
5978         return startsWith(str, prefix, false); 
5979     } 
5980  
5981     /** 
5982      * <p>Case insensitive check if a String starts with a specified prefix.</p> 
5983      * 
5984      * <p><code>null</code>s are handled without exceptions. Two <code>null</code> 
5985      * references are considered to be equal. The comparison is case insensitive.</p> 
5986      * 
5987      * <pre> 
5988      * StringUtils.startsWithIgnoreCase(null, null)      = true 
5989      * StringUtils.startsWithIgnoreCase(null, "abc")     = false 
5990      * StringUtils.startsWithIgnoreCase("abcdef", null)  = false 
5991      * StringUtils.startsWithIgnoreCase("abcdef", "abc") = true 
5992      * StringUtils.startsWithIgnoreCase("ABCDEF", "abc") = true 
5993      * </pre> 
5994      * 
5995      * @see java.lang.String#startsWith(String) 
5996      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5997      * @param prefix the prefix to find, may be null 
5998      * @return <code>true</code> if the String starts with the prefix, case insensitive, or 
5999      *  both <code>null</code> 
6000      * @since 2.4 
6001      */ 
6002     public static boolean startsWithIgnoreCase(String str, String prefix) { 
6003         return startsWith(str, prefix, true); 
6004     } 
6005  
6006     /** 
6007      * <p>Check if a String starts with a specified prefix (optionally case insensitive).</p> 
6008      * 
6009      * @see java.lang.String#startsWith(String) 
6010      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
6011      * @param prefix the prefix to find, may be null 
6012      * @param ignoreCase inidicates whether the compare should ignore case 
6013      *  (case insensitive) or not. 
6014      * @return <code>true</code> if the String starts with the prefix or 
6015      *  both <code>null</code> 
6016      */ 
6017     private static boolean startsWith(String str, String prefix, boolean ignoreCase) { 
6018         if (str == null || prefix == null) { 
6019             return (str == null && prefix == null); 
6020         } 
6021         if (prefix.length() > str.length()) { 
6022             return false; 
6023         } 
6024         return str.regionMatches(ignoreCase, 0, prefix, 0, prefix.length()); 
6025     } 
6026      
6027     /** 
6028      * <p>Check if a String starts with any of an array of specified strings.</p> 
6029      *  
6030      * <pre> 
6031      * StringUtils.startsWithAny(null, null)      = false 
6032      * StringUtils.startsWithAny(null, new String[] {"abc"})  = false 
6033      * StringUtils.startsWithAny("abcxyz", null)     = false 
6034      * StringUtils.startsWithAny("abcxyz", new String[] {""}) = false 
6035      * StringUtils.startsWithAny("abcxyz", new String[] {"abc"}) = true 
6036      * StringUtils.startsWithAny("abcxyz", new String[] {null, "xyz", "abc"}) = true 
6037      * </pre> 
6038      * 
6039      * @param string  the String to check, may be null 
6040      * @param searchStrings the Strings to find, may be null or empty 
6041      * @return <code>true</code> if the String starts with any of the the prefixes, case insensitive, or 
6042      *  both <code>null</code> 
6043      * @since 2.5 
6044      */ 
6045     public static boolean startsWithAny(String string, String[] searchStrings) { 
6046         if (isEmpty(string) || ArrayUtils.isEmpty(searchStrings)) { 
6047             return false; 
6048         } 
6049         for (int i = 0; i < searchStrings.length; i++) { 
6050             String searchString = searchStrings[i]; 
6051             if (StringUtils.startsWith(string, searchString)) { 
6052                 return true; 
6053             } 
6054         } 
6055         return false; 
6056     }

6061     /**

6065      * references are considered to be equal. The comparison is case sensitive.</p> 
6066      * 
6067      * <pre> 
6068      * StringUtils.endsWith(null, null)      = true 
6069      * StringUtils.endsWith(null, "def")     = false 
6070      * StringUtils.endsWith("abcdef", null)  = false 
6071      * StringUtils.endsWith("abcdef", "def") = true 
6072      * StringUtils.endsWith("ABCDEF", "def") = false 
6073      * StringUtils.endsWith("ABCDEF", "cde") = false 
6074      * </pre> 
6075      * 
6076      * @see java.lang.String#endsWith(String) 
6077      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
6078      * @param suffix the suffix to find, may be null 
6079      * @return <code>true</code> if the String ends with the suffix, case sensitive, or 
6080      *  both <code>null</code> 
6081      * @since 2.4 
6082      */ 
6083     public static boolean endsWith(String str, String suffix) { 
6084         return endsWith(str, suffix, false); 
6085     } 
6086  
6087     /** 
6088      * <p>Case insensitive check if a String ends with a specified suffix.</p> 
6089      * 
6090      * <p><code>null</code>s are handled without exceptions. Two <code>null</code> 
6091      * references are considered to be equal. The comparison is case insensitive.</p> 
6092      * 
6093      * <pre> 
6094      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase(null, null)      = true 
6095      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase(null, "def")     = false 
6096      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase("abcdef", null)  = false 
6097      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase("abcdef", "def") = true 
6098      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase("ABCDEF", "def") = true 
6099      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase("ABCDEF", "cde") = false 
6100      * </pre> 
6101      * 
6102      * @see java.lang.String#endsWith(String) 
6103      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
6104      * @param suffix the suffix to find, may be null 
6105      * @return <code>true</code> if the String ends with the suffix, case insensitive, or 
6106      *  both <code>null</code> 
6107      * @since 2.4 
6108      */ 
6109     public static boolean endsWithIgnoreCase(String str, String suffix) { 
6110         return endsWith(str, suffix, true); 
6111     } 
6112  
6113     /** 
6114      * <p>Check if a String ends with a specified suffix (optionally case insensitive).</p> 
6115      * 
6116      * @see java.lang.String#endsWith(String) 
6117      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
6118      * @param suffix the suffix to find, may be null 
6119      * @param ignoreCase inidicates whether the compare should ignore case 
6120      *  (case insensitive) or not. 
6121      * @return <code>true</code> if the String starts with the prefix or 
6122      *  both <code>null</code> 
6123      */ 
6124     private static boolean endsWith(String str, String suffix, boolean ignoreCase) { 
6125         if (str == null || suffix == null) { 
6126             return str == null && suffix == null; 
6127         } 
6128         if (suffix.length() > str.length()) { 
6129             return false; 
6130         } 
6131         int strOffset = str.length() - suffix.length(); 
6132         return str.regionMatches(ignoreCase, strOffset, suffix, 0, suffix.length()); 
6133     } 
6134 }

  17 package org.apache.commons.lang3; 
  18  
  19 import java.util.ArrayList; 
  20 import java.util.Iterator; 
  21 import java.util.List; 
  22 import java.util.Locale; 
  23  
  24 import org.apache.commons.lang3.text.WordUtils; 
  25  
  26 /** 
  27  * <p>Operations on {@link java.lang.String} that are 
  28  * <code>null</code> safe.</p> 
  29  * 
  30  * <ul> 
  31  *  <li><b>IsEmpty/IsBlank</b> 
  32  *      - checks if a String contains text</li> 
  33  *  <li><b>Trim/Strip</b> 
  34  *      - removes leading and trailing whitespace</li> 
  35  *  <li><b>Equals</b> 
  36  *      - compares two strings null-safe</li> 
  37  *  <li><b>startsWith</b> 
  38  *      - check if a String starts with a prefix null-safe</li> 
  39  *  <li><b>endsWith</b> 
  40  *      - check if a String ends with a suffix null-safe</li> 
  41  *  <li><b>IndexOf/LastIndexOf/Contains</b> 
  42  *      - null-safe index-of checks 
  43  *  <li><b>IndexOfAny/LastIndexOfAny/IndexOfAnyBut/LastIndexOfAnyBut</b> 
  44  *      - index-of any of a set of Strings</li> 
  45  *  <li><b>ContainsOnly/ContainsNone/ContainsAny</b> 
  46  *      - does String contains only/none/any of these characters</li> 
  47  *  <li><b>Substring/Left/Right/Mid</b> 
  48  *      - null-safe substring extractions</li> 
  49  *  <li><b>SubstringBefore/SubstringAfter/SubstringBetween</b> 
  50  *      - substring extraction relative to other strings</li> 
  51  *  <li><b>Split/Join</b> 
  52  *      - splits a String into an array of substrings and vice versa</li> 
  53  *  <li><b>Remove/Delete</b> 
  54  *      - removes part of a String</li> 
  55  *  <li><b>Replace/Overlay</b> 
  56  *      - Searches a String and replaces one String with another</li> 
  57  *  <li><b>Chomp/Chop</b> 
  58  *      - removes the last part of a String</li> 
  59  *  <li><b>LeftPad/RightPad/Center/Repeat</b> 
  60  *      - pads a String</li> 
  61  *  <li><b>UpperCase/LowerCase/SwapCase/Capitalize/Uncapitalize</b> 
  62  *      - changes the case of a String</li> 
  63  *  <li><b>CountMatches</b> 
  64  *      - counts the number of occurrences of one String in another</li> 
  65  *  <li><b>IsAlpha/IsNumeric/IsWhitespace/IsAsciiPrintable</b> 
  66  *      - checks the characters in a String</li> 
  67  *  <li><b>DefaultString</b> 
  68  *      - protects against a null input String</li> 
  69  *  <li><b>Reverse/ReverseDelimited</b> 
  70  *      - reverses a String</li> 
  71  *  <li><b>Abbreviate</b> 
  72  *      - abbreviates a string using ellipsis</li> 
  73  *  <li><b>Difference</b> 
  74  *      - compares Strings and reports on their differences</li> 
  75  *  <li><b>LevensteinDistance</b> 
  76  *      - the number of changes needed to change one String into another</li> 
  77  * </ul> 
  78  * 
  79  * <p>The <code>StringUtils</code> class defines certain words related to 
  80  * String handling.</p> 
  81  * 
  82  * <ul> 
  83  *  <li>null - <code>null</code></li> 
  84  *  <li>empty - a zero-length string (<code>""</code>)</li> 
  85  *  <li>space - the space character (<code>' '</code>, char 32)</li> 
  86  *  <li>whitespace - the characters defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}</li> 
  87  *  <li>trim - the characters &lt;= 32 as in {@link String#trim()}</li> 
  88  * </ul> 
  89  * 
  90  * <p><code>StringUtils</code> handles <code>null</code> input Strings quietly. 
  91  * That is to say that a <code>null</code> input will return <code>null</code>. 
  92  * Where a <code>boolean</code> or <code>int</code> is being returned 
  93  * details vary by method.</p> 
  94  * 
  95  * <p>A side effect of the <code>null</code> handling is that a 
  96  * <code>NullPointerException</code> should be considered a bug in 
  97  * <code>StringUtils</code>.</p> 
  98  * 
  99  * <p>Methods in this class give sample code to explain their operation. 
 100  * The symbol <code>*</code> is used to indicate any input including <code>null</code>.</p> 
 101  * 
 102  * <p>#ThreadSafe#</p> 
 103  * @see java.lang.String 
 104  * @author Apache Software Foundation 
 105  * @author <a href="http://jakarta.apache.org/turbine/">Apache Jakarta Turbine</a> 
 106  * @author <a href="mailto:jon@latchkey.com">Jon S. Stevens</a> 
 107  * @author Daniel L. Rall 
 108  * @author <a href="mailto:gcoladonato@yahoo.com">Greg Coladonato</a> 
 109  * @author <a href="mailto:ed@apache.org">Ed Korthof</a> 
 110  * @author <a href="mailto:rand_mcneely@yahoo.com">Rand McNeely</a> 
 111  * @author <a href="mailto:fredrik@westermarck.com">Fredrik Westermarck</a> 
 112  * @author Holger Krauth 
 113  * @author <a href="mailto:alex@purpletech.com">Alexander Day Chaffee</a> 
 114  * @author <a href="mailto:hps@intermeta.de">Henning P. Schmiedehausen</a> 
 115  * @author Arun Mammen Thomas 
 116  * @author Gary Gregory 
 117  * @author Phil Steitz 
 118  * @author Al Chou 
 119  * @author Michael Davey 
 120  * @author Reuben Sivan 
 121  * @author Chris Hyzer 
 122  * @author Scott Johnson 
 123  * @since 1.0 
 124  * @version $Id$ 
 125  */ 
 126 //@Immutable 
 127 public class StringUtils { 
 128     // Performance testing notes (JDK 1.4, Jul03, scolebourne) 
 129     // Whitespace: 
 130     // Character.isWhitespace() is faster than WHITESPACE.indexOf() 
 131     // where WHITESPACE is a string of all whitespace characters 
 132     // 
 133     // Character access: 
 134     // String.charAt(n) versus toCharArray(), then array[n] 
 135     // String.charAt(n) is about 15% worse for a 10K string 
 136     // They are about equal for a length 50 string 
 137     // String.charAt(n) is about 4 times better for a length 3 string 
 138     // String.charAt(n) is best bet overall 
 139     // 
 140     // Append: 
 141     // String.concat about twice as fast as StringBuffer.append 
 142     // (not sure who tested this) 
 143  
 144     /** 
 145      * The empty String <code>""</code>. 
 146      * @since 2.0 
 147      */ 
 148     public static final String EMPTY = ""; 
 149  
 150     /** 
 151      * Represents a failed index search. 
 152      * @since 2.1 
 153      */ 
 154     public static final int INDEX_NOT_FOUND = -1; 
 155  
 156     /** 
 157      * <p>The maximum size to which the padding constant(s) can expand.</p> 
 158      */ 
 159     private static final int PAD_LIMIT = 8192; 
 160  
 161     /** 
 162      * <p><code>StringUtils</code> instances should NOT be constructed in 
 163      * standard programming. Instead, the class should be used as 
 164      * <code>StringUtils.trim(" foo ");</code>.</p> 
 165      * 
 166      * <p>This constructor is public to permit tools that require a JavaBean 
 167      * instance to operate.</p> 
 168      */ 
 169     public StringUtils() { 
 170         super(); 
 171     } 
 172  
 173     // Empty checks 
 174     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
 175     /** 
 176      * <p>Checks if a CharSequence is empty ("") or null.</p> 
 177      * 
 178      * <pre> 
 179      * StringUtils.isEmpty(null)      = true 
 180      * StringUtils.isEmpty("")        = true 
 181      * StringUtils.isEmpty(" ")       = false 
 182      * StringUtils.isEmpty("bob")     = false 
 183      * StringUtils.isEmpty("  bob  ") = false 
 184      * </pre> 
 185      * 
 186      * <p>NOTE: This method changed in Lang version 2.0. 
 187      * It no longer trims the CharSequence. 
 188      * That functionality is available in isBlank().</p> 
 189      * 
 190      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
 191      * @return <code>true</code> if the CharSequence is empty or null 
 192      */ 
 193     public static boolean isEmpty(CharSequence cs) { 
 194         return cs == null || cs.length() == 0; 
 195     } 
 196  
 197     /** 
 198      * <p>Checks if a CharSequence is not empty ("") and not null.</p> 
 199      * 
 200      * <pre> 
 201      * StringUtils.isNotEmpty(null)      = false 
 202      * StringUtils.isNotEmpty("")        = false 
 203      * StringUtils.isNotEmpty(" ")       = true 
 204      * StringUtils.isNotEmpty("bob")     = true 
 205      * StringUtils.isNotEmpty("  bob  ") = true 
 206      * </pre> 
 207      * 
 208      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
 209      * @return <code>true</code> if the CharSequence is not empty and not null 
 210      */ 
 211     public static boolean isNotEmpty(CharSequence cs) { 
 212         return !StringUtils.isEmpty(cs); 
 213     } 
 214  
 215     /** 
 216      * <p>Checks if a CharSequence is whitespace, empty ("") or null.</p> 
 217      * 
 218      * <pre> 
 219      * StringUtils.isBlank(null)      = true 
 220      * StringUtils.isBlank("")        = true 
 221      * StringUtils.isBlank(" ")       = true 
 222      * StringUtils.isBlank("bob")     = false 
 223      * StringUtils.isBlank("  bob  ") = false 
 224      * </pre> 
 225      * 
 226      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
 227      * @return <code>true</code> if the CharSequence is null, empty or whitespace 
 228      * @since 2.0 
 229      */ 
 230     public static boolean isBlank(CharSequence cs) { 
 231         int strLen; 
 232         if (cs == null || (strLen = cs.length()) == 0) { 
 233             return true; 
 234         } 
 235         for (int i = 0; i < strLen; i++) { 
 236             if ((Character.isWhitespace(cs.charAt(i)) == false)) { 
 237                 return false; 
 238             } 
 239         } 
 240         return true; 
 241     } 
 242  
 243     /** 
 244      * <p>Checks if a CharSequence is not empty (""), not null and not whitespace only.</p> 
 245      * 
 246      * <pre> 
 247      * StringUtils.isNotBlank(null)      = false 
 248      * StringUtils.isNotBlank("")        = false 
 249      * StringUtils.isNotBlank(" ")       = false 
 250      * StringUtils.isNotBlank("bob")     = true 
 251      * StringUtils.isNotBlank("  bob  ") = true 
 252      * </pre> 
 253      * 
 254      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
 255      * @return <code>true</code> if the CharSequence is 
 256      *  not empty and not null and not whitespace 
 257      * @since 2.0 
 258      */ 
 259     public static boolean isNotBlank(CharSequence cs) { 
 260         return !StringUtils.isBlank(cs); 
 261     } 
 262  
 263     // Trim 
 264     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
 265     /** 
 266      * <p>Removes control characters (char &lt;= 32) from both 
 267      * ends of this String, handling <code>null</code> by returning 
 268      * <code>null</code>.</p> 
 269      * 
 270      * <p>The String is trimmed using {@link String#trim()}. 
 271      * Trim removes start and end characters &lt;= 32. 
 272      * To strip whitespace use {@link #strip(String)}.</p> 
 273      * 
 274      * <p>To trim your choice of characters, use the 
 275      * {@link #strip(String, String)} methods.</p> 
 276      * 
 277      * <pre> 
 278      * StringUtils.trim(null)          = null 
 279      * StringUtils.trim("")            = "" 
 280      * StringUtils.trim("     ")       = "" 
 281      * StringUtils.trim("abc")         = "abc" 
 282      * StringUtils.trim("    abc    ") = "abc" 
 283      * </pre> 
 284      * 
 285      * @param str  the String to be trimmed, may be null 
 286      * @return the trimmed string, <code>null</code> if null String input 
 287      */ 
 288     public static String trim(String str) { 
 289         return str == null ? null : str.trim(); 
 290     } 
 291  
 292     /** 
 293      * <p>Removes control characters (char &lt;= 32) from both 
 294      * ends of this String returning <code>null</code> if the String is 
 295      * empty ("") after the trim or if it is <code>null</code>. 
 296      * 
 297      * <p>The String is trimmed using {@link String#trim()}. 
 298      * Trim removes start and end characters &lt;= 32. 
 299      * To strip whitespace use {@link #stripToNull(String)}.</p> 
 300      * 
 301      * <pre> 
 302      * StringUtils.trimToNull(null)          = null 
 303      * StringUtils.trimToNull("")            = null 
 304      * StringUtils.trimToNull("     ")       = null 
 305      * StringUtils.trimToNull("abc")         = "abc" 
 306      * StringUtils.trimToNull("    abc    ") = "abc" 
 307      * </pre> 
 308      * 
 309      * @param str  the String to be trimmed, may be null 
 310      * @return the trimmed String, 
 311      *  <code>null</code> if only chars &lt;= 32, empty or null String input 
 312      * @since 2.0 
 313      */ 
 314     public static String trimToNull(String str) { 
 315         String ts = trim(str); 
 316         return isEmpty(ts) ? null : ts; 
 317     } 
 318  
 319     /** 
 320      * <p>Removes control characters (char &lt;= 32) from both 
 321      * ends of this String returning an empty String ("") if the String 
 322      * is empty ("") after the trim or if it is <code>null</code>. 
 323      * 
 324      * <p>The String is trimmed using {@link String#trim()}. 
 325      * Trim removes start and end characters &lt;= 32. 
 326      * To strip whitespace use {@link #stripToEmpty(String)}.</p> 
 327      * 
 328      * <pre> 
 329      * StringUtils.trimToEmpty(null)          = "" 
 330      * StringUtils.trimToEmpty("")            = "" 
 331      * StringUtils.trimToEmpty("     ")       = "" 
 332      * StringUtils.trimToEmpty("abc")         = "abc" 
 333      * StringUtils.trimToEmpty("    abc    ") = "abc" 
 334      * </pre> 
 335      * 
 336      * @param str  the String to be trimmed, may be null 
 337      * @return the trimmed String, or an empty String if <code>null</code> input 
 338      * @since 2.0 
 339      */ 
 340     public static String trimToEmpty(String str) { 
 341         return str == null ? EMPTY : str.trim(); 
 342     } 
 343  
 344     // Stripping 
 345     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
 346     /** 
 347      * <p>Strips whitespace from the start and end of a String.</p> 
 348      * 
 349      * <p>This is similar to {@link #trim(String)} but removes whitespace. 
 350      * Whitespace is defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
 351      * 
 352      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
 353      * 
 354      * <pre> 
 355      * StringUtils.strip(null)     = null 
 356      * StringUtils.strip("")       = "" 
 357      * StringUtils.strip("   ")    = "" 
 358      * StringUtils.strip("abc")    = "abc" 
 359      * StringUtils.strip("  abc")  = "abc" 
 360      * StringUtils.strip("abc  ")  = "abc" 
 361      * StringUtils.strip(" abc ")  = "abc" 
 362      * StringUtils.strip(" ab c ") = "ab c" 
 363      * </pre> 
 364      * 
 365      * @param str  the String to remove whitespace from, may be null 
 366      * @return the stripped String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
 367      */ 
 368     public static String strip(String str) { 
 369         return strip(str, null); 
 370     } 
 371  
 372     /** 
 373      * <p>Strips whitespace from the start and end of a String  returning 
 374      * <code>null</code> if the String is empty ("") after the strip.</p> 
 375      * 
 376      * <p>This is similar to {@link #trimToNull(String)} but removes whitespace. 
 377      * Whitespace is defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
 378      * 
 379      * <pre> 
 380      * StringUtils.stripToNull(null)     = null 
 381      * StringUtils.stripToNull("")       = null 
 382      * StringUtils.stripToNull("   ")    = null 
 383      * StringUtils.stripToNull("abc")    = "abc" 
 384      * StringUtils.stripToNull("  abc")  = "abc" 
 385      * StringUtils.stripToNull("abc  ")  = "abc" 
 386      * StringUtils.stripToNull(" abc ")  = "abc" 
 387      * StringUtils.stripToNull(" ab c ") = "ab c" 
 388      * </pre> 
 389      * 
 390      * @param str  the String to be stripped, may be null 
 391      * @return the stripped String, 
 392      *  <code>null</code> if whitespace, empty or null String input 
 393      * @since 2.0 
 394      */ 
 395     public static String stripToNull(String str) { 
 396         if (str == null) { 
 397             return null; 
 398         } 
 399         str = strip(str, null); 
 400         return str.length() == 0 ? null : str; 
 401     } 
 402  
 403     /** 
 404      * <p>Strips whitespace from the start and end of a String  returning 
 405      * an empty String if <code>null</code> input.</p> 
 406      * 
 407      * <p>This is similar to {@link #trimToEmpty(String)} but removes whitespace. 
 408      * Whitespace is defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
 409      * 
 410      * <pre> 
 411      * StringUtils.stripToEmpty(null)     = "" 
 412      * StringUtils.stripToEmpty("")       = "" 
 413      * StringUtils.stripToEmpty("   ")    = "" 
 414      * StringUtils.stripToEmpty("abc")    = "abc" 
 415      * StringUtils.stripToEmpty("  abc")  = "abc" 
 416      * StringUtils.stripToEmpty("abc  ")  = "abc" 
 417      * StringUtils.stripToEmpty(" abc ")  = "abc" 
 418      * StringUtils.stripToEmpty(" ab c ") = "ab c" 
 419      * </pre> 
 420      * 
 421      * @param str  the String to be stripped, may be null 
 422      * @return the trimmed String, or an empty String if <code>null</code> input 
 423      * @since 2.0 
 424      */ 
 425     public static String stripToEmpty(String str) { 
 426         return str == null ? EMPTY : strip(str, null); 
 427     } 
 428  
 429     /** 
 430      * <p>Strips any of a set of characters from the start and end of a String. 
 431      * This is similar to {@link String#trim()} but allows the characters 
 432      * to be stripped to be controlled.</p> 
 433      * 
 434      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
 435      * An empty string ("") input returns the empty string.</p> 
 436      * 
 437      * <p>If the stripChars String is <code>null</code>, whitespace is 
 438      * stripped as defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}. 
 439      * Alternatively use {@link #strip(String)}.</p> 
 440      * 
 441      * <pre> 
 442      * StringUtils.strip(null, *)          = null 
 443      * StringUtils.strip("", *)            = "" 
 444      * StringUtils.strip("abc", null)      = "abc" 
 445      * StringUtils.strip("  abc", null)    = "abc" 
 446      * StringUtils.strip("abc  ", null)    = "abc" 
 447      * StringUtils.strip(" abc ", null)    = "abc" 
 448      * StringUtils.strip("  abcyx", "xyz") = "  abc" 
 449      * </pre> 
 450      * 
 451      * @param str  the String to remove characters from, may be null 
 452      * @param stripChars  the characters to remove, null treated as whitespace 
 453      * @return the stripped String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
 454      */ 
 455     public static String strip(String str, String stripChars) { 
 456         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
 457             return str; 
 458         } 
 459         str = stripStart(str, stripChars); 
 460         return stripEnd(str, stripChars); 
 461     } 
 462  
 463     /** 
 464      * <p>Strips any of a set of characters from the start of a String.</p> 
 465      * 
 466      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
 467      * An empty string ("") input returns the empty string.</p> 
 468      * 
 469      * <p>If the stripChars String is <code>null</code>, whitespace is 
 470      * stripped as defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
 471      * 
 472      * <pre> 
 473      * StringUtils.stripStart(null, *)          = null 
 474      * StringUtils.stripStart("", *)            = "" 
 475      * StringUtils.stripStart("abc", "")        = "abc" 
 476      * StringUtils.stripStart("abc", null)      = "abc" 
 477      * StringUtils.stripStart("  abc", null)    = "abc" 
 478      * StringUtils.stripStart("abc  ", null)    = "abc  " 
 479      * StringUtils.stripStart(" abc ", null)    = "abc " 
 480      * StringUtils.stripStart("yxabc  ", "xyz") = "abc  " 
 481      * </pre> 
 482      * 
 483      * @param str  the String to remove characters from, may be null 
 484      * @param stripChars  the characters to remove, null treated as whitespace 
 485      * @return the stripped String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
 486      */ 
 487     public static String stripStart(String str, String stripChars) { 
 488         int strLen; 
 489         if (str == null || (strLen = str.length()) == 0) { 
 490             return str; 
 491         } 
 492         int start = 0; 
 493         if (stripChars == null) { 
 494             while ((start != strLen) && Character.isWhitespace(str.charAt(start))) { 
 495                 start++; 
 496             } 
 497         } else if (stripChars.length() == 0) { 
 498             return str; 
 499         } else { 
 500             while ((start != strLen) && (stripChars.indexOf(str.charAt(start)) != INDEX_NOT_FOUND)) { 
 501                 start++; 
 502             } 
 503         } 
 504         return str.substring(start); 
 505     } 
 506  
 507     /** 
 508      * <p>Strips any of a set of characters from the end of a String.</p> 
 509      * 
 510      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
 511      * An empty string ("") input returns the empty string.</p> 
 512      * 
 513      * <p>If the stripChars String is <code>null</code>, whitespace is 
 514      * stripped as defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
 515      * 
 516      * <pre> 
 517      * StringUtils.stripEnd(null, *)          = null 
 518      * StringUtils.stripEnd("", *)            = "" 
 519      * StringUtils.stripEnd("abc", "")        = "abc" 
 520      * StringUtils.stripEnd("abc", null)      = "abc" 
 521      * StringUtils.stripEnd("  abc", null)    = "  abc" 
 522      * StringUtils.stripEnd("abc  ", null)    = "abc" 
 523      * StringUtils.stripEnd(" abc ", null)    = " abc" 
 524      * StringUtils.stripEnd("  abcyx", "xyz") = "  abc" 
 525      * </pre> 
 526      * 
 527      * @param str  the String to remove characters from, may be null 
 528      * @param stripChars  the characters to remove, null treated as whitespace 
 529      * @return the stripped String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
 530      */ 
 531     public static String stripEnd(String str, String stripChars) { 
 532         int end; 
 533         if (str == null || (end = str.length()) == 0) { 
 534             return str; 
 535         } 
 536  
 537         if (stripChars == null) { 
 538             while ((end != 0) && Character.isWhitespace(str.charAt(end - 1))) { 
 539                 end--; 
 540             } 
 541         } else if (stripChars.length() == 0) { 
 542             return str; 
 543         } else { 
 544             while ((end != 0) && (stripChars.indexOf(str.charAt(end - 1)) != INDEX_NOT_FOUND)) { 
 545                 end--; 
 546             } 
 547         } 
 548         return str.substring(0, end); 
 549     } 
 550  
 551     // StripAll 
 552     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
 553     /** 
 554      * <p>Strips whitespace from the start and end of every String in an array. 
 555      * Whitespace is defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
 556      * 
 557      * <p>A new array is returned each time, except for length zero. 
 558      * A <code>null</code> array will return <code>null</code>. 
 559      * An empty array will return itself. 
 560      * A <code>null</code> array entry will be ignored.</p> 
 561      * 
 562      * <pre> 
 563      * StringUtils.stripAll(null)             = null 
 564      * StringUtils.stripAll([])               = [] 
 565      * StringUtils.stripAll(["abc", "  abc"]) = ["abc", "abc"] 
 566      * StringUtils.stripAll(["abc  ", null])  = ["abc", null] 
 567      * </pre> 
 568      * 
 569      * @param strs  the array to remove whitespace from, may be null 
 570      * @return the stripped Strings, <code>null</code> if null array input 
 571      */ 
 572     public static String[] stripAll(String[] strs) { 
 573         return stripAll(strs, null); 
 574     } 
 575  
 576     /** 
 577      * <p>Strips any of a set of characters from the start and end of every 
 578      * String in an array.</p> 
 579      * Whitespace is defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
 580      * 
 581      * <p>A new array is returned each time, except for length zero. 
 582      * A <code>null</code> array will return <code>null</code>. 
 583      * An empty array will return itself. 
 584      * A <code>null</code> array entry will be ignored. 
 585      * A <code>null</code> stripChars will strip whitespace as defined by 
 586      * {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
 587      * 
 588      * <pre> 
 589      * StringUtils.stripAll(null, *)                = null 
 590      * StringUtils.stripAll([], *)                  = [] 
 591      * StringUtils.stripAll(["abc", "  abc"], null) = ["abc", "abc"] 
 592      * StringUtils.stripAll(["abc  ", null], null)  = ["abc", null] 
 593      * StringUtils.stripAll(["abc  ", null], "yz")  = ["abc  ", null] 
 594      * StringUtils.stripAll(["yabcz", null], "yz")  = ["abc", null] 
 595      * </pre> 
 596      * 
 597      * @param strs  the array to remove characters from, may be null 
 598      * @param stripChars  the characters to remove, null treated as whitespace 
 599      * @return the stripped Strings, <code>null</code> if null array input 
 600      */ 
 601     public static String[] stripAll(String[] strs, String stripChars) { 
 602         int strsLen; 
 603         if (strs == null || (strsLen = strs.length) == 0) { 
 604             return strs; 
 605         } 
 606         String[] newArr = new String[strsLen]; 
 607         for (int i = 0; i < strsLen; i++) { 
 608             newArr[i] = strip(strs[i], stripChars); 
 609         } 
 610         return newArr; 
 611     } 
 612  
 613     /** 
 614      * <p>Removes the accents from a string. </p> 
 615      * <p>NOTE: This is a JDK 1.6 method, it will fail on JDK 1.5. </p> 
 616      * 
 617      * <pre> 
 618      * StringUtils.stripAccents(null)                = null 
 619      * StringUtils.stripAccents("")                  = "" 
 620      * StringUtils.stripAccents("control")           = "control" 
 621      * StringUtils.stripAccents("&ecute;clair")      = "eclair" 
 622      * </pre> 
 623      *  
 624      * @param input String to be stripped 
 625      * @return String without accents on the text 
 626      * 
 627      * @since 3.0 
 628      */ 
 629     public static String stripAccents(String input) { 
 630         if(input == null) { 
 631             return null; 
 632         } 
 633         if(SystemUtils.isJavaVersionAtLeast(1.6f)) { 
 634  
 635             // String decomposed = Normalizer.normalize(input, Normalizer.Form.NFD); 
 636  
 637             // START of 1.5 reflection - in 1.6 use the line commented out above 
 638             try { 
 639                 // get java.text.Normalizer.Form class 
 640                 Class<?> normalizerFormClass = ClassUtils.getClass("java.text.Normalizer$Form", false); 
 641  
 642                 // get Normlizer class 
 643                 Class<?> normalizerClass = ClassUtils.getClass("java.text.Normalizer", false); 
 644  
 645                 // get static method on Normalizer 
 646                 java.lang.reflect.Method method = normalizerClass.getMethod("normalize", CharSequence.class, normalizerFormClass ); 
 647  
 648                 // get Normalizer.NFD field 
 649                 java.lang.reflect.Field nfd = normalizerFormClass.getField("NFD"); 
 650  
 651                 // invoke method 
 652                 String decomposed = (String) method.invoke( null, input, nfd.get(null) ); 
 653                 // END of 1.5 reflection 
 654  
 655                 java.util.regex.Pattern accentPattern = java.util.regex.Pattern.compile("\\p{InCombiningDiacriticalMarks}+"); 
 656                 return accentPattern.matcher(decomposed).replaceAll(""); 
 657             } catch(ClassNotFoundException cnfe) { 
 658                 throw new RuntimeException("ClassNotFoundException occurred during 1.6 backcompat code", cnfe); 
 659             } catch(NoSuchMethodException nsme) { 
 660                 throw new RuntimeException("NoSuchMethodException occurred during 1.6 backcompat code", nsme); 
 661             } catch(NoSuchFieldException nsfe) { 
 662                 throw new RuntimeException("NoSuchFieldException occurred during 1.6 backcompat code", nsfe); 
 663             } catch(IllegalAccessException iae) { 
 664                 throw new RuntimeException("IllegalAccessException occurred during 1.6 backcompat code", iae); 
 665             } catch(IllegalArgumentException iae) { 
 666                 throw new RuntimeException("IllegalArgumentException occurred during 1.6 backcompat code", iae); 
 667             } catch(java.lang.reflect.InvocationTargetException ite) { 
 668                 throw new RuntimeException("InvocationTargetException occurred during 1.6 backcompat code", ite); 
 669             } catch(SecurityException se) { 
 670                 throw new RuntimeException("SecurityException occurred during 1.6 backcompat code", se); 
 671             } 
 672         } else { 
 673             throw new UnsupportedOperationException("The stripAccents(String) method is not supported until Java 1.6"); 
 674         } 
 675     }

 677     // Equals 
 678     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
 679     /** 
 680      * <p>Compares two CharSequences, returning <code>true</code> if they are equal.</p> 
 681      * 
 682      * <p><code>null</code>s are handled without exceptions. Two <code>null</code> 
 683      * references are considered to be equal. The comparison is case sensitive.</p> 
 684      * 
 685      * <pre> 
 686      * StringUtils.equals(null, null)   = true 
 687      * StringUtils.equals(null, "abc")  = false 
 688      * StringUtils.equals("abc", null)  = false 
 689      * StringUtils.equals("abc", "abc") = true 
 690      * StringUtils.equals("abc", "ABC") = false 
 691      * </pre> 
 692      * 
 693      * @see java.lang.String#equals(Object) 
 694      * @param cs1  the first CharSequence, may be null 
 695      * @param cs2  the second CharSequence, may be null 
 696      * @return <code>true</code> if the CharSequences are equal, case sensitive, or 
 697      *  both <code>null</code> 
 698      */ 
 699     public static boolean equals(CharSequence cs1, CharSequence cs2) { 
 700         return cs1 == null ? cs2 == null : cs1.equals(cs2); 
 701     } 
 702  
 703     /** 
 704      * <p>Compares two Strings, returning <code>true</code> if they are equal ignoring 
 705      * the case.</p> 
 706      * 
 707      * <p><code>null</code>s are handled without exceptions. Two <code>null</code> 
 708      * references are considered equal. Comparison is case insensitive.</p> 
 709      * 
 710      * <pre> 
 711      * StringUtils.equalsIgnoreCase(null, null)   = true 
 712      * StringUtils.equalsIgnoreCase(null, "abc")  = false 
 713      * StringUtils.equalsIgnoreCase("abc", null)  = false 
 714      * StringUtils.equalsIgnoreCase("abc", "abc") = true 
 715      * StringUtils.equalsIgnoreCase("abc", "ABC") = true 
 716      * </pre> 
 717      * 
 718      * @see java.lang.String#equalsIgnoreCase(String) 
 719      * @param str1  the first String, may be null 
 720      * @param str2  the second String, may be null 
 721      * @return <code>true</code> if the Strings are equal, case insensitive, or 
 722      *  both <code>null</code> 
 723      */ 
 724     public static boolean equalsIgnoreCase(String str1, String str2) { 
 725         return str1 == null ? str2 == null : str1.equalsIgnoreCase(str2); 
 726     }

 728     // IndexOf 
 729     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
 730     /** 
 731      * <p>Finds the first index within a String, handling <code>null</code>. 
 732      * This method uses {@link String#indexOf(int)}.</p> 
 733      * 
 734      * <p>A <code>null</code> or empty ("") String will return <code>INDEX_NOT_FOUND (-1)</code>.</p> 
 735      * 
 736      * <pre> 
 737      * StringUtils.indexOf(null, *)         = -1 
 738      * StringUtils.indexOf("", *)           = -1 
 739      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", 'a') = 0 
 740      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", 'b') = 2 
 741      * </pre> 
 742      * 
 743      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
 744      * @param searchChar  the character to find 
 745      * @return the first index of the search character, 
 746      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
 747      * @since 2.0 
 748      */ 
 749     public static int indexOf(String str, char searchChar) { 
 750         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
 751             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
 752         } 
 753         return str.indexOf(searchChar); 
 754     }

 756     /** 
 757      * <p>Finds the first index within a String from a start position, 
 758      * handling <code>null</code>. 
 759      * This method uses {@link String#indexOf(int, int)}.</p> 
 760      * 
 761      * <p>A <code>null</code> or empty ("") String will return <code>(INDEX_NOT_FOUND) -1</code>. 
 762      * A negative start position is treated as zero. 
 763      * A start position greater than the string length returns <code>-1</code>.</p> 
 764      * 
 765      * <pre> 
 766      * StringUtils.indexOf(null, *, *)          = -1 
 767      * StringUtils.indexOf("", *, *)            = -1 
 768      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", 'b', 0)  = 2 
 769      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", 'b', 3)  = 5 
 770      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", 'b', 9)  = -1 
 771      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", 'b', -1) = 2 
 772      * </pre> 
 773      * 
 774      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
 775      * @param searchChar  the character to find 
 776      * @param startPos  the start position, negative treated as zero 
 777      * @return the first index of the search character, 
 778      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
 779      * @since 2.0 
 780      */ 
 781     public static int indexOf(String str, char searchChar, int startPos) { 
 782         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
 783             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
 784         } 
 785         return str.indexOf(searchChar, startPos); 
 786     }
