 788     /** 
 789      * <p>Finds the first index within a String, handling <code>null</code>. 
 790      * This method uses {@link String#indexOf(String)}.</p> 
 791      * 
 792      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
 793      * 
 794      * <pre> 
 795      * StringUtils.indexOf(null, *)          = -1 
 796      * StringUtils.indexOf(*, null)          = -1 
 797      * StringUtils.indexOf("", "")           = 0 
 798      * StringUtils.indexOf("", *)            = -1 (except when * = "") 
 799      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "a")  = 0 
 800      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "b")  = 2 
 801      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "ab") = 1 
 802      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "")   = 0 
 803      * </pre> 
 804      * 
 805      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
 806      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
 807      * @return the first index of the search String, 
 808      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
 809      * @since 2.0 
 810      */ 
 811     public static int indexOf(String str, String searchStr) { 
 812         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
 813             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
 814         } 
 815         return str.indexOf(searchStr); 
 816     }

 818     /** 
 819      * <p>Finds the first index within a String, handling <code>null</code>. 
 820      * This method uses {@link String#indexOf(String, int)}.</p> 
 821      * 
 822      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
 823      * A negative start position is treated as zero. 
 824      * An empty ("") search String always matches. 
 825      * A start position greater than the string length only matches 
 826      * an empty search String.</p> 
 827      * 
 828      * <pre> 
 829      * StringUtils.indexOf(null, *, *)          = -1 
 830      * StringUtils.indexOf(*, null, *)          = -1 
 831      * StringUtils.indexOf("", "", 0)           = 0 
 832      * StringUtils.indexOf("", *, 0)            = -1 (except when * = "") 
 833      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "a", 0)  = 0 
 834      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "b", 0)  = 2 
 835      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "ab", 0) = 1 
 836      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "b", 3)  = 5 
 837      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "b", 9)  = -1 
 838      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "b", -1) = 2 
 839      * StringUtils.indexOf("aabaabaa", "", 2)   = 2 
 840      * StringUtils.indexOf("abc", "", 9)        = 3 
 841      * </pre> 
 842      * 
 843      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
 844      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
 845      * @param startPos  the start position, negative treated as zero 
 846      * @return the first index of the search String, 
 847      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
 848      * @since 2.0 
 849      */ 
 850     public static int indexOf(String str, String searchStr, int startPos) { 
 851         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
 852             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
 853         } 
 854         return str.indexOf(searchStr, startPos); 
 855     }

 857     /** 
 858      * <p>Finds the n-th index within a String, handling <code>null</code>. 
 859      * This method uses {@link String#indexOf(String)}.</p> 
 860      * 
 861      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
 862      * 
 863      * <pre> 
 864      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf(null, *, *)          = -1 
 865      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf(*, null, *)          = -1 
 866      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf("", "", *)           = 0 
 867      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "a", 1)  = 0 
 868      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "a", 2)  = 1 
 869      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "b", 1)  = 2 
 870      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "b", 2)  = 5 
 871      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "ab", 1) = 1 
 872      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "ab", 2) = 4 
 873      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "", 1)   = 0 
 874      * StringUtils.ordinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "", 2)   = 0 
 875      * </pre> 
 876      * 
 877      * <p>Note that 'head(String str, int n)' may be implemented as: </p> 
 878      * 
 879      * <pre> 
 880      *   str.substring(0, lastOrdinalIndexOf(str, "\n", n)) 
 881      * </pre> 
 882      * 
 883      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
 884      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
 885      * @param ordinal  the n-th <code>searchStr</code> to find 
 886      * @return the n-th index of the search String, 
 887      *  <code>-1</code> (<code>INDEX_NOT_FOUND</code>) if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
 888      * @since 2.1 
 889      */ 
 890     public static int ordinalIndexOf(String str, String searchStr, int ordinal) { 
 891         return ordinalIndexOf(str, searchStr, ordinal, false); 
 892     } 
 893  
 894     /** 
 895      * <p>Finds the n-th index within a String, handling <code>null</code>. 
 896      * This method uses {@link String#indexOf(String)}.</p> 
 897      * 
 898      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
 899      * 
 900      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
 901      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
 902      * @param ordinal  the n-th <code>searchStr</code> to find 
 903      * @param lastIndex true if lastOrdinalIndexOf() otherwise false if ordinalIndexOf() 
 904      * @return the n-th index of the search String, 
 905      *  <code>-1</code> (<code>INDEX_NOT_FOUND</code>) if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
 906      */ 
 907     // Shared code between ordinalIndexOf(String,String,int) and lastOrdinalIndexOf(String,String,int) 
 908     private static int ordinalIndexOf(String str, String searchStr, int ordinal, boolean lastIndex) { 
 909         if (str == null || searchStr == null || ordinal <= 0) { 
 910             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
 911         } 
 912         if (searchStr.length() == 0) { 
 913             return lastIndex ? str.length() : 0; 
 914         } 
 915         int found = 0; 
 916         int index = lastIndex ? str.length() : INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
 917         do { 
 918             if(lastIndex) { 
 919                 index = str.lastIndexOf(searchStr, index - 1); 
 920             } else { 
 921                 index = str.indexOf(searchStr, index + 1); 
 922             } 
 923             if (index < 0) { 
 924                 return index; 
 925             } 
 926             found++; 
 927         } while (found < ordinal); 
 928         return index; 
 929     } 
 930  
 931     /** 
 932      * <p>Case in-sensitive find of the first index within a String.</p> 
 933      * 
 934      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
 935      * A negative start position is treated as zero. 
 936      * An empty ("") search String always matches. 
 937      * A start position greater than the string length only matches 
 938      * an empty search String.</p> 
 939      * 
 940      * <pre> 
 941      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase(null, *)          = -1 
 942      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase(*, null)          = -1 
 943      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("", "")           = 0 
 944      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "a")  = 0 
 945      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "b")  = 2 
 946      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "ab") = 1 
 947      * </pre> 
 948      * 
 949      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
 950      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
 951      * @return the first index of the search String, 
 952      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
 953      * @since 2.5 
 954      */ 
 955     public static int indexOfIgnoreCase(String str, String searchStr) { 
 956         return indexOfIgnoreCase(str, searchStr, 0); 
 957     } 
 958  
 959     /** 
 960      * <p>Case in-sensitive find of the first index within a String 
 961      * from the specified position.</p> 
 962      * 
 963      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
 964      * A negative start position is treated as zero. 
 965      * An empty ("") search String always matches. 
 966      * A start position greater than the string length only matches 
 967      * an empty search String.</p> 
 968      * 
 969      * <pre> 
 970      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase(null, *, *)          = -1 
 971      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase(*, null, *)          = -1 
 972      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("", "", 0)           = 0 
 973      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "A", 0)  = 0 
 974      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "B", 0)  = 2 
 975      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "AB", 0) = 1 
 976      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "B", 3)  = 5 
 977      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "B", 9)  = -1 
 978      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "B", -1) = 2 
 979      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "", 2)   = 2 
 980      * StringUtils.indexOfIgnoreCase("abc", "", 9)        = 3 
 981      * </pre> 
 982      * 
 983      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
 984      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
 985      * @param startPos  the start position, negative treated as zero 
 986      * @return the first index of the search String, 
 987      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
 988      * @since 2.5 
 989      */ 
 990     public static int indexOfIgnoreCase(String str, String searchStr, int startPos) { 
 991         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
 992             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
 993         } 
 994         if (startPos < 0) { 
 995             startPos = 0; 
 996         } 
 997         int endLimit = (str.length() - searchStr.length()) + 1; 
 998         if (startPos > endLimit) { 
 999             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1000         } 
1001         if (searchStr.length() == 0) { 
1002             return startPos; 
1003         } 
1004         for (int i = startPos; i < endLimit; i++) { 
1005             if (str.regionMatches(true, i, searchStr, 0, searchStr.length())) { 
1006                 return i; 
1007             } 
1008         } 
1009         return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1010     }

1012     // LastIndexOf 
1013     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1014     /** 
1015      * <p>Finds the last index within a String, handling <code>null</code>. 
1016      * This method uses {@link String#lastIndexOf(int)}.</p> 
1017      * 
1018      * <p>A <code>null</code> or empty ("") String will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1019      * 
1020      * <pre> 
1021      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf(null, *)         = -1 
1022      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("", *)           = -1 
1023      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", 'a') = 7 
1024      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", 'b') = 5 
1025      * </pre> 
1026      * 
1027      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1028      * @param searchChar  the character to find 
1029      * @return the last index of the search character, 
1030      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
1031      * @since 2.0 
1032      */ 
1033     public static int lastIndexOf(String str, char searchChar) { 
1034         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
1035             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1036         } 
1037         return str.lastIndexOf(searchChar); 
1038     } 
1039  
1040     /** 
1041      * <p>Finds the last index within a String from a start position, 
1042      * handling <code>null</code>. 
1043      * This method uses {@link String#lastIndexOf(int, int)}.</p> 
1044      * 
1045      * <p>A <code>null</code> or empty ("") String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1046      * A negative start position returns <code>-1</code>. 
1047      * A start position greater than the string length searches the whole string.</p> 
1048      * 
1049      * <pre> 
1050      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf(null, *, *)          = -1 
1051      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("", *,  *)           = -1 
1052      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", 'b', 8)  = 5 
1053      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", 'b', 4)  = 2 
1054      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", 'b', 0)  = -1 
1055      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", 'b', 9)  = 5 
1056      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", 'b', -1) = -1 
1057      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", 'a', 0)  = 0 
1058      * </pre> 
1059      * 
1060      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1061      * @param searchChar  the character to find 
1062      * @param startPos  the start position 
1063      * @return the last index of the search character, 
1064      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
1065      * @since 2.0 
1066      */ 
1067     public static int lastIndexOf(String str, char searchChar, int startPos) { 
1068         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
1069             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1070         } 
1071         return str.lastIndexOf(searchChar, startPos); 
1072     }

1074     /** 
1075      * <p>Finds the last index within a String, handling <code>null</code>. 
1076      * This method uses {@link String#lastIndexOf(String)}.</p> 
1077      * 
1078      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1079      * 
1080      * <pre> 
1081      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf(null, *)          = -1 
1082      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf(*, null)          = -1 
1083      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("", "")           = 0 
1084      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "a")  = 0 
1085      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "b")  = 2 
1086      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "ab") = 1 
1087      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "")   = 8 
1088      * </pre> 
1089      * 
1090      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1091      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
1092      * @return the last index of the search String, 
1093      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
1094      * @since 2.0 
1095      */ 
1096     public static int lastIndexOf(String str, String searchStr) { 
1097         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
1098             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1099         } 
1100         return str.lastIndexOf(searchStr); 
1101     }

1103     /** 
1104      * <p>Finds the n-th last index within a String, handling <code>null</code>. 
1105      * This method uses {@link String#lastIndexOf(String)}.</p> 
1106      * 
1107      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1108      * 
1109      * <pre> 
1110      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf(null, *, *)          = -1 
1111      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf(*, null, *)          = -1 
1112      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf("", "", *)           = 0 
1113      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "a", 1)  = 7 
1114      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "a", 2)  = 6 
1115      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "b", 1)  = 5 
1116      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "b", 2)  = 2 
1117      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "ab", 1) = 4 
1118      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "ab", 2) = 1 
1119      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "", 1)   = 8 
1120      * StringUtils.lastOrdinalIndexOf("aabaabaa", "", 2)   = 8 
1121      * </pre> 
1122      * 
1123      * <p>Note that 'tail(String str, int n)' may be implemented as: </p> 
1124      * 
1125      * <pre> 
1126      *   str.substring(lastOrdinalIndexOf(str, "\n", n) + 1) 
1127      * </pre> 
1128      * 
1129      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1130      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
1131      * @param ordinal  the n-th last <code>searchStr</code> to find 
1132      * @return the n-th last index of the search String, 
1133      *  <code>-1</code> (<code>INDEX_NOT_FOUND</code>) if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
1134      * @since 2.5 
1135      */ 
1136     public static int lastOrdinalIndexOf(String str, String searchStr, int ordinal) { 
1137         return ordinalIndexOf(str, searchStr, ordinal, true); 
1138     }

1140     /** 
1141      * <p>Finds the first index within a String, handling <code>null</code>. 
1142      * This method uses {@link String#lastIndexOf(String, int)}.</p> 
1143      * 
1144      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1145      * A negative start position returns <code>-1</code>. 
1146      * An empty ("") search String always matches unless the start position is negative. 
1147      * A start position greater than the string length searches the whole string.</p> 
1148      * 
1149      * <pre> 
1150      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf(null, *, *)          = -1 
1151      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf(*, null, *)          = -1 
1152      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "a", 8)  = 7 
1153      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "b", 8)  = 5 
1154      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "ab", 8) = 4 
1155      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "b", 9)  = 5 
1156      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "b", -1) = -1 
1157      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "a", 0)  = 0 
1158      * StringUtils.lastIndexOf("aabaabaa", "b", 0)  = -1 
1159      * </pre> 
1160      * 
1161      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1162      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
1163      * @param startPos  the start position, negative treated as zero 
1164      * @return the first index of the search String, 
1165      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
1166      * @since 2.0 
1167      */ 
1168     public static int lastIndexOf(String str, String searchStr, int startPos) { 
1169         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
1170             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1171         } 
1172         return str.lastIndexOf(searchStr, startPos); 
1173     }

1175     /** 
1176      * <p>Case in-sensitive find of the last index within a String.</p> 
1177      * 
1178      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1179      * A negative start position returns <code>-1</code>. 
1180      * An empty ("") search String always matches unless the start position is negative. 
1181      * A start position greater than the string length searches the whole string.</p> 
1182      * 
1183      * <pre> 
1184      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase(null, *)          = -1 
1185      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase(*, null)          = -1 
1186      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "A")  = 7 
1187      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "B")  = 5 
1188      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "AB") = 4 
1189      * </pre> 
1190      * 
1191      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1192      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
1193      * @return the first index of the search String, 
1194      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
1195      * @since 2.5 
1196      */ 
1197     public static int lastIndexOfIgnoreCase(String str, String searchStr) { 
1198         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
1199             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1200         } 
1201         return lastIndexOfIgnoreCase(str, searchStr, str.length()); 
1202     } 
1203  
1204     /** 
1205      * <p>Case in-sensitive find of the last index within a String 
1206      * from the specified position.</p> 
1207      * 
1208      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1209      * A negative start position returns <code>-1</code>. 
1210      * An empty ("") search String always matches unless the start position is negative. 
1211      * A start position greater than the string length searches the whole string.</p> 
1212      * 
1213      * <pre> 
1214      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase(null, *, *)          = -1 
1215      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase(*, null, *)          = -1 
1216      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "A", 8)  = 7 
1217      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "B", 8)  = 5 
1218      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "AB", 8) = 4 
1219      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "B", 9)  = 5 
1220      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "B", -1) = -1 
1221      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "A", 0)  = 0 
1222      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfIgnoreCase("aabaabaa", "B", 0)  = -1 
1223      * </pre> 
1224      * 
1225      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1226      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
1227      * @param startPos  the start position 
1228      * @return the first index of the search String, 
1229      *  -1 if no match or <code>null</code> string input 
1230      * @since 2.5 
1231      */ 
1232     public static int lastIndexOfIgnoreCase(String str, String searchStr, int startPos) { 
1233         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
1234             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1235         } 
1236         if (startPos > (str.length() - searchStr.length())) { 
1237             startPos = str.length() - searchStr.length(); 
1238         } 
1239         if (startPos < 0) { 
1240             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1241         } 
1242         if (searchStr.length() == 0) { 
1243             return startPos; 
1244         } 
1245  
1246         for (int i = startPos; i >= 0; i--) { 
1247             if (str.regionMatches(true, i, searchStr, 0, searchStr.length())) { 
1248                 return i; 
1249             } 
1250         } 
1251         return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1252     }

1254     // Contains 
1255     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1256     /** 
1257      * <p>Checks if String contains a search character, handling <code>null</code>. 
1258      * This method uses {@link String#indexOf(int)}.</p> 
1259      * 
1260      * <p>A <code>null</code> or empty ("") String will return <code>false</code>.</p> 
1261      * 
1262      * <pre> 
1263      * StringUtils.contains(null, *)    = false 
1264      * StringUtils.contains("", *)      = false 
1265      * StringUtils.contains("abc", 'a') = true 
1266      * StringUtils.contains("abc", 'z') = false 
1267      * </pre> 
1268      * 
1269      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1270      * @param searchChar  the character to find 
1271      * @return true if the String contains the search character, 
1272      *  false if not or <code>null</code> string input 
1273      * @since 2.0 
1274      */ 
1275     public static boolean contains(String str, char searchChar) { 
1276         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
1277             return false; 
1278         } 
1279         return str.indexOf(searchChar) >= 0; 
1280     } 
1281  
1282     /** 
1283      * <p>Checks if String contains a search String, handling <code>null</code>. 
1284      * This method uses {@link String#indexOf(String)}.</p> 
1285      * 
1286      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>false</code>.</p> 
1287      * 
1288      * <pre> 
1289      * StringUtils.contains(null, *)     = false 
1290      * StringUtils.contains(*, null)     = false 
1291      * StringUtils.contains("", "")      = true 
1292      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "")   = true 
1293      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "a")  = true 
1294      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "z")  = false 
1295      * </pre> 
1296      * 
1297      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1298      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
1299      * @return true if the String contains the search String, 
1300      *  false if not or <code>null</code> string input 
1301      * @since 2.0 
1302      */ 
1303     public static boolean contains(String str, String searchStr) { 
1304         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
1305             return false; 
1306         } 
1307         return str.indexOf(searchStr) >= 0; 
1308     } 
1309  
1310     /** 
1311      * <p>Checks if String contains a search String irrespective of case, 
1312      * handling <code>null</code>. Case-insensitivity is defined as by 
1313      * {@link String#equalsIgnoreCase(String)}. 
1314      * 
1315      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>false</code>.</p> 
1316      * 
1317      * <pre> 
1318      * StringUtils.contains(null, *) = false 
1319      * StringUtils.contains(*, null) = false 
1320      * StringUtils.contains("", "") = true 
1321      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "") = true 
1322      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "a") = true 
1323      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "z") = false 
1324      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "A") = true 
1325      * StringUtils.contains("abc", "Z") = false 
1326      * </pre> 
1327      * 
1328      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1329      * @param searchStr  the String to find, may be null 
1330      * @return true if the String contains the search String irrespective of 
1331      * case or false if not or <code>null</code> string input 
1332      */ 
1333     public static boolean containsIgnoreCase(String str, String searchStr) { 
1334         if (str == null || searchStr == null) { 
1335             return false; 
1336         } 
1337         int len = searchStr.length(); 
1338         int max = str.length() - len; 
1339         for (int i = 0; i <= max; i++) { 
1340             if (str.regionMatches(true, i, searchStr, 0, len)) { 
1341                 return true; 
1342             } 
1343         } 
1344         return false; 
1345     } 
1346  
1347     // IndexOfAny chars 
1348     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1349     /** 
1350      * <p>Search a CharSequence to find the first index of any 
1351      * character in the given set of characters.</p> 
1352      * 
1353      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1354      * A <code>null</code> or zero length search array will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1355      * 
1356      * <pre> 
1357      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(null, *)                = -1 
1358      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("", *)                  = -1 
1359      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, null)                = -1 
1360      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, [])                  = -1 
1361      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx",['z','a']) = 0 
1362      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx",['b','y']) = 3 
1363      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("aba", ['z'])           = -1 
1364      * </pre> 
1365      * 
1366      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1367      * @param searchChars  the chars to search for, may be null 
1368      * @return the index of any of the chars, -1 if no match or null input 
1369      * @since 2.0 
1370      */ 
1371     public static int indexOfAny(CharSequence cs, char[] searchChars) { 
1372         if (isEmpty(cs) || ArrayUtils.isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
1373             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1374         } 
1375         for (int i = 0; i < cs.length(); i++) { 
1376             char ch = cs.charAt(i); 
1377             for (int j = 0; j < searchChars.length; j++) { 
1378                 if (searchChars[j] == ch) { 
1379                     return i; 
1380                 } 
1381             } 
1382         } 
1383         return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1384     } 
1385  
1386     /** 
1387      * <p>Search a CharSequence to find the first index of any 
1388      * character in the given set of characters.</p> 
1389      * 
1390      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1391      * A <code>null</code> search string will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1392      * 
1393      * <pre> 
1394      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(null, *)            = -1 
1395      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("", *)              = -1 
1396      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, null)            = -1 
1397      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, "")              = -1 
1398      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", "za") = 0 
1399      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", "by") = 3 
1400      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("aba","z")          = -1 
1401      * </pre> 
1402      * 
1403      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1404      * @param searchChars  the chars to search for, may be null 
1405      * @return the index of any of the chars, -1 if no match or null input 
1406      * @since 2.0 
1407      */ 
1408     public static int indexOfAny(CharSequence cs, String searchChars) { 
1409         if (isEmpty(cs) || isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
1410             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1411         } 
1412         return indexOfAny(cs, searchChars.toCharArray()); 
1413     } 
1414  
1415     // ContainsAny 
1416     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1417     /** 
1418      * <p>Checks if the CharSequence contains any character in the given 
1419      * set of characters.</p> 
1420      * 
1421      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>false</code>. 
1422      * A <code>null</code> or zero length search array will return <code>false</code>.</p> 
1423      * 
1424      * <pre> 
1425      * StringUtils.containsAny(null, *)                = false 
1426      * StringUtils.containsAny("", *)                  = false 
1427      * StringUtils.containsAny(*, null)                = false 
1428      * StringUtils.containsAny(*, [])                  = false 
1429      * StringUtils.containsAny("zzabyycdxx",['z','a']) = true 
1430      * StringUtils.containsAny("zzabyycdxx",['b','y']) = true 
1431      * StringUtils.containsAny("aba", ['z'])           = false 
1432      * </pre> 
1433      * 
1434      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1435      * @param searchChars  the chars to search for, may be null 
1436      * @return the <code>true</code> if any of the chars are found, 
1437      * <code>false</code> if no match or null input 
1438      * @since 2.4 
1439      */ 
1440 	public static boolean containsAny(CharSequence cs, char[] searchChars) { 
1441 		if (isEmpty(cs) || ArrayUtils.isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
1442 			return false; 
1443 		} 
1444 		int csLength = cs.length(); 
1445 		int searchLength = searchChars.length; 
1446 		int csLastIndex = csLength - 1; 
1447 		int searchLastIndex = searchLength - 1; 
1448 		for (int i = 0; i < csLength; i++) { 
1449 			char ch = cs.charAt(i); 
1450 			for (int j = 0; j < searchLength; j++) { 
1451 				if (searchChars[j] == ch) { 
1452 					if (i < csLastIndex && j < searchLastIndex && ch >= Character.MIN_HIGH_SURROGATE && ch <= Character.MAX_HIGH_SURROGATE) { 
1453 						// ch is a supplementary character 
1454 						if (searchChars[j + 1] == cs.charAt(i + 1)) { 
1455 							return true; 
1456 						} 
1457 					} else { 
1458 						// ch is in the Basic Multilingual Plane 
1459 						return true; 
1460 					} 
1461 				} 
1462 			} 
1463 		} 
1464 		return false; 
1465 	} 
1466  
1467     /** 
1468      * <p> 
1469      * Checks if the CharSequence contains any character in the given set of characters. 
1470      * </p> 
1471      *  
1472      * <p> 
1473      * A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>false</code>. A <code>null</code> search CharSequence will return 
1474      * <code>false</code>. 
1475      * </p> 
1476      *  
1477      * <pre> 
1478      * StringUtils.containsAny(null, *)            = false 
1479      * StringUtils.containsAny("", *)              = false 
1480      * StringUtils.containsAny(*, null)            = false 
1481      * StringUtils.containsAny(*, "")              = false 
1482      * StringUtils.containsAny("zzabyycdxx", "za") = true 
1483      * StringUtils.containsAny("zzabyycdxx", "by") = true 
1484      * StringUtils.containsAny("aba","z")          = false 
1485      * </pre> 
1486      *  
1487      * @param cs 
1488      *            the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1489      * @param searchChars 
1490      *            the chars to search for, may be null 
1491      * @return the <code>true</code> if any of the chars are found, <code>false</code> if no match or null input 
1492      * @since 2.4 
1493      */ 
1494     public static boolean containsAny(CharSequence cs, String searchChars) { 
1495         if (searchChars == null) { 
1496             return false; 
1497         } 
1498         return containsAny(cs, searchChars.toCharArray()); 
1499     } 
1500  
1501     // IndexOfAnyBut chars 
1502     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1503     /** 
1504      * <p>Searches a CharSequence to find the first index of any 
1505      * character not in the given set of characters.</p> 
1506      * 
1507      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>-1</code>. 
1508      * A <code>null</code> or zero length search array will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1509      * 
1510      * <pre> 
1511      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(null, *)           = -1 
1512      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("", *)             = -1 
1513      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(*, null)           = -1 
1514      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(*, [])             = -1 
1515      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("zzabyycdxx",'za') = 3 
1516      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("zzabyycdxx", '')  = 0 
1517      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("aba", 'ab')       = -1 
1518      * </pre> 
1519      * 
1520      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1521      * @param searchChars  the chars to search for, may be null 
1522      * @return the index of any of the chars, -1 if no match or null input 
1523      * @since 2.0 
1524      */ 
1525     public static int indexOfAnyBut(CharSequence cs, char[] searchChars) { 
1526         if (isEmpty(cs) || ArrayUtils.isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
1527             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1528         } 
1529         outer : for (int i = 0; i < cs.length(); i++) { 
1530             char ch = cs.charAt(i); 
1531             for (int j = 0; j < searchChars.length; j++) { 
1532                 if (searchChars[j] == ch) { 
1533                     continue outer; 
1534                 } 
1535             } 
1536             return i; 
1537         } 
1538         return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1539     } 
1540  
1541     /** 
1542      * <p>Search a String to find the first index of any 
1543      * character not in the given set of characters.</p> 
1544      * 
1545      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1546      * A <code>null</code> search string will return <code>-1</code>.</p> 
1547      * 
1548      * <pre> 
1549      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(null, *)            = -1 
1550      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("", *)              = -1 
1551      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(*, null)            = -1 
1552      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut(*, "")              = -1 
1553      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("zzabyycdxx", "za") = 3 
1554      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("zzabyycdxx", "")   = 0 
1555      * StringUtils.indexOfAnyBut("aba","ab")         = -1 
1556      * </pre> 
1557      * 
1558      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1559      * @param searchChars  the chars to search for, may be null 
1560      * @return the index of any of the chars, -1 if no match or null input 
1561      * @since 2.0 
1562      */ 
1563     public static int indexOfAnyBut(String str, String searchChars) { 
1564         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
1565             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1566         } 
1567         for (int i = 0; i < str.length(); i++) { 
1568             if (searchChars.indexOf(str.charAt(i)) < 0) { 
1569                 return i; 
1570             } 
1571         } 
1572         return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1573     } 
1574  
1575     // ContainsOnly 
1576     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1577     /** 
1578      * <p>Checks if the CharSequence contains only certain characters.</p> 
1579      * 
1580      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>false</code>. 
1581      * A <code>null</code> valid character array will return <code>false</code>. 
1582      * An empty CharSequence (length()=0) always returns <code>true</code>.</p> 
1583      * 
1584      * <pre> 
1585      * StringUtils.containsOnly(null, *)       = false 
1586      * StringUtils.containsOnly(*, null)       = false 
1587      * StringUtils.containsOnly("", *)         = true 
1588      * StringUtils.containsOnly("ab", '')      = false 
1589      * StringUtils.containsOnly("abab", 'abc') = true 
1590      * StringUtils.containsOnly("ab1", 'abc')  = false 
1591      * StringUtils.containsOnly("abz", 'abc')  = false 
1592      * </pre> 
1593      * 
1594      * @param cs  the String to check, may be null 
1595      * @param valid  an array of valid chars, may be null 
1596      * @return true if it only contains valid chars and is non-null 
1597      */ 
1598     public static boolean containsOnly(CharSequence cs, char[] valid) { 
1599         // All these pre-checks are to maintain API with an older version 
1600         if (valid == null || cs == null) { 
1601             return false; 
1602         } 
1603         if (cs.length() == 0) { 
1604             return true; 
1605         } 
1606         if (valid.length == 0) { 
1607             return false; 
1608         } 
1609         return indexOfAnyBut(cs, valid) == INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1610     } 
1611  
1612     /** 
1613      * <p>Checks if the CharSequence contains only certain characters.</p> 
1614      * 
1615      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>false</code>. 
1616      * A <code>null</code> valid character String will return <code>false</code>. 
1617      * An empty String (length()=0) always returns <code>true</code>.</p> 
1618      * 
1619      * <pre> 
1620      * StringUtils.containsOnly(null, *)       = false 
1621      * StringUtils.containsOnly(*, null)       = false 
1622      * StringUtils.containsOnly("", *)         = true 
1623      * StringUtils.containsOnly("ab", "")      = false 
1624      * StringUtils.containsOnly("abab", "abc") = true 
1625      * StringUtils.containsOnly("ab1", "abc")  = false 
1626      * StringUtils.containsOnly("abz", "abc")  = false 
1627      * </pre> 
1628      * 
1629      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1630      * @param validChars  a String of valid chars, may be null 
1631      * @return true if it only contains valid chars and is non-null 
1632      * @since 2.0 
1633      */ 
1634     public static boolean containsOnly(CharSequence cs, String validChars) { 
1635         if (cs == null || validChars == null) { 
1636             return false; 
1637         } 
1638         return containsOnly(cs, validChars.toCharArray()); 
1639     } 
1640  
1641     // ContainsNone 
1642     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1643     /** 
1644      * <p>Checks that the CharSequence does not contain certain characters.</p> 
1645      * 
1646      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>true</code>. 
1647      * A <code>null</code> invalid character array will return <code>true</code>. 
1648      * An empty CharSequence (length()=0) always returns true.</p> 
1649      * 
1650      * <pre> 
1651      * StringUtils.containsNone(null, *)       = true 
1652      * StringUtils.containsNone(*, null)       = true 
1653      * StringUtils.containsNone("", *)         = true 
1654      * StringUtils.containsNone("ab", '')      = true 
1655      * StringUtils.containsNone("abab", 'xyz') = true 
1656      * StringUtils.containsNone("ab1", 'xyz')  = true 
1657      * StringUtils.containsNone("abz", 'xyz')  = false 
1658      * </pre> 
1659      * 
1660      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1661      * @param invalidChars  an array of invalid chars, may be null 
1662      * @return true if it contains none of the invalid chars, or is null 
1663      * @since 2.0 
1664      */ 
1665     public static boolean containsNone(CharSequence cs, char[] invalidChars) { 
1666         if (cs == null || invalidChars == null) { 
1667             return true; 
1668         } 
1669         int strSize = cs.length(); 
1670         int validSize = invalidChars.length; 
1671         for (int i = 0; i < strSize; i++) { 
1672             char ch = cs.charAt(i); 
1673             for (int j = 0; j < validSize; j++) { 
1674                 if (invalidChars[j] == ch) { 
1675                     return false; 
1676                 } 
1677             } 
1678         } 
1679         return true; 
1680     } 
1681  
1682     /** 
1683      * <p>Checks that the CharSequence does not contain certain characters.</p> 
1684      * 
1685      * <p>A <code>null</code> CharSequence will return <code>true</code>. 
1686      * A <code>null</code> invalid character array will return <code>true</code>. 
1687      * An empty String ("") always returns true.</p> 
1688      * 
1689      * <pre> 
1690      * StringUtils.containsNone(null, *)       = true 
1691      * StringUtils.containsNone(*, null)       = true 
1692      * StringUtils.containsNone("", *)         = true 
1693      * StringUtils.containsNone("ab", "")      = true 
1694      * StringUtils.containsNone("abab", "xyz") = true 
1695      * StringUtils.containsNone("ab1", "xyz")  = true 
1696      * StringUtils.containsNone("abz", "xyz")  = false 
1697      * </pre> 
1698      * 
1699      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
1700      * @param invalidChars  a String of invalid chars, may be null 
1701      * @return true if it contains none of the invalid chars, or is null 
1702      * @since 2.0 
1703      */ 
1704     public static boolean containsNone(CharSequence cs, String invalidChars) { 
1705         if (cs == null || invalidChars == null) { 
1706             return true; 
1707         } 
1708         return containsNone(cs, invalidChars.toCharArray()); 
1709     } 
1710  
1711     // IndexOfAny strings 
1712     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1713     /** 
1714      * <p>Find the first index of any of a set of potential substrings.</p> 
1715      * 
1716      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1717      * A <code>null</code> or zero length search array will return <code>-1</code>. 
1718      * A <code>null</code> search array entry will be ignored, but a search 
1719      * array containing "" will return <code>0</code> if <code>str</code> is not 
1720      * null. This method uses {@link String#indexOf(String)}.</p> 
1721      * 
1722      * <pre> 
1723      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(null, *)                     = -1 
1724      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, null)                     = -1 
1725      * StringUtils.indexOfAny(*, [])                       = -1 
1726      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["ab","cd"])   = 2 
1727      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["cd","ab"])   = 2 
1728      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["mn","op"])   = -1 
1729      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["zab","aby"]) = 1 
1730      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", [""])          = 0 
1731      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("", [""])                    = 0 
1732      * StringUtils.indexOfAny("", ["a"])                   = -1 
1733      * </pre> 
1734      * 
1735      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1736      * @param searchStrs  the Strings to search for, may be null 
1737      * @return the first index of any of the searchStrs in str, -1 if no match 
1738      */ 
1739     public static int indexOfAny(String str, String[] searchStrs) { 
1740         if (str == null || searchStrs == null) { 
1741             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1742         } 
1743         int sz = searchStrs.length; 
1744  
1745         // String's can't have a MAX_VALUEth index. 
1746         int ret = Integer.MAX_VALUE; 
1747  
1748         int tmp = 0; 
1749         for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) { 
1750             String search = searchStrs[i]; 
1751             if (search == null) { 
1752                 continue; 
1753             } 
1754             tmp = str.indexOf(search); 
1755             if (tmp == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
1756                 continue; 
1757             } 
1758  
1759             if (tmp < ret) { 
1760                 ret = tmp; 
1761             } 
1762         } 
1763  
1764         return (ret == Integer.MAX_VALUE) ? INDEX_NOT_FOUND : ret; 
1765     } 
1766  
1767     /** 
1768      * <p>Find the latest index of any of a set of potential substrings.</p> 
1769      * 
1770      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>-1</code>. 
1771      * A <code>null</code> search array will return <code>-1</code>. 
1772      * A <code>null</code> or zero length search array entry will be ignored, 
1773      * but a search array containing "" will return the length of <code>str</code> 
1774      * if <code>str</code> is not null. This method uses {@link String#indexOf(String)}</p> 
1775      * 
1776      * <pre> 
1777      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny(null, *)                   = -1 
1778      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny(*, null)                   = -1 
1779      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny(*, [])                     = -1 
1780      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny(*, [null])                 = -1 
1781      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["ab","cd"]) = 6 
1782      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["cd","ab"]) = 6 
1783      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["mn","op"]) = -1 
1784      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["mn","op"]) = -1 
1785      * StringUtils.lastIndexOfAny("zzabyycdxx", ["mn",""])   = 10 
1786      * </pre> 
1787      * 
1788      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
1789      * @param searchStrs  the Strings to search for, may be null 
1790      * @return the last index of any of the Strings, -1 if no match 
1791      */ 
1792     public static int lastIndexOfAny(String str, String[] searchStrs) { 
1793         if (str == null || searchStrs == null) { 
1794             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1795         } 
1796         int sz = searchStrs.length; 
1797         int ret = INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
1798         int tmp = 0; 
1799         for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) { 
1800             String search = searchStrs[i]; 
1801             if (search == null) { 
1802                 continue; 
1803             } 
1804             tmp = str.lastIndexOf(search); 
1805             if (tmp > ret) { 
1806                 ret = tmp; 
1807             } 
1808         } 
1809         return ret; 
1810     } 
1811  
1812     // Substring 
1813     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1814     /** 
1815      * <p>Gets a substring from the specified String avoiding exceptions.</p> 
1816      * 
1817      * <p>A negative start position can be used to start <code>n</code> 
1818      * characters from the end of the String.</p> 
1819      * 
1820      * <p>A <code>null</code> String will return <code>null</code>. 
1821      * An empty ("") String will return "".</p> 
1822      * 
1823      * <pre> 
1824      * StringUtils.substring(null, *)   = null 
1825      * StringUtils.substring("", *)     = "" 
1826      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 0)  = "abc" 
1827      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 2)  = "c" 
1828      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 4)  = "" 
1829      * StringUtils.substring("abc", -2) = "bc" 
1830      * StringUtils.substring("abc", -4) = "abc" 
1831      * </pre> 
1832      * 
1833      * @param str  the String to get the substring from, may be null 
1834      * @param start  the position to start from, negative means 
1835      *  count back from the end of the String by this many characters 
1836      * @return substring from start position, <code>null</code> if null String input 
1837      */ 
1838     public static String substring(String str, int start) { 
1839         if (str == null) { 
1840             return null; 
1841         } 
1842  
1843         // handle negatives, which means last n characters 
1844         if (start < 0) { 
1845             start = str.length() + start; // remember start is negative 
1846         } 
1847  
1848         if (start < 0) { 
1849             start = 0; 
1850         } 
1851         if (start > str.length()) { 
1852             return EMPTY; 
1853         } 
1854  
1855         return str.substring(start); 
1856     } 
1857  
1858     /** 
1859      * <p>Gets a substring from the specified String avoiding exceptions.</p> 
1860      * 
1861      * <p>A negative start position can be used to start/end <code>n</code> 
1862      * characters from the end of the String.</p> 
1863      * 
1864      * <p>The returned substring starts with the character in the <code>start</code> 
1865      * position and ends before the <code>end</code> position. All position counting is 
1866      * zero-based -- i.e., to start at the beginning of the string use 
1867      * <code>start = 0</code>. Negative start and end positions can be used to 
1868      * specify offsets relative to the end of the String.</p> 
1869      * 
1870      * <p>If <code>start</code> is not strictly to the left of <code>end</code>, "" 
1871      * is returned.</p> 
1872      * 
1873      * <pre> 
1874      * StringUtils.substring(null, *, *)    = null 
1875      * StringUtils.substring("", * ,  *)    = ""; 
1876      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 0, 2)   = "ab" 
1877      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 2, 0)   = "" 
1878      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 2, 4)   = "c" 
1879      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 4, 6)   = "" 
1880      * StringUtils.substring("abc", 2, 2)   = "" 
1881      * StringUtils.substring("abc", -2, -1) = "b" 
1882      * StringUtils.substring("abc", -4, 2)  = "ab" 
1883      * </pre> 
1884      * 
1885      * @param str  the String to get the substring from, may be null 
1886      * @param start  the position to start from, negative means 
1887      *  count back from the end of the String by this many characters 
1888      * @param end  the position to end at (exclusive), negative means 
1889      *  count back from the end of the String by this many characters 
1890      * @return substring from start position to end positon, 
1891      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
1892      */ 
1893     public static String substring(String str, int start, int end) { 
1894         if (str == null) { 
1895             return null; 
1896         } 
1897  
1898         // handle negatives 
1899         if (end < 0) { 
1900             end = str.length() + end; // remember end is negative 
1901         } 
1902         if (start < 0) { 
1903             start = str.length() + start; // remember start is negative 
1904         } 
1905  
1906         // check length next 
1907         if (end > str.length()) { 
1908             end = str.length(); 
1909         } 
1910  
1911         // if start is greater than end, return "" 
1912         if (start > end) { 
1913             return EMPTY; 
1914         } 
1915  
1916         if (start < 0) { 
1917             start = 0; 
1918         } 
1919         if (end < 0) { 
1920             end = 0; 
1921         } 
1922  
1923         return str.substring(start, end); 
1924     } 
1925  
1926     // Left/Right/Mid 
1927     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
1928     /** 
1929      * <p>Gets the leftmost <code>len</code> characters of a String.</p> 
1930      * 
1931      * <p>If <code>len</code> characters are not available, or the 
1932      * String is <code>null</code>, the String will be returned without 
1933      * an exception. An exception is thrown if len is negative.</p> 
1934      * 
1935      * <pre> 
1936      * StringUtils.left(null, *)    = null 
1937      * StringUtils.left(*, -ve)     = "" 
1938      * StringUtils.left("", *)      = "" 
1939      * StringUtils.left("abc", 0)   = "" 
1940      * StringUtils.left("abc", 2)   = "ab" 
1941      * StringUtils.left("abc", 4)   = "abc" 
1942      * </pre> 
1943      * 
1944      * @param str  the String to get the leftmost characters from, may be null 
1945      * @param len  the length of the required String, must be zero or positive 
1946      * @return the leftmost characters, <code>null</code> if null String input 
1947      */ 
1948     public static String left(String str, int len) { 
1949         if (str == null) { 
1950             return null; 
1951         } 
1952         if (len < 0) { 
1953             return EMPTY; 
1954         } 
1955         if (str.length() <= len) { 
1956             return str; 
1957         } 
1958         return str.substring(0, len); 
1959     } 
1960  
1961     /** 
1962      * <p>Gets the rightmost <code>len</code> characters of a String.</p> 
1963      * 
1964      * <p>If <code>len</code> characters are not available, or the String 
1965      * is <code>null</code>, the String will be returned without an 
1966      * an exception. An exception is thrown if len is negative.</p> 
1967      * 
1968      * <pre> 
1969      * StringUtils.right(null, *)    = null 
1970      * StringUtils.right(*, -ve)     = "" 
1971      * StringUtils.right("", *)      = "" 
1972      * StringUtils.right("abc", 0)   = "" 
1973      * StringUtils.right("abc", 2)   = "bc" 
1974      * StringUtils.right("abc", 4)   = "abc" 
1975      * </pre> 
1976      * 
1977      * @param str  the String to get the rightmost characters from, may be null 
1978      * @param len  the length of the required String, must be zero or positive 
1979      * @return the rightmost characters, <code>null</code> if null String input 
1980      */ 
1981     public static String right(String str, int len) { 
1982         if (str == null) { 
1983             return null; 
1984         } 
1985         if (len < 0) { 
1986             return EMPTY; 
1987         } 
1988         if (str.length() <= len) { 
1989             return str; 
1990         } 
1991         return str.substring(str.length() - len); 
1992     } 
1993  
1994     /** 
1995      * <p>Gets <code>len</code> characters from the middle of a String.</p> 
1996      * 
1997      * <p>If <code>len</code> characters are not available, the remainder 
1998      * of the String will be returned without an exception. If the 
1999      * String is <code>null</code>, <code>null</code> will be returned. 
2000      * An exception is thrown if len is negative.</p> 
2001      * 
2002      * <pre> 
2003      * StringUtils.mid(null, *, *)    = null 
2004      * StringUtils.mid(*, *, -ve)     = "" 
2005      * StringUtils.mid("", 0, *)      = "" 
2006      * StringUtils.mid("abc", 0, 2)   = "ab" 
2007      * StringUtils.mid("abc", 0, 4)   = "abc" 
2008      * StringUtils.mid("abc", 2, 4)   = "c" 
2009      * StringUtils.mid("abc", 4, 2)   = "" 
2010      * StringUtils.mid("abc", -2, 2)  = "ab" 
2011      * </pre> 
2012      * 
2013      * @param str  the String to get the characters from, may be null 
2014      * @param pos  the position to start from, negative treated as zero 
2015      * @param len  the length of the required String, must be zero or positive 
2016      * @return the middle characters, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2017      */ 
2018     public static String mid(String str, int pos, int len) { 
2019         if (str == null) { 
2020             return null; 
2021         } 
2022         if (len < 0 || pos > str.length()) { 
2023             return EMPTY; 
2024         } 
2025         if (pos < 0) { 
2026             pos = 0; 
2027         } 
2028         if (str.length() <= (pos + len)) { 
2029             return str.substring(pos); 
2030         } 
2031         return str.substring(pos, pos + len); 
2032     } 
2033  
2034     // SubStringAfter/SubStringBefore 
2035     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
2036     /** 
2037      * <p>Gets the substring before the first occurrence of a separator. 
2038      * The separator is not returned.</p> 
2039      * 
2040      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input will return <code>null</code>. 
2041      * An empty ("") string input will return the empty string. 
2042      * A <code>null</code> separator will return the input string.</p> 
2043      * 
2044      * <p>If nothing is found, the string input is returned.</p> 
2045      * 
2046      * <pre> 
2047      * StringUtils.substringBefore(null, *)      = null 
2048      * StringUtils.substringBefore("", *)        = "" 
2049      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abc", "a")   = "" 
2050      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abcba", "b") = "a" 
2051      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abc", "c")   = "ab" 
2052      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abc", "d")   = "abc" 
2053      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abc", "")    = "" 
2054      * StringUtils.substringBefore("abc", null)  = "abc" 
2055      * </pre> 
2056      * 
2057      * @param str  the String to get a substring from, may be null 
2058      * @param separator  the String to search for, may be null 
2059      * @return the substring before the first occurrence of the separator, 
2060      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
2061      * @since 2.0 
2062      */ 
2063     public static String substringBefore(String str, String separator) { 
2064         if (isEmpty(str) || separator == null) { 
2065             return str; 
2066         } 
2067         if (separator.length() == 0) { 
2068             return EMPTY; 
2069         } 
2070         int pos = str.indexOf(separator); 
2071         if (pos == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
2072             return str; 
2073         } 
2074         return str.substring(0, pos); 
2075     } 
2076  
2077     /** 
2078      * <p>Gets the substring after the first occurrence of a separator. 
2079      * The separator is not returned.</p> 
2080      * 
2081      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input will return <code>null</code>. 
2082      * An empty ("") string input will return the empty string. 
2083      * A <code>null</code> separator will return the empty string if the 
2084      * input string is not <code>null</code>.</p> 
2085      * 
2086      * <p>If nothing is found, the empty string is returned.</p> 
2087      * 
2088      * <pre> 
2089      * StringUtils.substringAfter(null, *)      = null 
2090      * StringUtils.substringAfter("", *)        = "" 
2091      * StringUtils.substringAfter(*, null)      = "" 
2092      * StringUtils.substringAfter("abc", "a")   = "bc" 
2093      * StringUtils.substringAfter("abcba", "b") = "cba" 
2094      * StringUtils.substringAfter("abc", "c")   = "" 
2095      * StringUtils.substringAfter("abc", "d")   = "" 
2096      * StringUtils.substringAfter("abc", "")    = "abc" 
2097      * </pre> 
2098      * 
2099      * @param str  the String to get a substring from, may be null 
2100      * @param separator  the String to search for, may be null 
2101      * @return the substring after the first occurrence of the separator, 
2102      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
2103      * @since 2.0 
2104      */ 
2105     public static String substringAfter(String str, String separator) { 
2106         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
2107             return str; 
2108         } 
2109         if (separator == null) { 
2110             return EMPTY; 
2111         } 
2112         int pos = str.indexOf(separator); 
2113         if (pos == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
2114             return EMPTY; 
2115         } 
2116         return str.substring(pos + separator.length()); 
2117     } 
2118  
2119     /** 
2120      * <p>Gets the substring before the last occurrence of a separator. 
2121      * The separator is not returned.</p> 
2122      * 
2123      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input will return <code>null</code>. 
2124      * An empty ("") string input will return the empty string. 
2125      * An empty or <code>null</code> separator will return the input string.</p> 
2126      * 
2127      * <p>If nothing is found, the string input is returned.</p> 
2128      * 
2129      * <pre> 
2130      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast(null, *)      = null 
2131      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("", *)        = "" 
2132      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("abcba", "b") = "abc" 
2133      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("abc", "c")   = "ab" 
2134      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("a", "a")     = "" 
2135      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("a", "z")     = "a" 
2136      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("a", null)    = "a" 
2137      * StringUtils.substringBeforeLast("a", "")      = "a" 
2138      * </pre> 
2139      * 
2140      * @param str  the String to get a substring from, may be null 
2141      * @param separator  the String to search for, may be null 
2142      * @return the substring before the last occurrence of the separator, 
2143      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
2144      * @since 2.0 
2145      */ 
2146     public static String substringBeforeLast(String str, String separator) { 
2147         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(separator)) { 
2148             return str; 
2149         } 
2150         int pos = str.lastIndexOf(separator); 
2151         if (pos == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
2152             return str; 
2153         } 
2154         return str.substring(0, pos); 
2155     } 
2156  
2157     /** 
2158      * <p>Gets the substring after the last occurrence of a separator. 
2159      * The separator is not returned.</p> 
2160      * 
2161      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input will return <code>null</code>. 
2162      * An empty ("") string input will return the empty string. 
2163      * An empty or <code>null</code> separator will return the empty string if 
2164      * the input string is not <code>null</code>.</p> 
2165      * 
2166      * <p>If nothing is found, the empty string is returned.</p> 
2167      * 
2168      * <pre> 
2169      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast(null, *)      = null 
2170      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("", *)        = "" 
2171      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast(*, "")        = "" 
2172      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast(*, null)      = "" 
2173      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("abc", "a")   = "bc" 
2174      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("abcba", "b") = "a" 
2175      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("abc", "c")   = "" 
2176      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("a", "a")     = "" 
2177      * StringUtils.substringAfterLast("a", "z")     = "" 
2178      * </pre> 
2179      * 
2180      * @param str  the String to get a substring from, may be null 
2181      * @param separator  the String to search for, may be null 
2182      * @return the substring after the last occurrence of the separator, 
2183      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
2184      * @since 2.0 
2185      */ 
2186     public static String substringAfterLast(String str, String separator) { 
2187         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
2188             return str; 
2189         } 
2190         if (isEmpty(separator)) { 
2191             return EMPTY; 
2192         } 
2193         int pos = str.lastIndexOf(separator); 
2194         if (pos == INDEX_NOT_FOUND || pos == (str.length() - separator.length())) { 
2195             return EMPTY; 
2196         } 
2197         return str.substring(pos + separator.length()); 
2198     } 
2199  
2200     // Substring between 
2201     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
2202     /** 
2203      * <p>Gets the String that is nested in between two instances of the 
2204      * same String.</p> 
2205      * 
2206      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2207      * A <code>null</code> tag returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
2208      * 
2209      * <pre> 
2210      * StringUtils.substringBetween(null, *)            = null 
2211      * StringUtils.substringBetween("", "")             = "" 
2212      * StringUtils.substringBetween("", "tag")          = null 
2213      * StringUtils.substringBetween("tagabctag", null)  = null 
2214      * StringUtils.substringBetween("tagabctag", "")    = "" 
2215      * StringUtils.substringBetween("tagabctag", "tag") = "abc" 
2216      * </pre> 
2217      * 
2218      * @param str  the String containing the substring, may be null 
2219      * @param tag  the String before and after the substring, may be null 
2220      * @return the substring, <code>null</code> if no match 
2221      * @since 2.0 
2222      */ 
2223     public static String substringBetween(String str, String tag) { 
2224         return substringBetween(str, tag, tag); 
2225     } 
2226  
2227     /** 
2228      * <p>Gets the String that is nested in between two Strings. 
2229      * Only the first match is returned.</p> 
2230      * 
2231      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2232      * A <code>null</code> open/close returns <code>null</code> (no match). 
2233      * An empty ("") open and close returns an empty string.</p> 
2234      * 
2235      * <pre> 
2236      * StringUtils.substringBetween("wx[b]yz", "[", "]") = "b" 
2237      * StringUtils.substringBetween(null, *, *)          = null 
2238      * StringUtils.substringBetween(*, null, *)          = null 
2239      * StringUtils.substringBetween(*, *, null)          = null 
2240      * StringUtils.substringBetween("", "", "")          = "" 
2241      * StringUtils.substringBetween("", "", "]")         = null 
2242      * StringUtils.substringBetween("", "[", "]")        = null 
2243      * StringUtils.substringBetween("yabcz", "", "")     = "" 
2244      * StringUtils.substringBetween("yabcz", "y", "z")   = "abc" 
2245      * StringUtils.substringBetween("yabczyabcz", "y", "z")   = "abc" 
2246      * </pre> 
2247      * 
2248      * @param str  the String containing the substring, may be null 
2249      * @param open  the String before the substring, may be null 
2250      * @param close  the String after the substring, may be null 
2251      * @return the substring, <code>null</code> if no match 
2252      * @since 2.0 
2253      */ 
2254     public static String substringBetween(String str, String open, String close) { 
2255         if (str == null || open == null || close == null) { 
2256             return null; 
2257         } 
2258         int start = str.indexOf(open); 
2259         if (start != INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
2260             int end = str.indexOf(close, start + open.length()); 
2261             if (end != INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
2262                 return str.substring(start + open.length(), end); 
2263             } 
2264         } 
2265         return null; 
2266     } 
2267  
2268     /** 
2269      * <p>Searches a String for substrings delimited by a start and end tag, 
2270      * returning all matching substrings in an array.</p> 
2271      * 
2272      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2273      * A <code>null</code> open/close returns <code>null</code> (no match). 
2274      * An empty ("") open/close returns <code>null</code> (no match).</p> 
2275      * 
2276      * <pre> 
2277      * StringUtils.substringsBetween("[a][b][c]", "[", "]") = ["a","b","c"] 
2278      * StringUtils.substringsBetween(null, *, *)            = null 
2279      * StringUtils.substringsBetween(*, null, *)            = null 
2280      * StringUtils.substringsBetween(*, *, null)            = null 
2281      * StringUtils.substringsBetween("", "[", "]")          = [] 
2282      * </pre> 
2283      * 
2284      * @param str  the String containing the substrings, null returns null, empty returns empty 
2285      * @param open  the String identifying the start of the substring, empty returns null 
2286      * @param close  the String identifying the end of the substring, empty returns null 
2287      * @return a String Array of substrings, or <code>null</code> if no match 
2288      * @since 2.3 
2289      */ 
2290     public static String[] substringsBetween(String str, String open, String close) { 
2291         if (str == null || isEmpty(open) || isEmpty(close)) { 
2292             return null; 
2293         } 
2294         int strLen = str.length(); 
2295         if (strLen == 0) { 
2296             return ArrayUtils.EMPTY_STRING_ARRAY; 
2297         } 
2298         int closeLen = close.length(); 
2299         int openLen = open.length(); 
2300         List<String> list = new ArrayList<String>(); 
2301         int pos = 0; 
2302         while (pos < (strLen - closeLen)) { 
2303             int start = str.indexOf(open, pos); 
2304             if (start < 0) { 
2305                 break; 
2306             } 
2307             start += openLen; 
2308             int end = str.indexOf(close, start); 
2309             if (end < 0) { 
2310                 break; 
2311             } 
2312             list.add(str.substring(start, end)); 
2313             pos = end + closeLen; 
2314         } 
2315         if (list.isEmpty()) { 
2316             return null; 
2317         }  
2318         return list.toArray(new String [list.size()]); 
2319     }

2326     /** 
2327      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, using whitespace as the 
2328      * separator. 
2329      * Whitespace is defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
2330      * 
2331      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2332      * Adjacent separators are treated as one separator. 
2333      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2334      * 
2335      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
2336      * 
2337      * <pre> 
2338      * StringUtils.split(null)       = null 
2339      * StringUtils.split("")         = [] 
2340      * StringUtils.split("abc def")  = ["abc", "def"] 
2341      * StringUtils.split("abc  def") = ["abc", "def"] 
2342      * StringUtils.split(" abc ")    = ["abc"] 
2343      * </pre> 
2344      * 
2345      * @param str  the String to parse, may be null 
2346      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2347      */ 
2348     public static String[] split(String str) { 
2349         return split(str, null, -1); 
2350     }

2352     /** 
2353      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, separator specified. 
2354      * This is an alternative to using StringTokenizer.</p> 
2355      * 
2356      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2357      * Adjacent separators are treated as one separator. 
2358      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2359      * 
2360      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
2361      * 
2362      * <pre> 
2363      * StringUtils.split(null, *)         = null 
2364      * StringUtils.split("", *)           = [] 
2365      * StringUtils.split("a.b.c", '.')    = ["a", "b", "c"] 
2366      * StringUtils.split("a..b.c", '.')   = ["a", "b", "c"] 
2367      * StringUtils.split("a:b:c", '.')    = ["a:b:c"] 
2368      * StringUtils.split("a b c", ' ')    = ["a", "b", "c"] 
2369      * </pre> 
2370      * 
2371      * @param str  the String to parse, may be null 
2372      * @param separatorChar  the character used as the delimiter 
2373      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2374      * @since 2.0 
2375      */ 
2376     public static String[] split(String str, char separatorChar) { 
2377         return splitWorker(str, separatorChar, false); 
2378     }

2385      * Adjacent separators are treated as one separator. 
2386      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2387      * 
2388      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2389      * A <code>null</code> separatorChars splits on whitespace.</p>

2395      * StringUtils.split("abc def", " ")  = ["abc", "def"] 
2396      * StringUtils.split("abc  def", " ") = ["abc", "def"]

2409     /** 
2410      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array with a maximum length, 
2411      * separators specified.</p> 
2412      * 
2413      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2414      * Adjacent separators are treated as one separator.</p> 
2415      * 
2416      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2417      * A <code>null</code> separatorChars splits on whitespace.</p> 
2418      * 
2419      * <p>If more than <code>max</code> delimited substrings are found, the last 
2420      * returned string includes all characters after the first <code>max - 1</code> 
2421      * returned strings (including separator characters).</p> 
2422      * 
2423      * <pre> 
2424      * StringUtils.split(null, *, *)            = null 
2425      * StringUtils.split("", *, *)              = []

2429      * StringUtils.split("ab:cd:ef", ":", 2)    = ["ab", "cd:ef"] 
2430      * </pre> 
2431      *

2434      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace

2436      *  array. A zero or negative value implies no limit 
2437      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2438      */ 
2439     public static String[] split(String str, String separatorChars, int max) { 
2440         return splitWorker(str, separatorChars, max, false); 
2441     }

2501     /** 
2502      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, separator string specified. </p> 
2503      * 
2504      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2505      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2506      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p>

2508      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2509      * A <code>null</code> separator splits on whitespace.</p>

2511      * <pre>

2514      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab de fg", null)      = ["ab", "de", "fg"] 
2515      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null)    = ["ab", "", "", "de", "fg"] 
2516      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef", ":")       = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2517      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab-!-cd-!-ef", "-!-") = ["ab", "cd", "ef"]

2527         return splitByWholeSeparatorWorker(str, separator, -1, true); 
2528     }
