2530     /** 
2531      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, separator string specified. 
2532      * Returns a maximum of <code>max</code> substrings.</p> 
2533      * 
2534      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2535      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2536      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2537      * 
2538      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2539      * A <code>null</code> separator splits on whitespace.</p> 
2540      * 
2541      * <pre> 
2542      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens(null, *, *)               = null 
2543      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("", *, *)                 = [] 
2544      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab de fg", null, 0)      = ["ab", "de", "fg"] 
2545      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null, 0)    = ["ab", "", "", "de", "fg"] 
2546      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef", ":", 2)       = ["ab", "cd:ef"] 
2547      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab-!-cd-!-ef", "-!-", 5) = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2548      * StringUtils.splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens("ab-!-cd-!-ef", "-!-", 2) = ["ab", "cd-!-ef"] 
2549      * </pre> 
2550      * 
2551      * @param str  the String to parse, may be null 
2552      * @param separator  String containing the String to be used as a delimiter, 
2553      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace 
2554      * @param max  the maximum number of elements to include in the returned 
2555      *  array. A zero or negative value implies no limit. 
2556      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String was input 
2557      * @since 2.4 
2558      */ 
2559     public static String[] splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens(String str, String separator, int max) { 
2560         return splitByWholeSeparatorWorker(str, separator, max, true); 
2561     } 
2562  
2563     /** 
2564      * Performs the logic for the <code>splitByWholeSeparatorPreserveAllTokens</code> methods. 
2565      * 
2566      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2567      * @param separator  String containing the String to be used as a delimiter, 
2568      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace 
2569      * @param max  the maximum number of elements to include in the returned 
2570      *  array. A zero or negative value implies no limit. 
2571      * @param preserveAllTokens if <code>true</code>, adjacent separators are 
2572      * treated as empty token separators; if <code>false</code>, adjacent 
2573      * separators are treated as one separator. 
2574      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2575      * @since 2.4 
2576      */ 
2577     private static String[] splitByWholeSeparatorWorker(String str, String separator, int max,  
2578                                                         boolean preserveAllTokens)  
2579     { 
2580         if (str == null) { 
2581             return null; 
2582         } 
2583  
2584         int len = str.length(); 
2585  
2586         if (len == 0) { 
2587             return ArrayUtils.EMPTY_STRING_ARRAY; 
2588         } 
2589  
2590         if ((separator == null) || (EMPTY.equals(separator))) { 
2591             // Split on whitespace. 
2592             return splitWorker(str, null, max, preserveAllTokens); 
2593         } 
2594  
2595         int separatorLength = separator.length(); 
2596  
2597         ArrayList<String> substrings = new ArrayList<String>(); 
2598         int numberOfSubstrings = 0; 
2599         int beg = 0; 
2600         int end = 0; 
2601         while (end < len) { 
2602             end = str.indexOf(separator, beg); 
2603  
2604             if (end > -1) { 
2605                 if (end > beg) { 
2606                     numberOfSubstrings += 1; 
2607  
2608                     if (numberOfSubstrings == max) { 
2609                         end = len; 
2610                         substrings.add(str.substring(beg)); 
2611                     } else { 
2612                         // The following is OK, because String.substring( beg, end ) excludes 
2613                         // the character at the position 'end'. 
2614                         substrings.add(str.substring(beg, end)); 
2615  
2616                         // Set the starting point for the next search. 
2617                         // The following is equivalent to beg = end + (separatorLength - 1) + 1, 
2618                         // which is the right calculation: 
2619                         beg = end + separatorLength; 
2620                     } 
2621                 } else { 
2622                     // We found a consecutive occurrence of the separator, so skip it. 
2623                     if (preserveAllTokens) { 
2624                         numberOfSubstrings += 1; 
2625                         if (numberOfSubstrings == max) { 
2626                             end = len; 
2627                             substrings.add(str.substring(beg)); 
2628                         } else { 
2629                             substrings.add(EMPTY); 
2630                         } 
2631                     } 
2632                     beg = end + separatorLength; 
2633                 } 
2634             } else { 
2635                 // String.substring( beg ) goes from 'beg' to the end of the String. 
2636                 substrings.add(str.substring(beg)); 
2637                 end = len; 
2638             } 
2639         } 
2640  
2641         return substrings.toArray(new String[substrings.size()]); 
2642     } 
2643  
2644     // ----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
2645     /** 
2646      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, using whitespace as the 
2647      * separator, preserving all tokens, including empty tokens created by  
2648      * adjacent separators. This is an alternative to using StringTokenizer. 
2649      * Whitespace is defined by {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
2650      * 
2651      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2652      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2653      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2654      * 
2655      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
2656      * 
2657      * <pre> 
2658      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(null)       = null 
2659      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("")         = [] 
2660      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("abc def")  = ["abc", "def"] 
2661      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("abc  def") = ["abc", "", "def"] 
2662      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(" abc ")    = ["", "abc", ""] 
2663      * </pre> 
2664      * 
2665      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2666      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2667      * @since 2.1 
2668      */ 
2669     public static String[] splitPreserveAllTokens(String str) { 
2670         return splitWorker(str, null, -1, true); 
2671     } 
2672  
2673     /** 
2674      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, separator specified, 
2675      * preserving all tokens, including empty tokens created by adjacent 
2676      * separators. This is an alternative to using StringTokenizer.</p> 
2677      * 
2678      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2679      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2680      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2681      * 
2682      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
2683      * 
2684      * <pre> 
2685      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(null, *)         = null 
2686      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("", *)           = [] 
2687      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a.b.c", '.')    = ["a", "b", "c"] 
2688      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a..b.c", '.')   = ["a", "", "b", "c"] 
2689      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a:b:c", '.')    = ["a:b:c"] 
2690      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a\tb\nc", null) = ["a", "b", "c"] 
2691      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a b c", ' ')    = ["a", "b", "c"] 
2692      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a b c ", ' ')   = ["a", "b", "c", ""] 
2693      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("a b c  ", ' ')   = ["a", "b", "c", "", ""] 
2694      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(" a b c", ' ')   = ["", a", "b", "c"] 
2695      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("  a b c", ' ')  = ["", "", a", "b", "c"] 
2696      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(" a b c ", ' ')  = ["", a", "b", "c", ""] 
2697      * </pre> 
2698      * 
2699      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2700      * @param separatorChar  the character used as the delimiter, 
2701      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace 
2702      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2703      * @since 2.1 
2704      */ 
2705     public static String[] splitPreserveAllTokens(String str, char separatorChar) { 
2706         return splitWorker(str, separatorChar, true); 
2707     } 
2708  
2709     /** 
2710      * Performs the logic for the <code>split</code> and  
2711      * <code>splitPreserveAllTokens</code> methods that do not return a 
2712      * maximum array length. 
2713      * 
2714      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2715      * @param separatorChar the separate character 
2716      * @param preserveAllTokens if <code>true</code>, adjacent separators are 
2717      * treated as empty token separators; if <code>false</code>, adjacent 
2718      * separators are treated as one separator. 
2719      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2720      */ 
2721     private static String[] splitWorker(String str, char separatorChar, boolean preserveAllTokens) { 
2722         // Performance tuned for 2.0 (JDK1.4) 
2723  
2724         if (str == null) { 
2725             return null; 
2726         } 
2727         int len = str.length(); 
2728         if (len == 0) { 
2729             return ArrayUtils.EMPTY_STRING_ARRAY; 
2730         } 
2731         List<String> list = new ArrayList<String>(); 
2732         int i = 0, start = 0; 
2733         boolean match = false; 
2734         boolean lastMatch = false; 
2735         while (i < len) { 
2736             if (str.charAt(i) == separatorChar) { 
2737                 if (match || preserveAllTokens) { 
2738                     list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2739                     match = false; 
2740                     lastMatch = true; 
2741                 } 
2742                 start = ++i; 
2743                 continue; 
2744             } 
2745             lastMatch = false; 
2746             match = true; 
2747             i++; 
2748         } 
2749         if (match || (preserveAllTokens && lastMatch)) { 
2750             list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2751         } 
2752         return list.toArray(new String[list.size()]); 
2753     } 
2754  
2755     /** 
2756      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array, separators specified,  
2757      * preserving all tokens, including empty tokens created by adjacent 
2758      * separators. This is an alternative to using StringTokenizer.</p> 
2759      * 
2760      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2761      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2762      * For more control over the split use the StrTokenizer class.</p> 
2763      * 
2764      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2765      * A <code>null</code> separatorChars splits on whitespace.</p> 
2766      * 
2767      * <pre> 
2768      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(null, *)           = null 
2769      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("", *)             = [] 
2770      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("abc def", null)   = ["abc", "def"] 
2771      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("abc def", " ")    = ["abc", "def"] 
2772      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("abc  def", " ")   = ["abc", "", def"] 
2773      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef", ":")   = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2774      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef:", ":")  = ["ab", "cd", "ef", ""] 
2775      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef::", ":") = ["ab", "cd", "ef", "", ""] 
2776      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab::cd:ef", ":")  = ["ab", "", cd", "ef"] 
2777      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(":cd:ef", ":")     = ["", cd", "ef"] 
2778      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("::cd:ef", ":")    = ["", "", cd", "ef"] 
2779      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(":cd:ef:", ":")    = ["", cd", "ef", ""] 
2780      * </pre> 
2781      * 
2782      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2783      * @param separatorChars  the characters used as the delimiters, 
2784      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace 
2785      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2786      * @since 2.1 
2787      */ 
2788     public static String[] splitPreserveAllTokens(String str, String separatorChars) { 
2789         return splitWorker(str, separatorChars, -1, true); 
2790     } 
2791  
2792     /** 
2793      * <p>Splits the provided text into an array with a maximum length, 
2794      * separators specified, preserving all tokens, including empty tokens  
2795      * created by adjacent separators.</p> 
2796      * 
2797      * <p>The separator is not included in the returned String array. 
2798      * Adjacent separators are treated as separators for empty tokens. 
2799      * Adjacent separators are treated as one separator.</p> 
2800      * 
2801      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>. 
2802      * A <code>null</code> separatorChars splits on whitespace.</p> 
2803      * 
2804      * <p>If more than <code>max</code> delimited substrings are found, the last 
2805      * returned string includes all characters after the first <code>max - 1</code> 
2806      * returned strings (including separator characters).</p> 
2807      * 
2808      * <pre> 
2809      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens(null, *, *)            = null 
2810      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("", *, *)              = [] 
2811      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab de fg", null, 0)   = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2812      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null, 0) = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2813      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef", ":", 0)    = ["ab", "cd", "ef"] 
2814      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab:cd:ef", ":", 2)    = ["ab", "cd:ef"] 
2815      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null, 2) = ["ab", "  de fg"] 
2816      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null, 3) = ["ab", "", " de fg"] 
2817      * StringUtils.splitPreserveAllTokens("ab   de fg", null, 4) = ["ab", "", "", "de fg"] 
2818      * </pre> 
2819      * 
2820      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2821      * @param separatorChars  the characters used as the delimiters, 
2822      *  <code>null</code> splits on whitespace 
2823      * @param max  the maximum number of elements to include in the 
2824      *  array. A zero or negative value implies no limit 
2825      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2826      * @since 2.1 
2827      */ 
2828     public static String[] splitPreserveAllTokens(String str, String separatorChars, int max) { 
2829         return splitWorker(str, separatorChars, max, true); 
2830     } 
2831  
2832     /** 
2833      * Performs the logic for the <code>split</code> and  
2834      * <code>splitPreserveAllTokens</code> methods that return a maximum array  
2835      * length. 
2836      * 
2837      * @param str  the String to parse, may be <code>null</code> 
2838      * @param separatorChars the separate character 
2839      * @param max  the maximum number of elements to include in the 
2840      *  array. A zero or negative value implies no limit. 
2841      * @param preserveAllTokens if <code>true</code>, adjacent separators are 
2842      * treated as empty token separators; if <code>false</code>, adjacent 
2843      * separators are treated as one separator. 
2844      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2845      */ 
2846     private static String[] splitWorker(String str, String separatorChars, int max, boolean preserveAllTokens) { 
2847         // Performance tuned for 2.0 (JDK1.4) 
2848         // Direct code is quicker than StringTokenizer. 
2849         // Also, StringTokenizer uses isSpace() not isWhitespace() 
2850  
2851         if (str == null) { 
2852             return null; 
2853         } 
2854         int len = str.length(); 
2855         if (len == 0) { 
2856             return ArrayUtils.EMPTY_STRING_ARRAY; 
2857         } 
2858         List<String> list = new ArrayList<String>(); 
2859         int sizePlus1 = 1; 
2860         int i = 0, start = 0; 
2861         boolean match = false; 
2862         boolean lastMatch = false; 
2863         if (separatorChars == null) { 
2864             // Null separator means use whitespace 
2865             while (i < len) { 
2866                 if (Character.isWhitespace(str.charAt(i))) { 
2867                     if (match || preserveAllTokens) { 
2868                         lastMatch = true; 
2869                         if (sizePlus1++ == max) { 
2870                             i = len; 
2871                             lastMatch = false; 
2872                         } 
2873                         list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2874                         match = false; 
2875                     } 
2876                     start = ++i; 
2877                     continue; 
2878                 } 
2879                 lastMatch = false; 
2880                 match = true; 
2881                 i++; 
2882             } 
2883         } else if (separatorChars.length() == 1) { 
2884             // Optimise 1 character case 
2885             char sep = separatorChars.charAt(0); 
2886             while (i < len) { 
2887                 if (str.charAt(i) == sep) { 
2888                     if (match || preserveAllTokens) { 
2889                         lastMatch = true; 
2890                         if (sizePlus1++ == max) { 
2891                             i = len; 
2892                             lastMatch = false; 
2893                         } 
2894                         list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2895                         match = false; 
2896                     } 
2897                     start = ++i; 
2898                     continue; 
2899                 } 
2900                 lastMatch = false; 
2901                 match = true; 
2902                 i++; 
2903             } 
2904         } else { 
2905             // standard case 
2906             while (i < len) { 
2907                 if (separatorChars.indexOf(str.charAt(i)) >= 0) { 
2908                     if (match || preserveAllTokens) { 
2909                         lastMatch = true; 
2910                         if (sizePlus1++ == max) { 
2911                             i = len; 
2912                             lastMatch = false; 
2913                         } 
2914                         list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2915                         match = false; 
2916                     } 
2917                     start = ++i; 
2918                     continue; 
2919                 } 
2920                 lastMatch = false; 
2921                 match = true; 
2922                 i++; 
2923             } 
2924         } 
2925         if (match || (preserveAllTokens && lastMatch)) { 
2926             list.add(str.substring(start, i)); 
2927         } 
2928         return list.toArray(new String[list.size()]); 
2929     } 
2930  
2931     /** 
2932      * <p>Splits a String by Character type as returned by 
2933      * <code>java.lang.Character.getType(char)</code>. Groups of contiguous 
2934      * characters of the same type are returned as complete tokens.  
2935      * <pre> 
2936      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType(null)         = null 
2937      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("")           = [] 
2938      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("ab de fg")   = ["ab", " ", "de", " ", "fg"] 
2939      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("ab   de fg") = ["ab", "   ", "de", " ", "fg"] 
2940      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("ab:cd:ef")   = ["ab", ":", "cd", ":", "ef"] 
2941      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("number5")    = ["number", "5"] 
2942      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("fooBar")     = ["foo", "B", "ar"] 
2943      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("foo200Bar")  = ["foo", "200", "B", "ar"] 
2944      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterType("ASFRules")   = ["ASFR", "ules"] 
2945      * </pre> 
2946      * @param str the String to split, may be <code>null</code> 
2947      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2948      * @since 2.4 
2949      */ 
2950     public static String[] splitByCharacterType(String str) { 
2951         return splitByCharacterType(str, false); 
2952     } 
2953  
2954     /** 
2955      * <p>Splits a String by Character type as returned by 
2956      * <code>java.lang.Character.getType(char)</code>. Groups of contiguous 
2957      * characters of the same type are returned as complete tokens, with the 
2958      * following exception: the character of type 
2959      * <code>Character.UPPERCASE_LETTER</code>, if any, immediately 
2960      * preceding a token of type <code>Character.LOWERCASE_LETTER</code> 
2961      * will belong to the following token rather than to the preceding, if any, 
2962      * <code>Character.UPPERCASE_LETTER</code> token.  
2963      * <pre> 
2964      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase(null)         = null 
2965      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("")           = [] 
2966      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("ab de fg")   = ["ab", " ", "de", " ", "fg"] 
2967      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("ab   de fg") = ["ab", "   ", "de", " ", "fg"] 
2968      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("ab:cd:ef")   = ["ab", ":", "cd", ":", "ef"] 
2969      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("number5")    = ["number", "5"] 
2970      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("fooBar")     = ["foo", "Bar"] 
2971      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("foo200Bar")  = ["foo", "200", "Bar"] 
2972      * StringUtils.splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase("ASFRules")   = ["ASF", "Rules"] 
2973      * </pre> 
2974      * @param str the String to split, may be <code>null</code> 
2975      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2976      * @since 2.4 
2977      */ 
2978     public static String[] splitByCharacterTypeCamelCase(String str) { 
2979         return splitByCharacterType(str, true); 
2980     } 
2981  
2982     /** 
2983      * <p>Splits a String by Character type as returned by 
2984      * <code>java.lang.Character.getType(char)</code>. Groups of contiguous 
2985      * characters of the same type are returned as complete tokens, with the 
2986      * following exception: if <code>camelCase</code> is <code>true</code>, 
2987      * the character of type <code>Character.UPPERCASE_LETTER</code>, if any, 
2988      * immediately preceding a token of type <code>Character.LOWERCASE_LETTER</code> 
2989      * will belong to the following token rather than to the preceding, if any, 
2990      * <code>Character.UPPERCASE_LETTER</code> token.  
2991      * @param str the String to split, may be <code>null</code> 
2992      * @param camelCase whether to use so-called "camel-case" for letter types 
2993      * @return an array of parsed Strings, <code>null</code> if null String input 
2994      * @since 2.4 
2995      */ 
2996     private static String[] splitByCharacterType(String str, boolean camelCase) { 
2997         if (str == null) { 
2998             return null; 
2999         } 
3000         if (str.length() == 0) { 
3001             return ArrayUtils.EMPTY_STRING_ARRAY; 
3002         } 
3003         char[] c = str.toCharArray(); 
3004         List<String> list = new ArrayList<String>(); 
3005         int tokenStart = 0; 
3006         int currentType = Character.getType(c[tokenStart]); 
3007         for (int pos = tokenStart + 1; pos < c.length; pos++) { 
3008             int type = Character.getType(c[pos]); 
3009             if (type == currentType) { 
3010                 continue; 
3011             } 
3012             if (camelCase && type == Character.LOWERCASE_LETTER && currentType == Character.UPPERCASE_LETTER) { 
3013                 int newTokenStart = pos - 1; 
3014                 if (newTokenStart != tokenStart) { 
3015                     list.add(new String(c, tokenStart, newTokenStart - tokenStart)); 
3016                     tokenStart = newTokenStart; 
3017                 } 
3018             } else { 
3019                 list.add(new String(c, tokenStart, pos - tokenStart)); 
3020                 tokenStart = pos; 
3021             } 
3022             currentType = type; 
3023         } 
3024         list.add(new String(c, tokenStart, c.length - tokenStart)); 
3025         return list.toArray(new String[list.size()]); 
3026     }

3028     // Joining 
3029     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
3030     /** 
3031      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided array into a single String 
3032      * containing the provided list of elements.</p> 
3033      * 
3034      * <p>No separator is added to the joined String. 
3035      * Null objects or empty strings within the array are represented by 
3036      * empty strings.</p> 
3037      * 
3038      * <pre> 
3039      * StringUtils.join(null)            = null 
3040      * StringUtils.join([])              = "" 
3041      * StringUtils.join([null])          = "" 
3042      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"]) = "abc" 
3043      * StringUtils.join([null, "", "a"]) = "a" 
3044      * </pre> 
3045      * 
3046      * @param array  the array of values to join together, may be null 
3047      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null array input 
3048      * @since 2.0 
3049      */ 
3050     public static String join(Object[] array) { 
3051         return join(array, null); 
3052     } 
3053  
3054     /** 
3055      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided array into a single String 
3056      * containing the provided list of elements.</p> 
3057      * 
3058      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3059      * Null objects or empty strings within the array are represented by 
3060      * empty strings.</p> 
3061      * 
3062      * <pre> 
3063      * StringUtils.join(null, *)               = null 
3064      * StringUtils.join([], *)                 = "" 
3065      * StringUtils.join([null], *)             = "" 
3066      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], ';')  = "a;b;c" 
3067      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], null) = "abc" 
3068      * StringUtils.join([null, "", "a"], ';')  = ";;a" 
3069      * </pre> 
3070      * 
3071      * @param array  the array of values to join together, may be null 
3072      * @param separator  the separator character to use 
3073      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null array input 
3074      * @since 2.0 
3075      */ 
3076     public static String join(Object[] array, char separator) { 
3077         if (array == null) { 
3078             return null; 
3079         } 
3080  
3081         return join(array, separator, 0, array.length); 
3082     } 
3083  
3084     /** 
3085      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided array into a single String 
3086      * containing the provided list of elements.</p> 
3087      * 
3088      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3089      * Null objects or empty strings within the array are represented by 
3090      * empty strings.</p> 
3091      * 
3092      * <pre> 
3093      * StringUtils.join(null, *)               = null 
3094      * StringUtils.join([], *)                 = "" 
3095      * StringUtils.join([null], *)             = "" 
3096      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], ';')  = "a;b;c" 
3097      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], null) = "abc" 
3098      * StringUtils.join([null, "", "a"], ';')  = ";;a" 
3099      * </pre> 
3100      * 
3101      * @param array  the array of values to join together, may be null 
3102      * @param separator  the separator character to use 
3103      * @param startIndex the first index to start joining from.  It is 
3104      * an error to pass in an end index past the end of the array 
3105      * @param endIndex the index to stop joining from (exclusive). It is 
3106      * an error to pass in an end index past the end of the array 
3107      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null array input 
3108      * @since 2.0 
3109      */ 
3110     public static String join(Object[] array, char separator, int startIndex, int endIndex) { 
3111         if (array == null) { 
3112             return null; 
3113         } 
3114         int bufSize = (endIndex - startIndex); 
3115         if (bufSize <= 0) { 
3116             return EMPTY; 
3117         } 
3118  
3119         bufSize *= ((array[startIndex] == null ? 16 : array[startIndex].toString().length()) + 1); 
3120         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(bufSize); 
3121  
3122         for (int i = startIndex; i < endIndex; i++) { 
3123             if (i > startIndex) { 
3124                 buf.append(separator); 
3125             } 
3126             if (array[i] != null) { 
3127                 buf.append(array[i]); 
3128             } 
3129         } 
3130         return buf.toString(); 
3131     } 
3132  
3133  
3134     /** 
3135      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided array into a single String 
3136      * containing the provided list of elements.</p> 
3137      * 
3138      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3139      * A <code>null</code> separator is the same as an empty String (""). 
3140      * Null objects or empty strings within the array are represented by 
3141      * empty strings.</p> 
3142      * 
3143      * <pre> 
3144      * StringUtils.join(null, *)                = null 
3145      * StringUtils.join([], *)                  = "" 
3146      * StringUtils.join([null], *)              = "" 
3147      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], "--")  = "a--b--c" 
3148      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], null)  = "abc" 
3149      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], "")    = "abc" 
3150      * StringUtils.join([null, "", "a"], ',')   = ",,a" 
3151      * </pre> 
3152      * 
3153      * @param array  the array of values to join together, may be null 
3154      * @param separator  the separator character to use, null treated as "" 
3155      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null array input 
3156      */ 
3157     public static String join(Object[] array, String separator) { 
3158         if (array == null) { 
3159             return null; 
3160         } 
3161         return join(array, separator, 0, array.length); 
3162     } 
3163  
3164     /** 
3165      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided array into a single String 
3166      * containing the provided list of elements.</p> 
3167      * 
3168      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3169      * A <code>null</code> separator is the same as an empty String (""). 
3170      * Null objects or empty strings within the array are represented by 
3171      * empty strings.</p> 
3172      * 
3173      * <pre> 
3174      * StringUtils.join(null, *)                = null 
3175      * StringUtils.join([], *)                  = "" 
3176      * StringUtils.join([null], *)              = "" 
3177      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], "--")  = "a--b--c" 
3178      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], null)  = "abc" 
3179      * StringUtils.join(["a", "b", "c"], "")    = "abc" 
3180      * StringUtils.join([null, "", "a"], ',')   = ",,a" 
3181      * </pre> 
3182      * 
3183      * @param array  the array of values to join together, may be null 
3184      * @param separator  the separator character to use, null treated as "" 
3185      * @param startIndex the first index to start joining from.  It is 
3186      * an error to pass in an end index past the end of the array 
3187      * @param endIndex the index to stop joining from (exclusive). It is 
3188      * an error to pass in an end index past the end of the array 
3189      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null array input 
3190      */ 
3191     public static String join(Object[] array, String separator, int startIndex, int endIndex) { 
3192         if (array == null) { 
3193             return null; 
3194         } 
3195         if (separator == null) { 
3196             separator = EMPTY; 
3197         } 
3198  
3199         // endIndex - startIndex > 0:   Len = NofStrings *(len(firstString) + len(separator)) 
3200         //           (Assuming that all Strings are roughly equally long) 
3201         int bufSize = (endIndex - startIndex); 
3202         if (bufSize <= 0) { 
3203             return EMPTY; 
3204         } 
3205  
3206         bufSize *= ((array[startIndex] == null ? 16 : array[startIndex].toString().length()) 
3207                         + separator.length()); 
3208  
3209         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(bufSize); 
3210  
3211         for (int i = startIndex; i < endIndex; i++) { 
3212             if (i > startIndex) { 
3213                 buf.append(separator); 
3214             } 
3215             if (array[i] != null) { 
3216                 buf.append(array[i]); 
3217             } 
3218         } 
3219         return buf.toString(); 
3220     } 
3221  
3222     /** 
3223      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided <code>Iterator</code> into 
3224      * a single String containing the provided elements.</p> 
3225      * 
3226      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. Null objects or empty 
3227      * strings within the iteration are represented by empty strings.</p> 
3228      * 
3229      * <p>See the examples here: {@link #join(Object[],char)}. </p> 
3230      * 
3231      * @param iterator  the <code>Iterator</code> of values to join together, may be null 
3232      * @param separator  the separator character to use 
3233      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null iterator input 
3234      * @since 2.0 
3235      */ 
3236     public static String join(Iterator<?> iterator, char separator) { 
3237  
3238         // handle null, zero and one elements before building a buffer 
3239         if (iterator == null) { 
3240             return null; 
3241         } 
3242         if (!iterator.hasNext()) { 
3243             return EMPTY; 
3244         } 
3245         Object first = iterator.next(); 
3246         if (!iterator.hasNext()) { 
3247             return ObjectUtils.toString(first); 
3248         } 
3249  
3250         // two or more elements 
3251         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(256); // Java default is 16, probably too small 
3252         if (first != null) { 
3253             buf.append(first); 
3254         } 
3255  
3256         while (iterator.hasNext()) { 
3257             buf.append(separator); 
3258             Object obj = iterator.next(); 
3259             if (obj != null) { 
3260                 buf.append(obj); 
3261             } 
3262         } 
3263  
3264         return buf.toString(); 
3265     } 
3266  
3267     /** 
3268      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided <code>Iterator</code> into 
3269      * a single String containing the provided elements.</p> 
3270      * 
3271      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3272      * A <code>null</code> separator is the same as an empty String ("").</p> 
3273      * 
3274      * <p>See the examples here: {@link #join(Object[],String)}. </p> 
3275      * 
3276      * @param iterator  the <code>Iterator</code> of values to join together, may be null 
3277      * @param separator  the separator character to use, null treated as "" 
3278      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null iterator input 
3279      */ 
3280     public static String join(Iterator<?> iterator, String separator) { 
3281  
3282         // handle null, zero and one elements before building a buffer 
3283         if (iterator == null) { 
3284             return null; 
3285         } 
3286         if (!iterator.hasNext()) { 
3287             return EMPTY; 
3288         } 
3289         Object first = iterator.next(); 
3290         if (!iterator.hasNext()) { 
3291             return ObjectUtils.toString(first); 
3292         } 
3293  
3294         // two or more elements 
3295         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(256); // Java default is 16, probably too small 
3296         if (first != null) { 
3297             buf.append(first); 
3298         } 
3299  
3300         while (iterator.hasNext()) { 
3301             if (separator != null) { 
3302                 buf.append(separator); 
3303             } 
3304             Object obj = iterator.next(); 
3305             if (obj != null) { 
3306                 buf.append(obj); 
3307             } 
3308         } 
3309         return buf.toString(); 
3310     }

3312     /** 
3313      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided <code>Iterable</code> into 
3314      * a single String containing the provided elements.</p> 
3315      * 
3316      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. Null objects or empty 
3317      * strings within the iteration are represented by empty strings.</p> 
3318      * 
3319      * <p>See the examples here: {@link #join(Object[],char)}. </p> 
3320      * 
3321      * @param iterable  the <code>Iterable</code> providing the values to join together, may be null 
3322      * @param separator  the separator character to use 
3323      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null iterator input 
3324      * @since 2.3 
3325      */ 
3326     public static String join(Iterable<?> iterable, char separator) { 
3327         if (iterable == null) { 
3328             return null; 
3329         } 
3330         return join(iterable.iterator(), separator); 
3331     }

3333     /** 
3334      * <p>Joins the elements of the provided <code>Iterable</code> into 
3335      * a single String containing the provided elements.</p> 
3336      * 
3337      * <p>No delimiter is added before or after the list. 
3338      * A <code>null</code> separator is the same as an empty String ("").</p> 
3339      * 
3340      * <p>See the examples here: {@link #join(Object[],String)}. </p> 
3341      * 
3342      * @param iterable  the <code>Iterable</code> providing the values to join together, may be null 
3343      * @param separator  the separator character to use, null treated as "" 
3344      * @return the joined String, <code>null</code> if null iterator input 
3345      * @since 2.3 
3346      */ 
3347     public static String join(Iterable<?> iterable, String separator) { 
3348         if (iterable == null) { 
3349             return null; 
3350         } 
3351         return join(iterable.iterator(), separator); 
3352     }

3354     // Delete 
3355     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
3356     /** 
3357      * <p>Deletes all whitespaces from a String as defined by 
3358      * {@link Character#isWhitespace(char)}.</p> 
3359      * 
3360      * <pre> 
3361      * StringUtils.deleteWhitespace(null)         = null 
3362      * StringUtils.deleteWhitespace("")           = "" 
3363      * StringUtils.deleteWhitespace("abc")        = "abc" 
3364      * StringUtils.deleteWhitespace("   ab  c  ") = "abc" 
3365      * </pre> 
3366      * 
3367      * @param str  the String to delete whitespace from, may be null 
3368      * @return the String without whitespaces, <code>null</code> if null String input 
3369      */ 
3370     public static String deleteWhitespace(String str) { 
3371         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
3372             return str; 
3373         } 
3374         int sz = str.length(); 
3375         char[] chs = new char[sz]; 
3376         int count = 0; 
3377         for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) { 
3378             if (!Character.isWhitespace(str.charAt(i))) { 
3379                 chs[count++] = str.charAt(i); 
3380             } 
3381         } 
3382         if (count == sz) { 
3383             return str; 
3384         } 
3385         return new String(chs, 0, count); 
3386     }

3388     // Remove 
3389     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
3390     /** 
3391      * <p>Removes a substring only if it is at the begining of a source string, 
3392      * otherwise returns the source string.</p> 
3393      * 
3394      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3395      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string. 
3396      * A <code>null</code> search string will return the source string.</p> 
3397      * 
3398      * <pre> 
3399      * StringUtils.removeStart(null, *)      = null 
3400      * StringUtils.removeStart("", *)        = "" 
3401      * StringUtils.removeStart(*, null)      = * 
3402      * StringUtils.removeStart("www.domain.com", "www.")   = "domain.com" 
3403      * StringUtils.removeStart("domain.com", "www.")       = "domain.com" 
3404      * StringUtils.removeStart("www.domain.com", "domain") = "www.domain.com" 
3405      * StringUtils.removeStart("abc", "")    = "abc" 
3406      * </pre> 
3407      * 
3408      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3409      * @param remove  the String to search for and remove, may be null 
3410      * @return the substring with the string removed if found, 
3411      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3412      * @since 2.1 
3413      */ 
3414     public static String removeStart(String str, String remove) { 
3415         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(remove)) { 
3416             return str; 
3417         } 
3418         if (str.startsWith(remove)){ 
3419             return str.substring(remove.length()); 
3420         } 
3421         return str; 
3422     } 
3423  
3424     /** 
3425      * <p>Case insensitive removal of a substring if it is at the begining of a source string, 
3426      * otherwise returns the source string.</p> 
3427      * 
3428      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3429      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string. 
3430      * A <code>null</code> search string will return the source string.</p> 
3431      * 
3432      * <pre> 
3433      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase(null, *)      = null 
3434      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("", *)        = "" 
3435      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase(*, null)      = * 
3436      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", "www.")   = "domain.com" 
3437      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", "WWW.")   = "domain.com" 
3438      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("domain.com", "www.")       = "domain.com" 
3439      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", "domain") = "www.domain.com" 
3440      * StringUtils.removeStartIgnoreCase("abc", "")    = "abc" 
3441      * </pre> 
3442      * 
3443      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3444      * @param remove  the String to search for (case insensitive) and remove, may be null 
3445      * @return the substring with the string removed if found, 
3446      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3447      * @since 2.4 
3448      */ 
3449     public static String removeStartIgnoreCase(String str, String remove) { 
3450         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(remove)) { 
3451             return str; 
3452         } 
3453         if (startsWithIgnoreCase(str, remove)) { 
3454             return str.substring(remove.length()); 
3455         } 
3456         return str; 
3457     } 
3458  
3459     /** 
3460      * <p>Removes a substring only if it is at the end of a source string, 
3461      * otherwise returns the source string.</p> 
3462      * 
3463      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3464      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string. 
3465      * A <code>null</code> search string will return the source string.</p> 
3466      * 
3467      * <pre> 
3468      * StringUtils.removeEnd(null, *)      = null 
3469      * StringUtils.removeEnd("", *)        = "" 
3470      * StringUtils.removeEnd(*, null)      = * 
3471      * StringUtils.removeEnd("www.domain.com", ".com.")  = "www.domain.com" 
3472      * StringUtils.removeEnd("www.domain.com", ".com")   = "www.domain" 
3473      * StringUtils.removeEnd("www.domain.com", "domain") = "www.domain.com" 
3474      * StringUtils.removeEnd("abc", "")    = "abc" 
3475      * </pre> 
3476      * 
3477      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3478      * @param remove  the String to search for and remove, may be null 
3479      * @return the substring with the string removed if found, 
3480      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3481      * @since 2.1 
3482      */ 
3483     public static String removeEnd(String str, String remove) { 
3484         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(remove)) { 
3485             return str; 
3486         } 
3487         if (str.endsWith(remove)) { 
3488             return str.substring(0, str.length() - remove.length()); 
3489         } 
3490         return str; 
3491     } 
3492  
3493     /** 
3494      * <p>Case insensitive removal of a substring if it is at the end of a source string, 
3495      * otherwise returns the source string.</p> 
3496      * 
3497      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3498      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string. 
3499      * A <code>null</code> search string will return the source string.</p> 
3500      * 
3501      * <pre> 
3502      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase(null, *)      = null 
3503      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("", *)        = "" 
3504      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase(*, null)      = * 
3505      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", ".com.")  = "www.domain.com" 
3506      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", ".com")   = "www.domain" 
3507      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", "domain") = "www.domain.com" 
3508      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("abc", "")    = "abc" 
3509      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("www.domain.com", ".COM") = "www.domain") 
3510      * StringUtils.removeEndIgnoreCase("www.domain.COM", ".com") = "www.domain") 
3511      * </pre> 
3512      * 
3513      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3514      * @param remove  the String to search for (case insensitive) and remove, may be null 
3515      * @return the substring with the string removed if found, 
3516      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3517      * @since 2.4 
3518      */ 
3519     public static String removeEndIgnoreCase(String str, String remove) { 
3520         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(remove)) { 
3521             return str; 
3522         } 
3523         if (endsWithIgnoreCase(str, remove)) { 
3524             return str.substring(0, str.length() - remove.length()); 
3525         } 
3526         return str; 
3527     } 
3528  
3529     /** 
3530      * <p>Removes all occurrences of a substring from within the source string.</p> 
3531      * 
3532      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3533      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string. 
3534      * A <code>null</code> remove string will return the source string. 
3535      * An empty ("") remove string will return the source string.</p> 
3536      * 
3537      * <pre> 
3538      * StringUtils.remove(null, *)        = null 
3539      * StringUtils.remove("", *)          = "" 
3540      * StringUtils.remove(*, null)        = * 
3541      * StringUtils.remove(*, "")          = * 
3542      * StringUtils.remove("queued", "ue") = "qd" 
3543      * StringUtils.remove("queued", "zz") = "queued" 
3544      * </pre> 
3545      * 
3546      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3547      * @param remove  the String to search for and remove, may be null 
3548      * @return the substring with the string removed if found, 
3549      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3550      * @since 2.1 
3551      */ 
3552     public static String remove(String str, String remove) { 
3553         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(remove)) { 
3554             return str; 
3555         } 
3556         return replace(str, remove, EMPTY, -1); 
3557     } 
3558  
3559     /** 
3560      * <p>Removes all occurrences of a character from within the source string.</p> 
3561      * 
3562      * <p>A <code>null</code> source string will return <code>null</code>. 
3563      * An empty ("") source string will return the empty string.</p> 
3564      * 
3565      * <pre> 
3566      * StringUtils.remove(null, *)       = null 
3567      * StringUtils.remove("", *)         = "" 
3568      * StringUtils.remove("queued", 'u') = "qeed" 
3569      * StringUtils.remove("queued", 'z') = "queued" 
3570      * </pre> 
3571      * 
3572      * @param str  the source String to search, may be null 
3573      * @param remove  the char to search for and remove, may be null 
3574      * @return the substring with the char removed if found, 
3575      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3576      * @since 2.1 
3577      */ 
3578     public static String remove(String str, char remove) { 
3579         if (isEmpty(str) || str.indexOf(remove) == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
3580             return str; 
3581         } 
3582         char[] chars = str.toCharArray(); 
3583         int pos = 0; 
3584         for (int i = 0; i < chars.length; i++) { 
3585             if (chars[i] != remove) { 
3586                 chars[pos++] = chars[i]; 
3587             } 
3588         } 
3589         return new String(chars, 0, pos); 
3590     }
