3592     // Replacing 
3593     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
3594     /** 
3595      * <p>Replaces a String with another String inside a larger String, once.</p> 
3596      * 
3597      * <p>A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op.</p> 
3598      * 
3599      * <pre> 
3600      * StringUtils.replaceOnce(null, *, *)        = null 
3601      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("", *, *)          = "" 
3602      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("any", null, *)    = "any" 
3603      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("any", *, null)    = "any" 
3604      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("any", "", *)      = "any" 
3605      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("aba", "a", null)  = "aba" 
3606      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("aba", "a", "")    = "ba" 
3607      * StringUtils.replaceOnce("aba", "a", "z")   = "zba" 
3608      * </pre> 
3609      * 
3610      * @see #replace(String text, String searchString, String replacement, int max) 
3611      * @param text  text to search and replace in, may be null 
3612      * @param searchString  the String to search for, may be null 
3613      * @param replacement  the String to replace with, may be null 
3614      * @return the text with any replacements processed, 
3615      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3616      */ 
3617     public static String replaceOnce(String text, String searchString, String replacement) { 
3618         return replace(text, searchString, replacement, 1); 
3619     } 
3620  
3621     /** 
3622      * <p>Replaces all occurrences of a String within another String.</p> 
3623      * 
3624      * <p>A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op.</p> 
3625      * 
3626      * <pre> 
3627      * StringUtils.replace(null, *, *)        = null 
3628      * StringUtils.replace("", *, *)          = "" 
3629      * StringUtils.replace("any", null, *)    = "any" 
3630      * StringUtils.replace("any", *, null)    = "any" 
3631      * StringUtils.replace("any", "", *)      = "any" 
3632      * StringUtils.replace("aba", "a", null)  = "aba" 
3633      * StringUtils.replace("aba", "a", "")    = "b" 
3634      * StringUtils.replace("aba", "a", "z")   = "zbz" 
3635      * </pre> 
3636      * 
3637      * @see #replace(String text, String searchString, String replacement, int max) 
3638      * @param text  text to search and replace in, may be null 
3639      * @param searchString  the String to search for, may be null 
3640      * @param replacement  the String to replace it with, may be null 
3641      * @return the text with any replacements processed, 
3642      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3643      */ 
3644     public static String replace(String text, String searchString, String replacement) { 
3645         return replace(text, searchString, replacement, -1); 
3646     } 
3647  
3648     /** 
3649      * <p>Replaces a String with another String inside a larger String, 
3650      * for the first <code>max</code> values of the search String.</p> 
3651      * 
3652      * <p>A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op.</p> 
3653      * 
3654      * <pre> 
3655      * StringUtils.replace(null, *, *, *)         = null 
3656      * StringUtils.replace("", *, *, *)           = "" 
3657      * StringUtils.replace("any", null, *, *)     = "any" 
3658      * StringUtils.replace("any", *, null, *)     = "any" 
3659      * StringUtils.replace("any", "", *, *)       = "any" 
3660      * StringUtils.replace("any", *, *, 0)        = "any" 
3661      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", null, -1) = "abaa" 
3662      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", "", -1)   = "b" 
3663      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", "z", 0)   = "abaa" 
3664      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", "z", 1)   = "zbaa" 
3665      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", "z", 2)   = "zbza" 
3666      * StringUtils.replace("abaa", "a", "z", -1)  = "zbzz" 
3667      * </pre> 
3668      * 
3669      * @param text  text to search and replace in, may be null 
3670      * @param searchString  the String to search for, may be null 
3671      * @param replacement  the String to replace it with, may be null 
3672      * @param max  maximum number of values to replace, or <code>-1</code> if no maximum 
3673      * @return the text with any replacements processed, 
3674      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
3675      */ 
3676     public static String replace(String text, String searchString, String replacement, int max) { 
3677         if (isEmpty(text) || isEmpty(searchString) || replacement == null || max == 0) { 
3678             return text; 
3679         } 
3680         int start = 0; 
3681         int end = text.indexOf(searchString, start); 
3682         if (end == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
3683             return text; 
3684         } 
3685         int replLength = searchString.length(); 
3686         int increase = replacement.length() - replLength; 
3687         increase = (increase < 0 ? 0 : increase); 
3688         increase *= (max < 0 ? 16 : (max > 64 ? 64 : max)); 
3689         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(text.length() + increase); 
3690         while (end != INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
3691             buf.append(text.substring(start, end)).append(replacement); 
3692             start = end + replLength; 
3693             if (--max == 0) { 
3694                 break; 
3695             } 
3696             end = text.indexOf(searchString, start); 
3697         } 
3698         buf.append(text.substring(start)); 
3699         return buf.toString(); 
3700     } 
3701  
3702     /** 
3703      * <p> 
3704      * Replaces all occurrences of Strings within another String. 
3705      * </p> 
3706      *  
3707      * <p> 
3708      * A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op, or if 
3709      * any "search string" or "string to replace" is null, that replace will be 
3710      * ignored. This will not repeat. For repeating replaces, call the 
3711      * overloaded method. 
3712      * </p> 
3713      *  
3714      * <pre> 
3715      *  StringUtils.replaceEach(null, *, *)        = null 
3716      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("", *, *)          = "" 
3717      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, null) = "aba" 
3718      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[0], null) = "aba" 
3719      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, new String[0]) = "aba" 
3720      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, null)  = "aba" 
3721      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, new String[]{""})  = "b" 
3722      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{null}, new String[]{"a"})  = "aba" 
3723      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"w", "t"})  = "wcte" 
3724      *  (example of how it does not repeat) 
3725      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "t"})  = "dcte" 
3726      * </pre> 
3727      *  
3728      * @param text 
3729      *            text to search and replace in, no-op if null 
3730      * @param searchList 
3731      *            the Strings to search for, no-op if null 
3732      * @param replacementList 
3733      *            the Strings to replace them with, no-op if null 
3734      * @return the text with any replacements processed, <code>null</code> if 
3735      *         null String input 
3736      * @throws IndexOutOfBoundsException 
3737      *             if the lengths of the arrays are not the same (null is ok, 
3738      *             and/or size 0) 
3739      * @since 2.4 
3740      */ 
3741     public static String replaceEach(String text, String[] searchList, String[] replacementList) { 
3742         return replaceEach(text, searchList, replacementList, false, 0); 
3743     } 
3744  
3745     /** 
3746      * <p> 
3747      * Replaces all occurrences of Strings within another String. 
3748      * </p> 
3749      *  
3750      * <p> 
3751      * A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op, or if 
3752      * any "search string" or "string to replace" is null, that replace will be 
3753      * ignored. This will not repeat. For repeating replaces, call the 
3754      * overloaded method. 
3755      * </p> 
3756      *  
3757      * <pre> 
3758      *  StringUtils.replaceEach(null, *, *, *) = null 
3759      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("", *, *, *) = "" 
3760      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, null, *) = "aba" 
3761      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[0], null, *) = "aba" 
3762      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, new String[0], *) = "aba" 
3763      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, null, *) = "aba" 
3764      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, new String[]{""}, *) = "b" 
3765      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{null}, new String[]{"a"}, *) = "aba" 
3766      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"w", "t"}, *) = "wcte" 
3767      *  (example of how it repeats) 
3768      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "t"}, false) = "dcte" 
3769      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "t"}, true) = "tcte" 
3770      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "ab"}, true) = IllegalArgumentException 
3771      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "ab"}, false) = "dcabe" 
3772      * </pre> 
3773      *  
3774      * @param text 
3775      *            text to search and replace in, no-op if null 
3776      * @param searchList 
3777      *            the Strings to search for, no-op if null 
3778      * @param replacementList 
3779      *            the Strings to replace them with, no-op if null 
3780      * @return the text with any replacements processed, <code>null</code> if 
3781      *         null String input 
3782      * @throws IllegalArgumentException 
3783      *             if the search is repeating and there is an endless loop due 
3784      *             to outputs of one being inputs to another 
3785      * @throws IndexOutOfBoundsException 
3786      *             if the lengths of the arrays are not the same (null is ok, 
3787      *             and/or size 0) 
3788      * @since 2.4 
3789      */ 
3790     public static String replaceEachRepeatedly(String text, String[] searchList, String[] replacementList) { 
3791         // timeToLive should be 0 if not used or nothing to replace, else it's 
3792         // the length of the replace array 
3793         int timeToLive = searchList == null ? 0 : searchList.length; 
3794         return replaceEach(text, searchList, replacementList, true, timeToLive); 
3795     } 
3796  
3797     /** 
3798      * <p> 
3799      * Replaces all occurrences of Strings within another String. 
3800      * </p> 
3801      *  
3802      * <p> 
3803      * A <code>null</code> reference passed to this method is a no-op, or if 
3804      * any "search string" or "string to replace" is null, that replace will be 
3805      * ignored.  
3806      * </p> 
3807      *  
3808      * <pre> 
3809      *  StringUtils.replaceEach(null, *, *, *) = null 
3810      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("", *, *, *) = "" 
3811      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, null, *) = "aba" 
3812      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[0], null, *) = "aba" 
3813      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", null, new String[0], *) = "aba" 
3814      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, null, *) = "aba" 
3815      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{"a"}, new String[]{""}, *) = "b" 
3816      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("aba", new String[]{null}, new String[]{"a"}, *) = "aba" 
3817      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"w", "t"}, *) = "wcte" 
3818      *  (example of how it repeats) 
3819      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "t"}, false) = "dcte" 
3820      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "t"}, true) = "tcte" 
3821      *  StringUtils.replaceEach("abcde", new String[]{"ab", "d"}, new String[]{"d", "ab"}, *) = IllegalArgumentException 
3822      * </pre> 
3823      *  
3824      * @param text 
3825      *            text to search and replace in, no-op if null 
3826      * @param searchList 
3827      *            the Strings to search for, no-op if null 
3828      * @param replacementList 
3829      *            the Strings to replace them with, no-op if null 
3830      * @param repeat if true, then replace repeatedly  
3831      *       until there are no more possible replacements or timeToLive < 0 
3832      * @param timeToLive 
3833      *            if less than 0 then there is a circular reference and endless 
3834      *            loop 
3835      * @return the text with any replacements processed, <code>null</code> if 
3836      *         null String input 
3837      * @throws IllegalArgumentException 
3838      *             if the search is repeating and there is an endless loop due 
3839      *             to outputs of one being inputs to another 
3840      * @throws IndexOutOfBoundsException 
3841      *             if the lengths of the arrays are not the same (null is ok, 
3842      *             and/or size 0) 
3843      * @since 2.4 
3844      */ 
3845     private static String replaceEach(String text, String[] searchList, String[] replacementList,  
3846                                       boolean repeat, int timeToLive)  
3847     { 
3848  
3849         // mchyzer Performance note: This creates very few new objects (one major goal) 
3850         // let me know if there are performance requests, we can create a harness to measure 
3851  
3852         if (text == null || text.length() == 0 || searchList == null ||  
3853             searchList.length == 0 || replacementList == null || replacementList.length == 0)  
3854         { 
3855             return text; 
3856         } 
3857  
3858         // if recursing, this shouldnt be less than 0 
3859         if (timeToLive < 0) { 
3860             throw new IllegalStateException("TimeToLive of " + timeToLive + " is less than 0: " + text); 
3861         } 
3862  
3863         int searchLength = searchList.length; 
3864         int replacementLength = replacementList.length; 
3865  
3866         // make sure lengths are ok, these need to be equal 
3867         if (searchLength != replacementLength) { 
3868             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Search and Replace array lengths don't match: " 
3869                 + searchLength 
3870                 + " vs " 
3871                 + replacementLength); 
3872         } 
3873  
3874         // keep track of which still have matches 
3875         boolean[] noMoreMatchesForReplIndex = new boolean[searchLength]; 
3876  
3877         // index on index that the match was found 
3878         int textIndex = -1; 
3879         int replaceIndex = -1; 
3880         int tempIndex = -1; 
3881  
3882         // index of replace array that will replace the search string found 
3883         // NOTE: logic duplicated below START 
3884         for (int i = 0; i < searchLength; i++) { 
3885             if (noMoreMatchesForReplIndex[i] || searchList[i] == null ||  
3886                 searchList[i].length() == 0 || replacementList[i] == null)  
3887             { 
3888                 continue; 
3889             } 
3890             tempIndex = text.indexOf(searchList[i]); 
3891  
3892             // see if we need to keep searching for this 
3893             if (tempIndex == -1) { 
3894                 noMoreMatchesForReplIndex[i] = true; 
3895             } else { 
3896                 if (textIndex == -1 || tempIndex < textIndex) { 
3897                     textIndex = tempIndex; 
3898                     replaceIndex = i; 
3899                 } 
3900             } 
3901         } 
3902         // NOTE: logic mostly below END 
3903  
3904         // no search strings found, we are done 
3905         if (textIndex == -1) { 
3906             return text; 
3907         } 
3908  
3909         int start = 0; 
3910  
3911         // get a good guess on the size of the result buffer so it doesnt have to double if it goes over a bit 
3912         int increase = 0; 
3913  
3914         // count the replacement text elements that are larger than their corresponding text being replaced 
3915         for (int i = 0; i < searchList.length; i++) { 
3916             if (searchList[i] == null || replacementList[i] == null) { 
3917                 continue; 
3918             } 
3919             int greater = replacementList[i].length() - searchList[i].length(); 
3920             if (greater > 0) { 
3921                 increase += 3 * greater; // assume 3 matches 
3922             } 
3923         } 
3924         // have upper-bound at 20% increase, then let Java take over 
3925         increase = Math.min(increase, text.length() / 5); 
3926  
3927         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(text.length() + increase); 
3928  
3929         while (textIndex != -1) { 
3930  
3931             for (int i = start; i < textIndex; i++) { 
3932                 buf.append(text.charAt(i)); 
3933             } 
3934             buf.append(replacementList[replaceIndex]); 
3935  
3936             start = textIndex + searchList[replaceIndex].length(); 
3937  
3938             textIndex = -1; 
3939             replaceIndex = -1; 
3940             tempIndex = -1; 
3941             // find the next earliest match 
3942             // NOTE: logic mostly duplicated above START 
3943             for (int i = 0; i < searchLength; i++) { 
3944                 if (noMoreMatchesForReplIndex[i] || searchList[i] == null ||  
3945                     searchList[i].length() == 0 || replacementList[i] == null)  
3946                 { 
3947                     continue; 
3948                 } 
3949                 tempIndex = text.indexOf(searchList[i], start); 
3950  
3951                 // see if we need to keep searching for this 
3952                 if (tempIndex == -1) { 
3953                     noMoreMatchesForReplIndex[i] = true; 
3954                 } else { 
3955                     if (textIndex == -1 || tempIndex < textIndex) { 
3956                         textIndex = tempIndex; 
3957                         replaceIndex = i; 
3958                     } 
3959                 } 
3960             } 
3961             // NOTE: logic duplicated above END 
3962  
3963         } 
3964         int textLength = text.length(); 
3965         for (int i = start; i < textLength; i++) { 
3966             buf.append(text.charAt(i)); 
3967         } 
3968         String result = buf.toString(); 
3969         if (!repeat) { 
3970             return result; 
3971         } 
3972  
3973         return replaceEach(result, searchList, replacementList, repeat, timeToLive - 1); 
3974     } 
3975  
3976     // Replace, character based 
3977     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
3978     /** 
3979      * <p>Replaces all occurrences of a character in a String with another. 
3980      * This is a null-safe version of {@link String#replace(char, char)}.</p> 
3981      * 
3982      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input returns <code>null</code>. 
3983      * An empty ("") string input returns an empty string.</p> 
3984      * 
3985      * <pre> 
3986      * StringUtils.replaceChars(null, *, *)        = null 
3987      * StringUtils.replaceChars("", *, *)          = "" 
3988      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abcba", 'b', 'y') = "aycya" 
3989      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abcba", 'z', 'y') = "abcba" 
3990      * </pre> 
3991      * 
3992      * @param str  String to replace characters in, may be null 
3993      * @param searchChar  the character to search for, may be null 
3994      * @param replaceChar  the character to replace, may be null 
3995      * @return modified String, <code>null</code> if null string input 
3996      * @since 2.0 
3997      */ 
3998     public static String replaceChars(String str, char searchChar, char replaceChar) { 
3999         if (str == null) { 
4000             return null; 
4001         } 
4002         return str.replace(searchChar, replaceChar); 
4003     } 
4004  
4005     /** 
4006      * <p>Replaces multiple characters in a String in one go. 
4007      * This method can also be used to delete characters.</p> 
4008      * 
4009      * <p>For example:<br /> 
4010      * <code>replaceChars(&quot;hello&quot;, &quot;ho&quot;, &quot;jy&quot;) = jelly</code>.</p> 
4011      * 
4012      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input returns <code>null</code>. 
4013      * An empty ("") string input returns an empty string. 
4014      * A null or empty set of search characters returns the input string.</p> 
4015      * 
4016      * <p>The length of the search characters should normally equal the length 
4017      * of the replace characters. 
4018      * If the search characters is longer, then the extra search characters 
4019      * are deleted. 
4020      * If the search characters is shorter, then the extra replace characters 
4021      * are ignored.</p> 
4022      * 
4023      * <pre> 
4024      * StringUtils.replaceChars(null, *, *)           = null 
4025      * StringUtils.replaceChars("", *, *)             = "" 
4026      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abc", null, *)       = "abc" 
4027      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abc", "", *)         = "abc" 
4028      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abc", "b", null)     = "ac" 
4029      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abc", "b", "")       = "ac" 
4030      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abcba", "bc", "yz")  = "ayzya" 
4031      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abcba", "bc", "y")   = "ayya" 
4032      * StringUtils.replaceChars("abcba", "bc", "yzx") = "ayzya" 
4033      * </pre> 
4034      * 
4035      * @param str  String to replace characters in, may be null 
4036      * @param searchChars  a set of characters to search for, may be null 
4037      * @param replaceChars  a set of characters to replace, may be null 
4038      * @return modified String, <code>null</code> if null string input 
4039      * @since 2.0 
4040      */ 
4041     public static String replaceChars(String str, String searchChars, String replaceChars) { 
4042         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(searchChars)) { 
4043             return str; 
4044         } 
4045         if (replaceChars == null) { 
4046             replaceChars = EMPTY; 
4047         } 
4048         boolean modified = false; 
4049         int replaceCharsLength = replaceChars.length(); 
4050         int strLength = str.length(); 
4051         StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(strLength); 
4052         for (int i = 0; i < strLength; i++) { 
4053             char ch = str.charAt(i); 
4054             int index = searchChars.indexOf(ch); 
4055             if (index >= 0) { 
4056                 modified = true; 
4057                 if (index < replaceCharsLength) { 
4058                     buf.append(replaceChars.charAt(index)); 
4059                 } 
4060             } else { 
4061                 buf.append(ch); 
4062             } 
4063         } 
4064         if (modified) { 
4065             return buf.toString(); 
4066         } 
4067         return str; 
4068     } 
4069  
4070     // Overlay 
4071     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4072     /** 
4073      * <p>Overlays part of a String with another String.</p> 
4074      * 
4075      * <p>A <code>null</code> string input returns <code>null</code>. 
4076      * A negative index is treated as zero. 
4077      * An index greater than the string length is treated as the string length. 
4078      * The start index is always the smaller of the two indices.</p> 
4079      * 
4080      * <pre> 
4081      * StringUtils.overlay(null, *, *, *)            = null 
4082      * StringUtils.overlay("", "abc", 0, 0)          = "abc" 
4083      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", null, 2, 4)     = "abef" 
4084      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "", 2, 4)       = "abef" 
4085      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "", 4, 2)       = "abef" 
4086      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", 2, 4)   = "abzzzzef" 
4087      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", 4, 2)   = "abzzzzef" 
4088      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", -1, 4)  = "zzzzef" 
4089      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", 2, 8)   = "abzzzz" 
4090      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", -2, -3) = "zzzzabcdef" 
4091      * StringUtils.overlay("abcdef", "zzzz", 8, 10)  = "abcdefzzzz" 
4092      * </pre> 
4093      * 
4094      * @param str  the String to do overlaying in, may be null 
4095      * @param overlay  the String to overlay, may be null 
4096      * @param start  the position to start overlaying at 
4097      * @param end  the position to stop overlaying before 
4098      * @return overlayed String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4099      * @since 2.0 
4100      */ 
4101     public static String overlay(String str, String overlay, int start, int end) { 
4102         if (str == null) { 
4103             return null; 
4104         } 
4105         if (overlay == null) { 
4106             overlay = EMPTY; 
4107         } 
4108         int len = str.length(); 
4109         if (start < 0) { 
4110             start = 0; 
4111         } 
4112         if (start > len) { 
4113             start = len; 
4114         } 
4115         if (end < 0) { 
4116             end = 0; 
4117         } 
4118         if (end > len) { 
4119             end = len; 
4120         } 
4121         if (start > end) { 
4122             int temp = start; 
4123             start = end; 
4124             end = temp; 
4125         } 
4126         return new StringBuilder(len + start - end + overlay.length() + 1) 
4127             .append(str.substring(0, start)) 
4128             .append(overlay) 
4129             .append(str.substring(end)) 
4130             .toString(); 
4131     } 
4132  
4133     // Chomping 
4134     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4135     /** 
4136      * <p>Removes one newline from end of a String if it's there, 
4137      * otherwise leave it alone.  A newline is &quot;<code>\n</code>&quot;, 
4138      * &quot;<code>\r</code>&quot;, or &quot;<code>\r\n</code>&quot;.</p> 
4139      * 
4140      * <p>NOTE: This method changed in 2.0. 
4141      * It now more closely matches Perl chomp.</p> 
4142      * 
4143      * <pre> 
4144      * StringUtils.chomp(null)          = null 
4145      * StringUtils.chomp("")            = "" 
4146      * StringUtils.chomp("abc \r")      = "abc " 
4147      * StringUtils.chomp("abc\n")       = "abc" 
4148      * StringUtils.chomp("abc\r\n")     = "abc" 
4149      * StringUtils.chomp("abc\r\n\r\n") = "abc\r\n" 
4150      * StringUtils.chomp("abc\n\r")     = "abc\n" 
4151      * StringUtils.chomp("abc\n\rabc")  = "abc\n\rabc" 
4152      * StringUtils.chomp("\r")          = "" 
4153      * StringUtils.chomp("\n")          = "" 
4154      * StringUtils.chomp("\r\n")        = "" 
4155      * </pre> 
4156      * 
4157      * @param str  the String to chomp a newline from, may be null 
4158      * @return String without newline, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4159      */ 
4160     public static String chomp(String str) { 
4161         if (isEmpty(str)) { 
4162             return str; 
4163         } 
4164  
4165         if (str.length() == 1) { 
4166             char ch = str.charAt(0); 
4167             if (ch == CharUtils.CR || ch == CharUtils.LF) { 
4168                 return EMPTY; 
4169             } 
4170             return str; 
4171         } 
4172  
4173         int lastIdx = str.length() - 1; 
4174         char last = str.charAt(lastIdx); 
4175  
4176         if (last == CharUtils.LF) { 
4177             if (str.charAt(lastIdx - 1) == CharUtils.CR) { 
4178                 lastIdx--; 
4179             } 
4180         } else if (last != CharUtils.CR) { 
4181             lastIdx++; 
4182         } 
4183         return str.substring(0, lastIdx); 
4184     } 
4185  
4186     /** 
4187      * <p>Removes <code>separator</code> from the end of 
4188      * <code>str</code> if it's there, otherwise leave it alone.</p> 
4189      * 
4190      * <p>NOTE: This method changed in version 2.0. 
4191      * It now more closely matches Perl chomp. 
4192      * For the previous behavior, use {@link #substringBeforeLast(String, String)}. 
4193      * This method uses {@link String#endsWith(String)}.</p> 
4194      * 
4195      * <pre> 
4196      * StringUtils.chomp(null, *)         = null 
4197      * StringUtils.chomp("", *)           = "" 
4198      * StringUtils.chomp("foobar", "bar") = "foo" 
4199      * StringUtils.chomp("foobar", "baz") = "foobar" 
4200      * StringUtils.chomp("foo", "foo")    = "" 
4201      * StringUtils.chomp("foo ", "foo")   = "foo " 
4202      * StringUtils.chomp(" foo", "foo")   = " " 
4203      * StringUtils.chomp("foo", "foooo")  = "foo" 
4204      * StringUtils.chomp("foo", "")       = "foo" 
4205      * StringUtils.chomp("foo", null)     = "foo" 
4206      * </pre> 
4207      * 
4208      * @param str  the String to chomp from, may be null 
4209      * @param separator  separator String, may be null 
4210      * @return String without trailing separator, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4211      */ 
4212     public static String chomp(String str, String separator) { 
4213         if (isEmpty(str) || separator == null) { 
4214             return str; 
4215         } 
4216         if (str.endsWith(separator)) { 
4217             return str.substring(0, str.length() - separator.length()); 
4218         } 
4219         return str; 
4220     } 
4221  
4222     // Chopping 
4223     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4224     /** 
4225      * <p>Remove the last character from a String.</p> 
4226      * 
4227      * <p>If the String ends in <code>\r\n</code>, then remove both 
4228      * of them.</p> 
4229      * 
4230      * <pre> 
4231      * StringUtils.chop(null)          = null 
4232      * StringUtils.chop("")            = "" 
4233      * StringUtils.chop("abc \r")      = "abc " 
4234      * StringUtils.chop("abc\n")       = "abc" 
4235      * StringUtils.chop("abc\r\n")     = "abc" 
4236      * StringUtils.chop("abc")         = "ab" 
4237      * StringUtils.chop("abc\nabc")    = "abc\nab" 
4238      * StringUtils.chop("a")           = "" 
4239      * StringUtils.chop("\r")          = "" 
4240      * StringUtils.chop("\n")          = "" 
4241      * StringUtils.chop("\r\n")        = "" 
4242      * </pre> 
4243      * 
4244      * @param str  the String to chop last character from, may be null 
4245      * @return String without last character, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4246      */ 
4247     public static String chop(String str) { 
4248         if (str == null) { 
4249             return null; 
4250         } 
4251         int strLen = str.length(); 
4252         if (strLen < 2) { 
4253             return EMPTY; 
4254         } 
4255         int lastIdx = strLen - 1; 
4256         String ret = str.substring(0, lastIdx); 
4257         char last = str.charAt(lastIdx); 
4258         if (last == CharUtils.LF) { 
4259             if (ret.charAt(lastIdx - 1) == CharUtils.CR) { 
4260                 return ret.substring(0, lastIdx - 1); 
4261             } 
4262         } 
4263         return ret; 
4264     } 
4265  
4266     // Conversion 
4267     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4268  
4269     // Padding 
4270     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4271     /** 
4272      * <p>Repeat a String <code>repeat</code> times to form a 
4273      * new String.</p> 
4274      * 
4275      * <pre> 
4276      * StringUtils.repeat(null, 2) = null 
4277      * StringUtils.repeat("", 0)   = "" 
4278      * StringUtils.repeat("", 2)   = "" 
4279      * StringUtils.repeat("a", 3)  = "aaa" 
4280      * StringUtils.repeat("ab", 2) = "abab" 
4281      * StringUtils.repeat("a", -2) = "" 
4282      * </pre> 
4283      * 
4284      * @param str  the String to repeat, may be null 
4285      * @param repeat  number of times to repeat str, negative treated as zero 
4286      * @return a new String consisting of the original String repeated, 
4287      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4288      * @since 2.5 
4289      */ 
4290     public static String repeat(String str, int repeat) { 
4291         // Performance tuned for 2.0 (JDK1.4) 
4292  
4293         if (str == null) { 
4294             return null; 
4295         } 
4296         if (repeat <= 0) { 
4297             return EMPTY; 
4298         } 
4299         int inputLength = str.length(); 
4300         if (repeat == 1 || inputLength == 0) { 
4301             return str; 
4302         } 
4303         if (inputLength == 1 && repeat <= PAD_LIMIT) { 
4304             return padding(repeat, str.charAt(0)); 
4305         } 
4306  
4307         int outputLength = inputLength * repeat; 
4308         switch (inputLength) { 
4309             case 1 : 
4310                 char ch = str.charAt(0); 
4311                 char[] output1 = new char[outputLength]; 
4312                 for (int i = repeat - 1; i >= 0; i--) { 
4313                     output1[i] = ch; 
4314                 } 
4315                 return new String(output1); 
4316             case 2 : 
4317                 char ch0 = str.charAt(0); 
4318                 char ch1 = str.charAt(1); 
4319                 char[] output2 = new char[outputLength]; 
4320                 for (int i = repeat * 2 - 2; i >= 0; i--, i--) { 
4321                     output2[i] = ch0; 
4322                     output2[i + 1] = ch1; 
4323                 } 
4324                 return new String(output2); 
4325             default : 
4326                 StringBuilder buf = new StringBuilder(outputLength); 
4327                 for (int i = 0; i < repeat; i++) { 
4328                     buf.append(str); 
4329                 } 
4330                 return buf.toString(); 
4331         } 
4332     } 
4333  
4334     /** 
4335      * <p>Repeat a String <code>repeat</code> times to form a 
4336      * new String, with a String separator injected each time. </p> 
4337      * 
4338      * <pre> 
4339      * StringUtils.repeat(null, null, 2) = null 
4340      * StringUtils.repeat(null, "x", 2)  = null 
4341      * StringUtils.repeat("", null, 0)   = "" 
4342      * StringUtils.repeat("", "", 2)     = "" 
4343      * StringUtils.repeat("", "x", 3)    = "xxx" 
4344      * StringUtils.repeat("?", ", ", 3)  = "?, ?, ?" 
4345      * </pre> 
4346      * 
4347      * @param str        the String to repeat, may be null 
4348      * @param separator  the String to inject, may be null 
4349      * @param repeat     number of times to repeat str, negative treated as zero 
4350      * @return a new String consisting of the original String repeated, 
4351      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4352      */ 
4353     public static String repeat(String str, String separator, int repeat) { 
4354         if(str == null || separator == null) { 
4355             return repeat(str, repeat); 
4356         } else { 
4357             // given that repeat(String, int) is quite optimized, better to rely on it than try and splice this into it 
4358             String result = repeat(str + separator, repeat); 
4359             return removeEnd(result, separator); 
4360         } 
4361     } 
4362  
4363     /** 
4364      * <p>Returns padding using the specified delimiter repeated 
4365      * to a given length.</p> 
4366      * 
4367      * <pre> 
4368      * StringUtils.padding(0, 'e')  = "" 
4369      * StringUtils.padding(3, 'e')  = "eee" 
4370      * StringUtils.padding(-2, 'e') = IndexOutOfBoundsException 
4371      * </pre> 
4372      * 
4373      * <p>Note: this method doesn't not support padding with 
4374      * <a href="http://www.unicode.org/glossary/#supplementary_character">Unicode Supplementary Characters</a> 
4375      * as they require a pair of <code>char</code>s to be represented. 
4376      * If you are needing to support full I18N of your applications 
4377      * consider using {@link #repeat(String, int)} instead.  
4378      * </p> 
4379      * 
4380      * @param repeat  number of times to repeat delim 
4381      * @param padChar  character to repeat 
4382      * @return String with repeated character 
4383      * @throws IndexOutOfBoundsException if <code>repeat &lt; 0</code> 
4384      * @see #repeat(String, int) 
4385      */ 
4386     private static String padding(int repeat, char padChar) throws IndexOutOfBoundsException { 
4387         if (repeat < 0) { 
4388             throw new IndexOutOfBoundsException("Cannot pad a negative amount: " + repeat); 
4389         } 
4390         final char[] buf = new char[repeat]; 
4391         for (int i = 0; i < buf.length; i++) { 
4392             buf[i] = padChar; 
4393         } 
4394         return new String(buf); 
4395     } 
4396  
4397     /** 
4398      * <p>Right pad a String with spaces (' ').</p> 
4399      * 
4400      * <p>The String is padded to the size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4401      * 
4402      * <pre> 
4403      * StringUtils.rightPad(null, *)   = null 
4404      * StringUtils.rightPad("", 3)     = "   " 
4405      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 3)  = "bat" 
4406      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 5)  = "bat  " 
4407      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 1)  = "bat" 
4408      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", -1) = "bat" 
4409      * </pre> 
4410      * 
4411      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4412      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4413      * @return right padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4414      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4415      */ 
4416     public static String rightPad(String str, int size) { 
4417         return rightPad(str, size, ' '); 
4418     } 
4419  
4420     /** 
4421      * <p>Right pad a String with a specified character.</p> 
4422      * 
4423      * <p>The String is padded to the size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4424      * 
4425      * <pre> 
4426      * StringUtils.rightPad(null, *, *)     = null 
4427      * StringUtils.rightPad("", 3, 'z')     = "zzz" 
4428      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 3, 'z')  = "bat" 
4429      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 5, 'z')  = "batzz" 
4430      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 1, 'z')  = "bat" 
4431      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", -1, 'z') = "bat" 
4432      * </pre> 
4433      * 
4434      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4435      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4436      * @param padChar  the character to pad with 
4437      * @return right padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4438      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4439      * @since 2.0 
4440      */ 
4441     public static String rightPad(String str, int size, char padChar) { 
4442         if (str == null) { 
4443             return null; 
4444         } 
4445         int pads = size - str.length(); 
4446         if (pads <= 0) { 
4447             return str; // returns original String when possible 
4448         } 
4449         if (pads > PAD_LIMIT) { 
4450             return rightPad(str, size, String.valueOf(padChar)); 
4451         } 
4452         return str.concat(padding(pads, padChar)); 
4453     } 
4454  
4455     /** 
4456      * <p>Right pad a String with a specified String.</p> 
4457      * 
4458      * <p>The String is padded to the size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4459      * 
4460      * <pre> 
4461      * StringUtils.rightPad(null, *, *)      = null 
4462      * StringUtils.rightPad("", 3, "z")      = "zzz" 
4463      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 3, "yz")  = "bat" 
4464      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 5, "yz")  = "batyz" 
4465      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 8, "yz")  = "batyzyzy" 
4466      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 1, "yz")  = "bat" 
4467      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", -1, "yz") = "bat" 
4468      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 5, null)  = "bat  " 
4469      * StringUtils.rightPad("bat", 5, "")    = "bat  " 
4470      * </pre> 
4471      * 
4472      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4473      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4474      * @param padStr  the String to pad with, null or empty treated as single space 
4475      * @return right padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4476      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4477      */ 
4478     public static String rightPad(String str, int size, String padStr) { 
4479         if (str == null) { 
4480             return null; 
4481         } 
4482         if (isEmpty(padStr)) { 
4483             padStr = " "; 
4484         } 
4485         int padLen = padStr.length(); 
4486         int strLen = str.length(); 
4487         int pads = size - strLen; 
4488         if (pads <= 0) { 
4489             return str; // returns original String when possible 
4490         } 
4491         if (padLen == 1 && pads <= PAD_LIMIT) { 
4492             return rightPad(str, size, padStr.charAt(0)); 
4493         } 
4494  
4495         if (pads == padLen) { 
4496             return str.concat(padStr); 
4497         } else if (pads < padLen) { 
4498             return str.concat(padStr.substring(0, pads)); 
4499         } else { 
4500             char[] padding = new char[pads]; 
4501             char[] padChars = padStr.toCharArray(); 
4502             for (int i = 0; i < pads; i++) { 
4503                 padding[i] = padChars[i % padLen]; 
4504             } 
4505             return str.concat(new String(padding)); 
4506         } 
4507     } 
4508  
4509     /** 
4510      * <p>Left pad a String with spaces (' ').</p> 
4511      * 
4512      * <p>The String is padded to the size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4513      * 
4514      * <pre> 
4515      * StringUtils.leftPad(null, *)   = null 
4516      * StringUtils.leftPad("", 3)     = "   " 
4517      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 3)  = "bat" 
4518      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 5)  = "  bat" 
4519      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 1)  = "bat" 
4520      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", -1) = "bat" 
4521      * </pre> 
4522      * 
4523      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4524      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4525      * @return left padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4526      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4527      */ 
4528     public static String leftPad(String str, int size) { 
4529         return leftPad(str, size, ' '); 
4530     } 
4531  
4532     /** 
4533      * <p>Left pad a String with a specified character.</p> 
4534      * 
4535      * <p>Pad to a size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4536      * 
4537      * <pre> 
4538      * StringUtils.leftPad(null, *, *)     = null 
4539      * StringUtils.leftPad("", 3, 'z')     = "zzz" 
4540      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 3, 'z')  = "bat" 
4541      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 5, 'z')  = "zzbat" 
4542      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 1, 'z')  = "bat" 
4543      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", -1, 'z') = "bat" 
4544      * </pre> 
4545      * 
4546      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4547      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4548      * @param padChar  the character to pad with 
4549      * @return left padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4550      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4551      * @since 2.0 
4552      */ 
4553     public static String leftPad(String str, int size, char padChar) { 
4554         if (str == null) { 
4555             return null; 
4556         } 
4557         int pads = size - str.length(); 
4558         if (pads <= 0) { 
4559             return str; // returns original String when possible 
4560         } 
4561         if (pads > PAD_LIMIT) { 
4562             return leftPad(str, size, String.valueOf(padChar)); 
4563         } 
4564         return padding(pads, padChar).concat(str); 
4565     } 
4566  
4567     /** 
4568      * <p>Left pad a String with a specified String.</p> 
4569      * 
4570      * <p>Pad to a size of <code>size</code>.</p> 
4571      * 
4572      * <pre> 
4573      * StringUtils.leftPad(null, *, *)      = null 
4574      * StringUtils.leftPad("", 3, "z")      = "zzz" 
4575      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 3, "yz")  = "bat" 
4576      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 5, "yz")  = "yzbat" 
4577      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 8, "yz")  = "yzyzybat" 
4578      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 1, "yz")  = "bat" 
4579      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", -1, "yz") = "bat" 
4580      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 5, null)  = "  bat" 
4581      * StringUtils.leftPad("bat", 5, "")    = "  bat" 
4582      * </pre> 
4583      * 
4584      * @param str  the String to pad out, may be null 
4585      * @param size  the size to pad to 
4586      * @param padStr  the String to pad with, null or empty treated as single space 
4587      * @return left padded String or original String if no padding is necessary, 
4588      *  <code>null</code> if null String input 
4589      */ 
4590     public static String leftPad(String str, int size, String padStr) { 
4591         if (str == null) { 
4592             return null; 
4593         } 
4594         if (isEmpty(padStr)) { 
4595             padStr = " "; 
4596         } 
4597         int padLen = padStr.length(); 
4598         int strLen = str.length(); 
4599         int pads = size - strLen; 
4600         if (pads <= 0) { 
4601             return str; // returns original String when possible 
4602         } 
4603         if (padLen == 1 && pads <= PAD_LIMIT) { 
4604             return leftPad(str, size, padStr.charAt(0)); 
4605         } 
4606  
4607         if (pads == padLen) { 
4608             return padStr.concat(str); 
4609         } else if (pads < padLen) { 
4610             return padStr.substring(0, pads).concat(str); 
4611         } else { 
4612             char[] padding = new char[pads]; 
4613             char[] padChars = padStr.toCharArray(); 
4614             for (int i = 0; i < pads; i++) { 
4615                 padding[i] = padChars[i % padLen]; 
4616             } 
4617             return new String(padding).concat(str); 
4618         } 
4619     }

4623 	 * <code>null</code>. 
4624 	 *

4628 	 *         <code>null</code>. 
4629 	 * @since 2.4 
4630 	 * @deprecated See {@link CharSequenceUtils#length(CharSequence)}

4632 	public static int length(CharSequence cs) { 
4633 		return CharSequenceUtils.length(cs);

4636     // Centering 
4637     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4638     /** 
4639      * <p>Centers a String in a larger String of size <code>size</code> 
4640      * using the space character (' ').<p> 
4641      * 
4642      * <p>If the size is less than the String length, the String is returned. 
4643      * A <code>null</code> String returns <code>null</code>. 
4644      * A negative size is treated as zero.</p> 
4645      * 
4646      * <p>Equivalent to <code>center(str, size, " ")</code>.</p> 
4647      * 
4648      * <pre> 
4649      * StringUtils.center(null, *)   = null 
4650      * StringUtils.center("", 4)     = "    " 
4651      * StringUtils.center("ab", -1)  = "ab" 
4652      * StringUtils.center("ab", 4)   = " ab " 
4653      * StringUtils.center("abcd", 2) = "abcd" 
4654      * StringUtils.center("a", 4)    = " a  " 
4655      * </pre> 
4656      * 
4657      * @param str  the String to center, may be null 
4658      * @param size  the int size of new String, negative treated as zero 
4659      * @return centered String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4660      */ 
4661     public static String center(String str, int size) { 
4662         return center(str, size, ' '); 
4663     } 
4664  
4665     /** 
4666      * <p>Centers a String in a larger String of size <code>size</code>. 
4667      * Uses a supplied character as the value to pad the String with.</p> 
4668      * 
4669      * <p>If the size is less than the String length, the String is returned. 
4670      * A <code>null</code> String returns <code>null</code>. 
4671      * A negative size is treated as zero.</p> 
4672      * 
4673      * <pre> 
4674      * StringUtils.center(null, *, *)     = null 
4675      * StringUtils.center("", 4, ' ')     = "    " 
4676      * StringUtils.center("ab", -1, ' ')  = "ab" 
4677      * StringUtils.center("ab", 4, ' ')   = " ab" 
4678      * StringUtils.center("abcd", 2, ' ') = "abcd" 
4679      * StringUtils.center("a", 4, ' ')    = " a  " 
4680      * StringUtils.center("a", 4, 'y')    = "yayy" 
4681      * </pre> 
4682      * 
4683      * @param str  the String to center, may be null 
4684      * @param size  the int size of new String, negative treated as zero 
4685      * @param padChar  the character to pad the new String with 
4686      * @return centered String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4687      * @since 2.0 
4688      */ 
4689     public static String center(String str, int size, char padChar) { 
4690         if (str == null || size <= 0) { 
4691             return str; 
4692         } 
4693         int strLen = str.length(); 
4694         int pads = size - strLen; 
4695         if (pads <= 0) { 
4696             return str; 
4697         } 
4698         str = leftPad(str, strLen + pads / 2, padChar); 
4699         str = rightPad(str, size, padChar); 
4700         return str; 
4701     } 
4702  
4703     /** 
4704      * <p>Centers a String in a larger String of size <code>size</code>. 
4705      * Uses a supplied String as the value to pad the String with.</p> 
4706      * 
4707      * <p>If the size is less than the String length, the String is returned. 
4708      * A <code>null</code> String returns <code>null</code>. 
4709      * A negative size is treated as zero.</p> 
4710      * 
4711      * <pre> 
4712      * StringUtils.center(null, *, *)     = null 
4713      * StringUtils.center("", 4, " ")     = "    " 
4714      * StringUtils.center("ab", -1, " ")  = "ab" 
4715      * StringUtils.center("ab", 4, " ")   = " ab" 
4716      * StringUtils.center("abcd", 2, " ") = "abcd" 
4717      * StringUtils.center("a", 4, " ")    = " a  " 
4718      * StringUtils.center("a", 4, "yz")   = "yayz" 
4719      * StringUtils.center("abc", 7, null) = "  abc  " 
4720      * StringUtils.center("abc", 7, "")   = "  abc  " 
4721      * </pre> 
4722      * 
4723      * @param str  the String to center, may be null 
4724      * @param size  the int size of new String, negative treated as zero 
4725      * @param padStr  the String to pad the new String with, must not be null or empty 
4726      * @return centered String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4727      * @throws IllegalArgumentException if padStr is <code>null</code> or empty 
4728      */ 
4729     public static String center(String str, int size, String padStr) { 
4730         if (str == null || size <= 0) { 
4731             return str; 
4732         } 
4733         if (isEmpty(padStr)) { 
4734             padStr = " "; 
4735         } 
4736         int strLen = str.length(); 
4737         int pads = size - strLen; 
4738         if (pads <= 0) { 
4739             return str; 
4740         } 
4741         str = leftPad(str, strLen + pads / 2, padStr); 
4742         str = rightPad(str, size, padStr); 
4743         return str; 
4744     }

4746     // Case conversion

4748     /** 
4749      * <p>Converts a String to upper case as per {@link String#toUpperCase()}.</p> 
4750      * 
4751      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
4752      * 
4753      * <pre> 
4754      * StringUtils.upperCase(null)  = null 
4755      * StringUtils.upperCase("")    = "" 
4756      * StringUtils.upperCase("aBc") = "ABC" 
4757      * </pre> 
4758      * 
4759      * <p><strong>Note:</strong> As described in the documentation for {@link String#toUpperCase()}, 
4760      * the result of this method is affected by the current locale. 
4761      * For platform-independent case transformations, the method {@link #lowerCase(String, Locale)} 
4762      * should be used with a specific locale (e.g. {@link Locale#ENGLISH}).</p> 
4763      * 
4764      * @param str  the String to upper case, may be null 
4765      * @return the upper cased String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4766      */ 
4767     public static String upperCase(String str) { 
4768         if (str == null) { 
4769             return null; 
4770         } 
4771         return str.toUpperCase(); 
4772     } 
4773  
4774     /** 
4775      * <p>Converts a String to upper case as per {@link String#toUpperCase(Locale)}.</p> 
4776      * 
4777      * <p>A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
4778      * 
4779      * <pre> 
4780      * StringUtils.upperCase(null, Locale.ENGLISH)  = null 
4781      * StringUtils.upperCase("", Locale.ENGLISH)    = "" 
4782      * StringUtils.upperCase("aBc", Locale.ENGLISH) = "ABC" 
4783      * </pre> 
4784      * 
4785      * @param str  the String to upper case, may be null 
4786      * @param locale  the locale that defines the case transformation rules, must not be null 
4787      * @return the upper cased String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4788      * @since 2.5 
4789      */ 
4790     public static String upperCase(String str, Locale locale) { 
4791         if (str == null) { 
4792             return null; 
4793         } 
4794         return str.toUpperCase(locale); 
4795     }

4804      * StringUtils.lowerCase("")    = "" 
4805      * StringUtils.lowerCase("aBc") = "abc" 
4806      * </pre> 
4807      * 
4808      * <p><strong>Note:</strong> As described in the documentation for {@link String#toLowerCase()}, 
4809      * the result of this method is affected by the current locale. 
4810      * For platform-independent case transformations, the method {@link #lowerCase(String, Locale)} 
4811      * should be used with a specific locale (e.g. {@link Locale#ENGLISH}).</p> 
4812      * 
4813      * @param str  the String to lower case, may be null

4846     /** 
4847      * <p>Capitalizes a String changing the first letter to title case as 
4848      * per {@link Character#toTitleCase(char)}. No other letters are changed.</p> 
4849      * 
4850      * <p>For a word based algorithm, see {@link WordUtils#capitalize(String)}. 
4851      * A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
4852      * 
4853      * <pre> 
4854      * StringUtils.capitalize(null)  = null 
4855      * StringUtils.capitalize("")    = "" 
4856      * StringUtils.capitalize("cat") = "Cat" 
4857      * StringUtils.capitalize("cAt") = "CAt" 
4858      * </pre> 
4859      * 
4860      * @param cs the String to capitalize, may be null 
4861      * @return the capitalized String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4862      * @see WordUtils#capitalize(String) 
4863      * @see #uncapitalize(String) 
4864      * @since 2.0 
4865      */ 
4866     public static String capitalize(CharSequence cs) { 
4867         if (cs == null ) { 
4868             return null; 
4869         } 
4870         int strLen; 
4871         if ((strLen = cs.length()) == 0) { 
4872             return cs.toString(); 
4873         } 
4874         return new StringBuilder(strLen) 
4875             .append(Character.toTitleCase(cs.charAt(0))) 
4876             .append(CharSequenceUtils.subSequence(cs, 1)) 
4877             .toString(); 
4878     } 
4879  
4880     /** 
4881      * <p>Uncapitalizes a CharSequence changing the first letter to title case as 
4882      * per {@link Character#toLowerCase(char)}. No other letters are changed.</p> 
4883      * 
4884      * <p>For a word based algorithm, see {@link WordUtils#uncapitalize(String)}. 
4885      * A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
4886      * 
4887      * <pre> 
4888      * StringUtils.uncapitalize(null)  = null 
4889      * StringUtils.uncapitalize("")    = "" 
4890      * StringUtils.uncapitalize("Cat") = "cat" 
4891      * StringUtils.uncapitalize("CAT") = "cAT" 
4892      * </pre> 
4893      * 
4894      * @param cs the String to uncapitalize, may be null 
4895      * @return the uncapitalized String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4896      * @see WordUtils#uncapitalize(String) 
4897      * @see #capitalize(CharSequence) 
4898      * @since 2.0 
4899      */ 
4900     public static String uncapitalize(CharSequence cs) { 
4901         if (cs == null ) { 
4902             return null; 
4903         } 
4904         int strLen; 
4905         if ((strLen = cs.length()) == 0) { 
4906             return cs.toString(); 
4907         } 
4908         return new StringBuilder(strLen) 
4909             .append(Character.toLowerCase(cs.charAt(0))) 
4910             .append(CharSequenceUtils.subSequence(cs, 1)) 
4911             .toString(); 
4912     } 
4913  
4914     /** 
4915      * <p>Swaps the case of a String changing upper and title case to 
4916      * lower case, and lower case to upper case.</p> 
4917      * 
4918      * <ul> 
4919      *  <li>Upper case character converts to Lower case</li> 
4920      *  <li>Title case character converts to Lower case</li> 
4921      *  <li>Lower case character converts to Upper case</li> 
4922      * </ul> 
4923      * 
4924      * <p>For a word based algorithm, see {@link WordUtils#swapCase(String)}. 
4925      * A <code>null</code> input String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
4926      * 
4927      * <pre> 
4928      * StringUtils.swapCase(null)                 = null 
4929      * StringUtils.swapCase("")                   = "" 
4930      * StringUtils.swapCase("The dog has a BONE") = "tHE DOG HAS A bone" 
4931      * </pre> 
4932      * 
4933      * <p>NOTE: This method changed in Lang version 2.0. 
4934      * It no longer performs a word based algorithm. 
4935      * If you only use ASCII, you will notice no change. 
4936      * That functionality is available in WordUtils.</p> 
4937      * 
4938      * @param str  the String to swap case, may be null 
4939      * @return the changed String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
4940      */ 
4941     public static String swapCase(String str) { 
4942         int strLen; 
4943         if (str == null || (strLen = str.length()) == 0) { 
4944             return str; 
4945         } 
4946         StringBuilder buffer = new StringBuilder(strLen); 
4947  
4948         char ch = 0; 
4949         for (int i = 0; i < strLen; i++) { 
4950             ch = str.charAt(i); 
4951             if (Character.isUpperCase(ch)) { 
4952                 ch = Character.toLowerCase(ch); 
4953             } else if (Character.isTitleCase(ch)) { 
4954                 ch = Character.toLowerCase(ch); 
4955             } else if (Character.isLowerCase(ch)) { 
4956                 ch = Character.toUpperCase(ch); 
4957             } 
4958             buffer.append(ch); 
4959         } 
4960         return buffer.toString(); 
4961     } 
4962  
4963     // Count matches 
4964     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
4965     /** 
4966      * <p>Counts how many times the substring appears in the larger String.</p> 
4967      * 
4968      * <p>A <code>null</code> or empty ("") String input returns <code>0</code>.</p> 
4969      * 
4970      * <pre> 
4971      * StringUtils.countMatches(null, *)       = 0 
4972      * StringUtils.countMatches("", *)         = 0 
4973      * StringUtils.countMatches("abba", null)  = 0 
4974      * StringUtils.countMatches("abba", "")    = 0 
4975      * StringUtils.countMatches("abba", "a")   = 2 
4976      * StringUtils.countMatches("abba", "ab")  = 1 
4977      * StringUtils.countMatches("abba", "xxx") = 0 
4978      * </pre> 
4979      * 
4980      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
4981      * @param sub  the substring to count, may be null 
4982      * @return the number of occurrences, 0 if either String is <code>null</code> 
4983      */ 
4984     public static int countMatches(String str, String sub) { 
4985         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(sub)) { 
4986             return 0; 
4987         } 
4988         int count = 0; 
4989         int idx = 0; 
4990         while ((idx = str.indexOf(sub, idx)) != INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
4991             count++; 
4992             idx += sub.length(); 
4993         } 
4994         return count; 
4995     }

4999     /** 
5000      * <p>Checks if the CharSequence contains only unicode letters.</p> 
5001      * 
5002      * <p><code>null</code> will return <code>false</code>. 
5003      * An empty CharSequence (length()=0) will return <code>true</code>.</p> 
5004      * 
5005      * <pre> 
5006      * StringUtils.isAlpha(null)   = false 
5007      * StringUtils.isAlpha("")     = true 
5008      * StringUtils.isAlpha("  ")   = false 
5009      * StringUtils.isAlpha("abc")  = true 
5010      * StringUtils.isAlpha("ab2c") = false 
5011      * StringUtils.isAlpha("ab-c") = false 
5012      * </pre> 
5013      * 
5014      * @param cs  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
5015      * @return <code>true</code> if only contains letters, and is non-null 
5016      */ 
5017     public static boolean isAlpha(CharSequence cs) { 
5018         if (cs == null) { 
5019             return false; 
5020         } 
5021         int sz = cs.length(); 
5022         for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) { 
5023             if (Character.isLetter(cs.charAt(i)) == false) { 
5024                 return false; 
5025             } 
5026         } 
5027         return true; 
5028     }

5032      * space (' ').</p>

5035      * An empty CharSequence (length()=0) will return <code>true</code>.</p> 
5036      * 
5037      * <pre> 
5038      * StringUtils.isAlphaSpace(null)   = false 
5039      * StringUtils.isAlphaSpace("")     = true 
5040      * StringUtils.isAlphaSpace("  ")   = true 
5041      * StringUtils.isAlphaSpace("abc")  = true 
5042      * StringUtils.isAlphaSpace("ab c") = true 
5043      * StringUtils.isAlphaSpace("ab2c") = false 
5044      * StringUtils.isAlphaSpace("ab-c") = false 
5045      * </pre> 
5046      *

5049      *  and is non-null

5051     public static boolean isAlphaSpace(CharSequence cs) { 
5052         if (cs == null) { 
5053             return false; 
5054         } 
5055         int sz = cs.length(); 
5056         for (int i = 0; i < sz; i++) { 
5057             if ((Character.isLetter(cs.charAt(i)) == false) && (cs.charAt(i) != ' ')) { 
5058                 return false; 
5059             } 
5060         } 
5061         return true; 
5062     }

5166         return true;

5333     // Defaults 
5334     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5335     /** 
5336      * <p>Returns either the passed in String, 
5337      * or if the String is <code>null</code>, an empty String ("").</p> 
5338      * 
5339      * <pre> 
5340      * StringUtils.defaultString(null)  = "" 
5341      * StringUtils.defaultString("")    = "" 
5342      * StringUtils.defaultString("bat") = "bat" 
5343      * </pre> 
5344      * 
5345      * @see ObjectUtils#toString(Object) 
5346      * @see String#valueOf(Object) 
5347      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5348      * @return the passed in String, or the empty String if it 
5349      *  was <code>null</code> 
5350      */ 
5351     public static String defaultString(String str) { 
5352         return str == null ? EMPTY : str; 
5353     } 
5354  
5355     /** 
5356      * <p>Returns either the passed in String, or if the String is 
5357      * <code>null</code>, the value of <code>defaultStr</code>.</p> 
5358      * 
5359      * <pre> 
5360      * StringUtils.defaultString(null, "NULL")  = "NULL" 
5361      * StringUtils.defaultString("", "NULL")    = "" 
5362      * StringUtils.defaultString("bat", "NULL") = "bat" 
5363      * </pre> 
5364      * 
5365      * @see ObjectUtils#toString(Object,String) 
5366      * @see String#valueOf(Object) 
5367      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5368      * @param defaultStr  the default String to return 
5369      *  if the input is <code>null</code>, may be null 
5370      * @return the passed in String, or the default if it was <code>null</code> 
5371      */ 
5372     public static String defaultString(String str, String defaultStr) { 
5373         return str == null ? defaultStr : str; 
5374     } 
5375  
5376     /** 
5377      * <p>Returns either the passed in CharSequence, or if the CharSequence is 
5378      * empty or <code>null</code>, the value of <code>defaultStr</code>.</p> 
5379      * 
5380      * <pre> 
5381      * StringUtils.defaultIfEmpty(null, "NULL")  = "NULL" 
5382      * StringUtils.defaultIfEmpty("", "NULL")    = "NULL" 
5383      * StringUtils.defaultIfEmpty("bat", "NULL") = "bat" 
5384      * StringUtils.defaultIfEmpty("", null)      = null 
5385      * </pre> 
5386      * 
5387      * @see StringUtils#defaultString(String, String) 
5388      * @param str  the CharSequence to check, may be null 
5389      * @param defaultStr  the default CharSequence to return 
5390      *  if the input is empty ("") or <code>null</code>, may be null 
5391      * @return the passed in CharSequence, or the default 
5392      */ 
5393     public static CharSequence defaultIfEmpty(CharSequence str, CharSequence defaultStr) { 
5394         return StringUtils.isEmpty(str) ? defaultStr : str; 
5395     }
