5397     // Reversing 
5398     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5399     /** 
5400      * <p>Reverses a String as per {@link StringBuilder#reverse()}.</p> 
5401      * 
5402      * <p>A <code>null</code> String returns <code>null</code>.</p> 
5403      * 
5404      * <pre> 
5405      * StringUtils.reverse(null)  = null 
5406      * StringUtils.reverse("")    = "" 
5407      * StringUtils.reverse("bat") = "tab" 
5408      * </pre> 
5409      * 
5410      * @param str  the String to reverse, may be null 
5411      * @return the reversed String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
5412      */ 
5413     public static String reverse(String str) { 
5414         if (str == null) { 
5415             return null; 
5416         } 
5417         return CharSequenceUtils.reverse(str).toString(); 
5418     } 
5419  
5420     /** 
5421      * <p>Reverses a String that is delimited by a specific character.</p> 
5422      * 
5423      * <p>The Strings between the delimiters are not reversed. 
5424      * Thus java.lang.String becomes String.lang.java (if the delimiter 
5425      * is <code>'.'</code>).</p> 
5426      * 
5427      * <pre> 
5428      * StringUtils.reverseDelimited(null, *)      = null 
5429      * StringUtils.reverseDelimited("", *)        = "" 
5430      * StringUtils.reverseDelimited("a.b.c", 'x') = "a.b.c" 
5431      * StringUtils.reverseDelimited("a.b.c", ".") = "c.b.a" 
5432      * </pre> 
5433      * 
5434      * @param str  the String to reverse, may be null 
5435      * @param separatorChar  the separator character to use 
5436      * @return the reversed String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
5437      * @since 2.0 
5438      */ 
5439     public static String reverseDelimited(String str, char separatorChar) { 
5440         if (str == null) { 
5441             return null; 
5442         } 
5443         // could implement manually, but simple way is to reuse other, 
5444         // probably slower, methods. 
5445         String[] strs = split(str, separatorChar); 
5446         ArrayUtils.reverse(strs); 
5447         return join(strs, separatorChar); 
5448     } 
5449  
5450     // Abbreviating 
5451     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5452     /** 
5453      * <p>Abbreviates a String using ellipses. This will turn 
5454      * "Now is the time for all good men" into "Now is the time for..."</p> 
5455      * 
5456      * <p>Specifically: 
5457      * <ul> 
5458      *   <li>If <code>str</code> is less than <code>maxWidth</code> characters 
5459      *       long, return it.</li> 
5460      *   <li>Else abbreviate it to <code>(substring(str, 0, max-3) + "...")</code>.</li> 
5461      *   <li>If <code>maxWidth</code> is less than <code>4</code>, throw an 
5462      *       <code>IllegalArgumentException</code>.</li> 
5463      *   <li>In no case will it return a String of length greater than 
5464      *       <code>maxWidth</code>.</li> 
5465      * </ul> 
5466      * </p> 
5467      * 
5468      * <pre> 
5469      * StringUtils.abbreviate(null, *)      = null 
5470      * StringUtils.abbreviate("", 4)        = "" 
5471      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefg", 6) = "abc..." 
5472      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefg", 7) = "abcdefg" 
5473      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefg", 8) = "abcdefg" 
5474      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefg", 4) = "a..." 
5475      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefg", 3) = IllegalArgumentException 
5476      * </pre> 
5477      * 
5478      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5479      * @param maxWidth  maximum length of result String, must be at least 4 
5480      * @return abbreviated String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
5481      * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the width is too small 
5482      * @since 2.0 
5483      */ 
5484     public static String abbreviate(String str, int maxWidth) { 
5485         return abbreviate(str, 0, maxWidth); 
5486     } 
5487  
5488     /** 
5489      * <p>Abbreviates a String using ellipses. This will turn 
5490      * "Now is the time for all good men" into "...is the time for..."</p> 
5491      * 
5492      * <p>Works like <code>abbreviate(String, int)</code>, but allows you to specify 
5493      * a "left edge" offset.  Note that this left edge is not necessarily going to 
5494      * be the leftmost character in the result, or the first character following the 
5495      * ellipses, but it will appear somewhere in the result. 
5496      * 
5497      * <p>In no case will it return a String of length greater than 
5498      * <code>maxWidth</code>.</p> 
5499      * 
5500      * <pre> 
5501      * StringUtils.abbreviate(null, *, *)                = null 
5502      * StringUtils.abbreviate("", 0, 4)                  = "" 
5503      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", -1, 10) = "abcdefg..." 
5504      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 0, 10)  = "abcdefg..." 
5505      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 1, 10)  = "abcdefg..." 
5506      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 4, 10)  = "abcdefg..." 
5507      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 5, 10)  = "...fghi..." 
5508      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 6, 10)  = "...ghij..." 
5509      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 8, 10)  = "...ijklmno" 
5510      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 10, 10) = "...ijklmno" 
5511      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghijklmno", 12, 10) = "...ijklmno" 
5512      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghij", 0, 3)        = IllegalArgumentException 
5513      * StringUtils.abbreviate("abcdefghij", 5, 6)        = IllegalArgumentException 
5514      * </pre> 
5515      * 
5516      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5517      * @param offset  left edge of source String 
5518      * @param maxWidth  maximum length of result String, must be at least 4 
5519      * @return abbreviated String, <code>null</code> if null String input 
5520      * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the width is too small 
5521      * @since 2.0 
5522      */ 
5523     public static String abbreviate(String str, int offset, int maxWidth) { 
5524         if (str == null) { 
5525             return null; 
5526         } 
5527         if (maxWidth < 4) { 
5528             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Minimum abbreviation width is 4"); 
5529         } 
5530         if (str.length() <= maxWidth) { 
5531             return str; 
5532         } 
5533         if (offset > str.length()) { 
5534             offset = str.length(); 
5535         } 
5536         if ((str.length() - offset) < (maxWidth - 3)) { 
5537             offset = str.length() - (maxWidth - 3); 
5538         } 
5539         final String abrevMarker = "..."; 
5540 		if (offset <= 4) { 
5541             return str.substring(0, maxWidth - 3) + abrevMarker; 
5542         } 
5543         if (maxWidth < 7) { 
5544             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Minimum abbreviation width with offset is 7"); 
5545         } 
5546         if ((offset + (maxWidth - 3)) < str.length()) { 
5547             return abrevMarker + abbreviate(str.substring(offset), maxWidth - 3); 
5548         } 
5549         return abrevMarker + str.substring(str.length() - (maxWidth - 3)); 
5550     } 
5551      
5552     /** 
5553      * <p>Abbreviates a String to the length passed, replacing the middle characters with the supplied 
5554      * replacement String.</p> 
5555      * 
5556      * <p>This abbreviation only occurs if the following criteria is met: 
5557      * <ul> 
5558      * <li>Neither the String for abbreviation nor the replacement String are null or empty </li> 
5559      * <li>The length to truncate to is less than the length of the supplied String</li> 
5560      * <li>The length to truncate to is greater than 0</li> 
5561      * <li>The abbreviated String will have enough room for the length supplied replacement String 
5562      * and the first and last characters of the supplied String for abbreviation</li> 
5563      * </ul> 
5564      * Otherwise, the returned String will be the same as the supplied String for abbreviation. 
5565      * </p> 
5566      * 
5567      * <pre> 
5568      * StringUtils.abbreviateMiddle(null, null, 0)      = null 
5569      * StringUtils.abbreviateMiddle("abc", null, 0)      = "abc" 
5570      * StringUtils.abbreviateMiddle("abc", ".", 0)      = "abc" 
5571      * StringUtils.abbreviateMiddle("abc", ".", 3)      = "abc" 
5572      * StringUtils.abbreviateMiddle("abcdef", ".", 4)     = "ab.f" 
5573      * </pre> 
5574      * 
5575      * @param str  the String to abbreviate, may be null 
5576      * @param middle the String to replace the middle characters with, may be null 
5577      * @param length the length to abbreviate <code>str</code> to. 
5578      * @return the abbreviated String if the above criteria is met, or the original String supplied for abbreviation. 
5579      * @since 2.5 
5580      */ 
5581     public static String abbreviateMiddle(String str, String middle, int length) { 
5582         if (isEmpty(str) || isEmpty(middle)) { 
5583             return str; 
5584         } 
5585        
5586         if (length >= str.length() || length < (middle.length()+2)) { 
5587             return str; 
5588         } 
5589  
5590         int targetSting = length-middle.length(); 
5591         int startOffset = targetSting/2+targetSting%2; 
5592         int endOffset = str.length()-targetSting/2; 
5593          
5594         StringBuilder builder = new StringBuilder(length); 
5595         builder.append(str.substring(0,startOffset)); 
5596         builder.append(middle); 
5597         builder.append(str.substring(endOffset)); 
5598          
5599         return builder.toString(); 
5600     } 
5601  
5602     // Difference 
5603     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5604     /** 
5605      * <p>Compares two Strings, and returns the portion where they differ. 
5606      * (More precisely, return the remainder of the second String, 
5607      * starting from where it's different from the first.)</p> 
5608      * 
5609      * <p>For example, 
5610      * <code>difference("i am a machine", "i am a robot") -> "robot"</code>.</p> 
5611      * 
5612      * <pre> 
5613      * StringUtils.difference(null, null) = null 
5614      * StringUtils.difference("", "") = "" 
5615      * StringUtils.difference("", "abc") = "abc" 
5616      * StringUtils.difference("abc", "") = "" 
5617      * StringUtils.difference("abc", "abc") = "" 
5618      * StringUtils.difference("ab", "abxyz") = "xyz" 
5619      * StringUtils.difference("abcde", "abxyz") = "xyz" 
5620      * StringUtils.difference("abcde", "xyz") = "xyz" 
5621      * </pre> 
5622      * 
5623      * @param str1  the first String, may be null 
5624      * @param str2  the second String, may be null 
5625      * @return the portion of str2 where it differs from str1; returns the 
5626      * empty String if they are equal 
5627      * @since 2.0 
5628      */ 
5629     public static String difference(String str1, String str2) { 
5630         if (str1 == null) { 
5631             return str2; 
5632         } 
5633         if (str2 == null) { 
5634             return str1; 
5635         } 
5636         int at = indexOfDifference(str1, str2); 
5637         if (at == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
5638             return EMPTY; 
5639         } 
5640         return str2.substring(at); 
5641     } 
5642  
5643     /** 
5644      * <p>Compares two CharSequences, and returns the index at which the 
5645      * CharSequences begin to differ.</p> 
5646      * 
5647      * <p>For example, 
5648      * <code>indexOfDifference("i am a machine", "i am a robot") -> 7</code></p> 
5649      * 
5650      * <pre> 
5651      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(null, null) = -1 
5652      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("", "") = -1 
5653      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("", "abc") = 0 
5654      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("abc", "") = 0 
5655      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("abc", "abc") = -1 
5656      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("ab", "abxyz") = 2 
5657      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("abcde", "abxyz") = 2 
5658      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference("abcde", "xyz") = 0 
5659      * </pre> 
5660      * 
5661      * @param cs1  the first CharSequence, may be null 
5662      * @param cs2  the second CharSequence, may be null 
5663      * @return the index where cs1 and cs2 begin to differ; -1 if they are equal 
5664      * @since 2.0 
5665      */ 
5666     public static int indexOfDifference(CharSequence cs1, CharSequence cs2) { 
5667         if (cs1 == cs2) { 
5668             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
5669         } 
5670         if (cs1 == null || cs2 == null) { 
5671             return 0; 
5672         } 
5673         int i; 
5674         for (i = 0; i < cs1.length() && i < cs2.length(); ++i) { 
5675             if (cs1.charAt(i) != cs2.charAt(i)) { 
5676                 break; 
5677             } 
5678         } 
5679         if (i < cs2.length() || i < cs1.length()) { 
5680             return i; 
5681         } 
5682         return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
5683     } 
5684  
5685     /** 
5686      * <p>Compares all CharSequences in an array and returns the index at which the 
5687      * CharSequences begin to differ.</p> 
5688      * 
5689      * <p>For example, 
5690      * <code>indexOfDifference(new String[] {"i am a machine", "i am a robot"}) -> 7</code></p> 
5691      * 
5692      * <pre> 
5693      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(null) = -1 
5694      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {}) = -1 
5695      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abc"}) = -1 
5696      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {null, null}) = -1 
5697      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"", ""}) = -1 
5698      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"", null}) = 0 
5699      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abc", null, null}) = 0 
5700      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {null, null, "abc"}) = 0 
5701      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"", "abc"}) = 0 
5702      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abc", ""}) = 0 
5703      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abc", "abc"}) = -1 
5704      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abc", "a"}) = 1 
5705      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"ab", "abxyz"}) = 2 
5706      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abcde", "abxyz"}) = 2 
5707      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"abcde", "xyz"}) = 0 
5708      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"xyz", "abcde"}) = 0 
5709      * StringUtils.indexOfDifference(new String[] {"i am a machine", "i am a robot"}) = 7 
5710      * </pre> 
5711      * 
5712      * @param css  array of CharSequences, entries may be null 
5713      * @return the index where the strings begin to differ; -1 if they are all equal 
5714      * @since 2.4 
5715      */ 
5716     public static int indexOfDifference(CharSequence[] css) { 
5717         if (css == null || css.length <= 1) { 
5718             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
5719         } 
5720         boolean anyStringNull = false; 
5721         boolean allStringsNull = true; 
5722         int arrayLen = css.length; 
5723         int shortestStrLen = Integer.MAX_VALUE; 
5724         int longestStrLen = 0; 
5725  
5726         // find the min and max string lengths; this avoids checking to make 
5727         // sure we are not exceeding the length of the string each time through 
5728         // the bottom loop. 
5729         for (int i = 0; i < arrayLen; i++) { 
5730             if (css[i] == null) { 
5731                 anyStringNull = true; 
5732                 shortestStrLen = 0; 
5733             } else { 
5734                 allStringsNull = false; 
5735                 shortestStrLen = Math.min(css[i].length(), shortestStrLen); 
5736                 longestStrLen = Math.max(css[i].length(), longestStrLen); 
5737             } 
5738         } 
5739  
5740         // handle lists containing all nulls or all empty strings 
5741         if (allStringsNull || (longestStrLen == 0 && !anyStringNull)) { 
5742             return INDEX_NOT_FOUND; 
5743         } 
5744  
5745         // handle lists containing some nulls or some empty strings 
5746         if (shortestStrLen == 0) { 
5747             return 0; 
5748         } 
5749  
5750         // find the position with the first difference across all strings 
5751         int firstDiff = -1; 
5752         for (int stringPos = 0; stringPos < shortestStrLen; stringPos++) { 
5753             char comparisonChar = css[0].charAt(stringPos); 
5754             for (int arrayPos = 1; arrayPos < arrayLen; arrayPos++) { 
5755                 if (css[arrayPos].charAt(stringPos) != comparisonChar) { 
5756                     firstDiff = stringPos; 
5757                     break; 
5758                 } 
5759             } 
5760             if (firstDiff != -1) { 
5761                 break; 
5762             } 
5763         } 
5764  
5765         if (firstDiff == -1 && shortestStrLen != longestStrLen) { 
5766             // we compared all of the characters up to the length of the 
5767             // shortest string and didn't find a match, but the string lengths 
5768             // vary, so return the length of the shortest string. 
5769             return shortestStrLen; 
5770         } 
5771         return firstDiff; 
5772     } 
5773      
5774     /** 
5775      * <p>Compares all Strings in an array and returns the initial sequence of  
5776      * characters that is common to all of them.</p> 
5777      * 
5778      * <p>For example, 
5779      * <code>getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"i am a machine", "i am a robot"}) -> "i am a "</code></p> 
5780      * 
5781      * <pre> 
5782      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(null) = "" 
5783      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {}) = "" 
5784      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abc"}) = "abc" 
5785      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {null, null}) = "" 
5786      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"", ""}) = "" 
5787      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"", null}) = "" 
5788      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abc", null, null}) = "" 
5789      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {null, null, "abc"}) = "" 
5790      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"", "abc"}) = "" 
5791      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abc", ""}) = "" 
5792      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abc", "abc"}) = "abc" 
5793      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abc", "a"}) = "a" 
5794      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"ab", "abxyz"}) = "ab" 
5795      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abcde", "abxyz"}) = "ab" 
5796      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"abcde", "xyz"}) = "" 
5797      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"xyz", "abcde"}) = "" 
5798      * StringUtils.getCommonPrefix(new String[] {"i am a machine", "i am a robot"}) = "i am a " 
5799      * </pre> 
5800      * 
5801      * @param strs  array of String objects, entries may be null 
5802      * @return the initial sequence of characters that are common to all Strings 
5803      * in the array; empty String if the array is null, the elements are all null  
5804      * or if there is no common prefix.  
5805      * @since 2.4 
5806      */ 
5807     public static String getCommonPrefix(String[] strs) { 
5808         if (strs == null || strs.length == 0) { 
5809             return EMPTY; 
5810         } 
5811         int smallestIndexOfDiff = indexOfDifference(strs); 
5812         if (smallestIndexOfDiff == INDEX_NOT_FOUND) { 
5813             // all strings were identical 
5814             if (strs[0] == null) { 
5815                 return EMPTY; 
5816             } 
5817             return strs[0]; 
5818         } else if (smallestIndexOfDiff == 0) { 
5819             // there were no common initial characters 
5820             return EMPTY; 
5821         } else { 
5822             // we found a common initial character sequence 
5823             return strs[0].substring(0, smallestIndexOfDiff); 
5824         } 
5825     }   
5826      
5827     // Misc 
5828     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5829     /** 
5830      * <p>Find the Levenshtein distance between two Strings.</p> 
5831      * 
5832      * <p>This is the number of changes needed to change one String into 
5833      * another, where each change is a single character modification (deletion, 
5834      * insertion or substitution).</p> 
5835      * 
5836      * <p>The previous implementation of the Levenshtein distance algorithm 
5837      * was from <a href="http://www.merriampark.com/ld.htm">http://www.merriampark.com/ld.htm</a></p> 
5838      * 
5839      * <p>Chas Emerick has written an implementation in Java, which avoids an OutOfMemoryError 
5840      * which can occur when my Java implementation is used with very large strings.<br> 
5841      * This implementation of the Levenshtein distance algorithm 
5842      * is from <a href="http://www.merriampark.com/ldjava.htm">http://www.merriampark.com/ldjava.htm</a></p> 
5843      * 
5844      * <pre> 
5845      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance(null, *)             = IllegalArgumentException 
5846      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance(*, null)             = IllegalArgumentException 
5847      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("","")               = 0 
5848      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("","a")              = 1 
5849      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("aaapppp", "")       = 7 
5850      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("frog", "fog")       = 1 
5851      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("fly", "ant")        = 3 
5852      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("elephant", "hippo") = 7 
5853      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("hippo", "elephant") = 7 
5854      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("hippo", "zzzzzzzz") = 8 
5855      * StringUtils.getLevenshteinDistance("hello", "hallo")    = 1 
5856      * </pre> 
5857      * 
5858      * @param s  the first String, must not be null 
5859      * @param t  the second String, must not be null 
5860      * @return result distance 
5861      * @throws IllegalArgumentException if either String input <code>null</code> 
5862      */ 
5863     public static int getLevenshteinDistance(CharSequence s, CharSequence t) { 
5864         if (s == null || t == null) { 
5865             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Strings must not be null"); 
5866         } 
5867  
5868         /* 
5869            The difference between this impl. and the previous is that, rather  
5870            than creating and retaining a matrix of size s.length()+1 by t.length()+1,  
5871            we maintain two single-dimensional arrays of length s.length()+1.  The first, d, 
5872            is the 'current working' distance array that maintains the newest distance cost 
5873            counts as we iterate through the characters of String s.  Each time we increment 
5874            the index of String t we are comparing, d is copied to p, the second int[].  Doing so 
5875            allows us to retain the previous cost counts as required by the algorithm (taking  
5876            the minimum of the cost count to the left, up one, and diagonally up and to the left 
5877            of the current cost count being calculated).  (Note that the arrays aren't really  
5878            copied anymore, just switched...this is clearly much better than cloning an array  
5879            or doing a System.arraycopy() each time  through the outer loop.) 
5880  
5881            Effectively, the difference between the two implementations is this one does not  
5882            cause an out of memory condition when calculating the LD over two very large strings. 
5883          */ 
5884  
5885         int n = s.length(); // length of s 
5886         int m = t.length(); // length of t 
5887  
5888         if (n == 0) { 
5889             return m; 
5890         } else if (m == 0) { 
5891             return n; 
5892         } 
5893  
5894         if (n > m) { 
5895             // swap the input strings to consume less memory 
5896         	CharSequence tmp = s; 
5897             s = t; 
5898             t = tmp; 
5899             n = m; 
5900             m = t.length(); 
5901         } 
5902  
5903         int p[] = new int[n+1]; //'previous' cost array, horizontally 
5904         int d[] = new int[n+1]; // cost array, horizontally 
5905         int _d[]; //placeholder to assist in swapping p and d 
5906  
5907         // indexes into strings s and t 
5908         int i; // iterates through s 
5909         int j; // iterates through t 
5910  
5911         char t_j; // jth character of t 
5912  
5913         int cost; // cost 
5914  
5915         for (i = 0; i<=n; i++) { 
5916             p[i] = i; 
5917         } 
5918  
5919         for (j = 1; j<=m; j++) { 
5920             t_j = t.charAt(j-1); 
5921             d[0] = j; 
5922  
5923             for (i=1; i<=n; i++) { 
5924                 cost = s.charAt(i-1)==t_j ? 0 : 1; 
5925                 // minimum of cell to the left+1, to the top+1, diagonally left and up +cost 
5926                 d[i] = Math.min(Math.min(d[i-1]+1, p[i]+1),  p[i-1]+cost); 
5927             } 
5928  
5929             // copy current distance counts to 'previous row' distance counts 
5930             _d = p; 
5931             p = d; 
5932             d = _d; 
5933         } 
5934  
5935         // our last action in the above loop was to switch d and p, so p now  
5936         // actually has the most recent cost counts 
5937         return p[n]; 
5938     } 
5939  
5940     /** 
5941      * <p>Gets the minimum of three <code>int</code> values.</p> 
5942      * 
5943      * @param a  value 1 
5944      * @param b  value 2 
5945      * @param c  value 3 
5946      * @return  the smallest of the values 
5947      */ 
5948 /* 
5949     private static int min(int a, int b, int c) { 
5950         // Method copied from NumberUtils to avoid dependency on subpackage 
5951         if (b < a) { 
5952             a = b; 
5953         } 
5954         if (c < a) { 
5955             a = c; 
5956         } 
5957         return a; 
5958     } 
5959 */

5961     // startsWith 
5962     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
5963  
5964     /** 
5965      * <p>Check if a String starts with a specified prefix.</p> 
5966      * 
5967      * <p><code>null</code>s are handled without exceptions. Two <code>null</code> 
5968      * references are considered to be equal. The comparison is case sensitive.</p> 
5969      * 
5970      * <pre> 
5971      * StringUtils.startsWith(null, null)      = true 
5972      * StringUtils.startsWith(null, "abc")     = false 
5973      * StringUtils.startsWith("abcdef", null)  = false 
5974      * StringUtils.startsWith("abcdef", "abc") = true 
5975      * StringUtils.startsWith("ABCDEF", "abc") = false 
5976      * </pre> 
5977      * 
5978      * @see java.lang.String#startsWith(String) 
5979      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
5980      * @param prefix the prefix to find, may be null 
5981      * @return <code>true</code> if the String starts with the prefix, case sensitive, or 
5982      *  both <code>null</code> 
5983      * @since 2.4 
5984      */ 
5985     public static boolean startsWith(String str, String prefix) { 
5986         return startsWith(str, prefix, false); 
5987     } 
5988  
5989     /** 
5990      * <p>Case insensitive check if a String starts with a specified prefix.</p> 
5991      * 
5992      * <p><code>null</code>s are handled without exceptions. Two <code>null</code> 
5993      * references are considered to be equal. The comparison is case insensitive.</p> 
5994      * 
5995      * <pre> 
5996      * StringUtils.startsWithIgnoreCase(null, null)      = true 
5997      * StringUtils.startsWithIgnoreCase(null, "abc")     = false 
5998      * StringUtils.startsWithIgnoreCase("abcdef", null)  = false 
5999      * StringUtils.startsWithIgnoreCase("abcdef", "abc") = true 
6000      * StringUtils.startsWithIgnoreCase("ABCDEF", "abc") = true 
6001      * </pre> 
6002      * 
6003      * @see java.lang.String#startsWith(String) 
6004      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
6005      * @param prefix the prefix to find, may be null 
6006      * @return <code>true</code> if the String starts with the prefix, case insensitive, or 
6007      *  both <code>null</code> 
6008      * @since 2.4 
6009      */ 
6010     public static boolean startsWithIgnoreCase(String str, String prefix) { 
6011         return startsWith(str, prefix, true); 
6012     } 
6013  
6014     /** 
6015      * <p>Check if a String starts with a specified prefix (optionally case insensitive).</p> 
6016      * 
6017      * @see java.lang.String#startsWith(String) 
6018      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
6019      * @param prefix the prefix to find, may be null 
6020      * @param ignoreCase inidicates whether the compare should ignore case 
6021      *  (case insensitive) or not. 
6022      * @return <code>true</code> if the String starts with the prefix or 
6023      *  both <code>null</code> 
6024      */ 
6025     private static boolean startsWith(String str, String prefix, boolean ignoreCase) { 
6026         if (str == null || prefix == null) { 
6027             return (str == null && prefix == null); 
6028         } 
6029         if (prefix.length() > str.length()) { 
6030             return false; 
6031         } 
6032         return str.regionMatches(ignoreCase, 0, prefix, 0, prefix.length()); 
6033     } 
6034      
6035     /** 
6036      * <p>Check if a String starts with any of an array of specified strings.</p> 
6037      *  
6038      * <pre> 
6039      * StringUtils.startsWithAny(null, null)      = false 
6040      * StringUtils.startsWithAny(null, new String[] {"abc"})  = false 
6041      * StringUtils.startsWithAny("abcxyz", null)     = false 
6042      * StringUtils.startsWithAny("abcxyz", new String[] {""}) = false 
6043      * StringUtils.startsWithAny("abcxyz", new String[] {"abc"}) = true 
6044      * StringUtils.startsWithAny("abcxyz", new String[] {null, "xyz", "abc"}) = true 
6045      * </pre> 
6046      * 
6047      * @param string  the String to check, may be null 
6048      * @param searchStrings the Strings to find, may be null or empty 
6049      * @return <code>true</code> if the String starts with any of the the prefixes, case insensitive, or 
6050      *  both <code>null</code> 
6051      * @since 2.5 
6052      */ 
6053     public static boolean startsWithAny(String string, String[] searchStrings) { 
6054         if (isEmpty(string) || ArrayUtils.isEmpty(searchStrings)) { 
6055             return false; 
6056         } 
6057         for (int i = 0; i < searchStrings.length; i++) { 
6058             String searchString = searchStrings[i]; 
6059             if (StringUtils.startsWith(string, searchString)) { 
6060                 return true; 
6061             } 
6062         } 
6063         return false; 
6064     } 
6065  
6066     // endsWith 
6067     //----------------------------------------------------------------------- 
6068  
6069     /** 
6070      * <p>Check if a String ends with a specified suffix.</p> 
6071      * 
6072      * <p><code>null</code>s are handled without exceptions. Two <code>null</code> 
6073      * references are considered to be equal. The comparison is case sensitive.</p> 
6074      * 
6075      * <pre> 
6076      * StringUtils.endsWith(null, null)      = true 
6077      * StringUtils.endsWith(null, "def")     = false 
6078      * StringUtils.endsWith("abcdef", null)  = false 
6079      * StringUtils.endsWith("abcdef", "def") = true 
6080      * StringUtils.endsWith("ABCDEF", "def") = false 
6081      * StringUtils.endsWith("ABCDEF", "cde") = false 
6082      * </pre> 
6083      * 
6084      * @see java.lang.String#endsWith(String) 
6085      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
6086      * @param suffix the suffix to find, may be null 
6087      * @return <code>true</code> if the String ends with the suffix, case sensitive, or 
6088      *  both <code>null</code> 
6089      * @since 2.4 
6090      */ 
6091     public static boolean endsWith(String str, String suffix) { 
6092         return endsWith(str, suffix, false); 
6093     } 
6094  
6095     /** 
6096      * <p>Case insensitive check if a String ends with a specified suffix.</p> 
6097      * 
6098      * <p><code>null</code>s are handled without exceptions. Two <code>null</code> 
6099      * references are considered to be equal. The comparison is case insensitive.</p> 
6100      * 
6101      * <pre> 
6102      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase(null, null)      = true 
6103      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase(null, "def")     = false 
6104      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase("abcdef", null)  = false 
6105      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase("abcdef", "def") = true 
6106      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase("ABCDEF", "def") = true 
6107      * StringUtils.endsWithIgnoreCase("ABCDEF", "cde") = false 
6108      * </pre> 
6109      * 
6110      * @see java.lang.String#endsWith(String) 
6111      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
6112      * @param suffix the suffix to find, may be null 
6113      * @return <code>true</code> if the String ends with the suffix, case insensitive, or 
6114      *  both <code>null</code> 
6115      * @since 2.4 
6116      */ 
6117     public static boolean endsWithIgnoreCase(String str, String suffix) { 
6118         return endsWith(str, suffix, true); 
6119     } 
6120  
6121     /** 
6122      * <p>Check if a String ends with a specified suffix (optionally case insensitive).</p> 
6123      * 
6124      * @see java.lang.String#endsWith(String) 
6125      * @param str  the String to check, may be null 
6126      * @param suffix the suffix to find, may be null 
6127      * @param ignoreCase inidicates whether the compare should ignore case 
6128      *  (case insensitive) or not. 
6129      * @return <code>true</code> if the String starts with the prefix or 
6130      *  both <code>null</code> 
6131      */ 
6132     private static boolean endsWith(String str, String suffix, boolean ignoreCase) { 
6133         if (str == null || suffix == null) { 
6134             return str == null && suffix == null; 
6135         } 
6136         if (suffix.length() > str.length()) { 
6137             return false; 
6138         } 
6139         int strOffset = str.length() - suffix.length(); 
6140         return str.regionMatches(ignoreCase, strOffset, suffix, 0, suffix.length()); 
6141     } 
6142 }
